HANDY DANDIES -- CHANGELOG
================================================================================

PROJECT START: 2026-09-12  -  06:53 AM EDT (approximate; see this entry's own note)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12  -  ~06:54 AM - 07:22 AM EDT (bounded by checkpoints; see Verified note)

Initial build: a three.js scene filled with a grid of rigged hand instances
that each independently rotate to point at the live cursor. Built per
explicit instruction to follow HANDO's own hand-rendering architecture as
the reference.

Added:
- Reused HANDO's rigged hand asset (`data/HAND3D/HAND-021/Hand2.glb`,
  22-bone skeleton, GLB format), physically copied into this project's own
  `data/processed/HAND3D/Hand2.glb` (no cross-project asset serving).
- Reused HANDO's generic dev-panel engine (`src/devpanel/devPanel.js`) and
  its dev-panel CSS chrome (`src/style.css`) verbatim.
- New: cursor-to-3D-target math (HANDO itself has no cursor-tracking --
  it's a manual single-hand pose tool). Each frame, the cursor's screen
  position is raycast against a plane positioned in front of the hand
  field (toward the camera); each hand instance slerps its rotation toward
  a look-at quaternion aimed at that 3D point.
- New: per-instance skeleton cloning (`SkeletonUtils.clone()`) so each of
  the 54 (default 6x9 grid) hand instances can aim independently -- HANDO
  only ever renders one hand, so this had no precedent to follow.
- New: bind-pose "pointing direction" measurement (wrist bone `rHand` to
  fingertip bone `rMid3`, in world space at load time) rather than assuming
  a fixed axis -- matches HANDO's own "measured, not assumed" convention
  for this rig.
- Dev panel groups: Field Layout (rows/columns/spacing/hand scale), Cursor
  Tracking (damping, target depth, target-marker toggle), Camera (FOV,
  distance), Lighting (key light azimuth/elevation/intensity/color,
  ambient), Background, Debug (grid helper).

Fixed (both caught live during the build, before this entry):
- `three/addons/utils/SkeletonUtils.js` (three@0.169.0, the pinned CDN
  version) exports `clone`/`retarget`/`retargetClip` directly, not a
  `SkeletonUtils` namespace object -- confirmed by directly fetching and
  reading the served module source. `import { SkeletonUtils } from '...'`
  threw `SyntaxError: does not provide an export named 'SkeletonUtils'` at
  module-load time. Fixed via `import { clone as cloneSkeletal } from
  'three/addons/utils/SkeletonUtils.js'`.
- `initDevPanel()`'s synchronous onChange-on-restore behavior would throw a
  TDZ (temporal dead zone) error against `camera`/`keyLight`/etc. (declared
  later in the file, via `const`) the first time a returning visitor had
  saved settings. Fixed with the same detach-onChange-before-init/
  reattach-after mitigation HANDO's own `main.js` already uses for this
  exact documented gotcha.

Verified:
- Live in the browser (a local `python3 -m http.server` on port 8421):
  loaded with 0 real console errors (one lingering console-log entry from
  the SkeletonUtils error was confirmed STALE -- it persisted in
  `read_console_messages`'s own history even after a fresh reload where
  the hands were already rendering correctly on screen, confirmed via
  screenshot at the same moment; see CODE_SUMMARY.txt's own Gotchas).
- The core requested behavior, via screenshots at 3 cursor positions:
  center (idle default) showed all 54 hands radially converging on one
  point in front of the field; cursor near the top of the browser pitched
  every hand into a darker, silhouette-like underside view; cursor near
  the bottom pitched them into a clearly-lit top/back view with visible
  finger detail -- matching the user's stated spec (top -> see hand
  undersides, bottom -> see hand tops) directly, not just plausibly.
- The "Field Layout" dev-panel group renders and its controls (e.g. "Rows
  (Count)") are reachable/clickable in the live panel.
- Did NOT independently verify: the look-at math's behavior when a hand's
  X position exactly matches the cursor target's X (a look-at degenerate
  case), dev-panel Save/Reset round-tripping for this project's own new
  controls, or any resolution/aspect ratio other than the ~800x450 and
  1280x720 browser-pane sizes used during this session.

Timing note: no single clean start timestamp was captured for this task
(it began mid-conversation, not at a fresh prompt) -- the entry's own
timestamp range is bounded by 2 real checkpoints taken during the work
(07:01:07 and 07:13:28 EDT) plus this entry's own finish time, not a
single measured start-to-finish interval. See the project's ETA History
log entry for the same task for the precise measured segments.

2026-09-12 (continued)  -  ~07:36 AM - 07:52 AM EDT

10 follow-on requests in one turn: camera click-drag-to-pan + scroll/pinch-
to-zoom with full mobile porting; Field Layout decoupled from view scale;
independent Row/Column Spacing; Alternate + Progressive row-offset patterns
with a mode-switch checkbox; Camera X/Y/Z position sliders; Lighting/Toon
Shading/Outline settings "just like HANDO."

Added:
- OrbitControls with `enableRotate: false` -- click-drag pans, scroll/pinch
  zooms, on both desktop and mobile via OrbitControls' own `touches: {
  ONE: PAN, TWO: DOLLY_PAN }` config (no custom touch-handling code
  needed). Camera X/Y/Z Position + FOV dev-panel sliders, 2-way synced with
  live mouse pan/zoom (`applyCameraControl()`/`syncCameraPanelFromLive()`,
  ported from HANDO's own Camera-group pattern, simplified since this
  camera never orbits).
- Row Spacing and Column Spacing as 2 independent sliders, replacing the
  single shared "Cell Spacing." Alternate Row Offset (brick/hex-style,
  every other row shifted along the column axis) and Progressive Row
  Offset (row `r` shifted by `r` times the setting) as 2 mutually-exclusive
  modes via a new checkbox.
- Full Lighting group parity with HANDO (added Key Light Aim Height). New
  Toon Shading group (steps/threshold/floor/ceiling/base tint/texture
  influence/tint/rim intensity-power-color) and Outline group (enabled/
  use-screen-space-pass/color/hull thickness/pass thickness-strength-glow)
  -- both ported from HANDO's own shader code and control sets, adapted for
  N hand instances (one shared toon material; a per-instance hull-outline
  clone OR a single OutlinePass covering every instance's skinned mesh,
  toggleable).

Fixed:
- Field Layout previously called camera/lighting/target-plane framing
  functions on every rows/cols/spacing change, so changing the grid
  visibly rescaled the camera view -- explicitly not wanted ("Field
  layout shouldn't affect view scale"). Framing is now derived from the
  field's bounding size exactly ONCE (`framedOnce` flag in
  `rebuildField()`) and frozen after that; Field Layout changes only ever
  touch `relayoutField()` (positions/scale), never camera/light/
  target-plane again.
- `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` reading 0 at script-parse time in this
  dev environment left the renderer/`EffectComposer`/`OutlinePass`
  permanently zero-sized once post-processing was added (confirmed:
  `renderer.getSize()` reporting `[0,0]`, `GL_INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION:
  ...Attachment has zero size` spamming the console). Fixed with a
  per-frame self-healing size check in `animate()`, sharing one
  `applyRendererSize()` function with the `resize` event listener --
  same fix shape as a previously-documented DOTFLICKO gotcha for this
  exact class of quirk.

Verified:
- Click-drag pan and scroll-wheel zoom, via screenshots showing the field
  genuinely shift and scale on each gesture.
- Field-layout/camera decoupling, DIRECTLY (not just visually): dispatched
  a real `input`/`change` event on the live "Rows (Count)" slider (6 -> 10,
  hand count 54 -> 90) and confirmed `camera.position` was numerically
  IDENTICAL before and after, via a `window.__debug` inspection hook.
- Progressive Row Offset visually produces the expected cascading/
  staircase pattern across rows (screenshot-confirmed).
- Both outline techniques (hull-mesh clone and screen-space OutlinePass)
  render a visible black outline when toggled independently -- confirmed
  via 2 separate screenshots with `Use OutlinePass` on and off.
- Every new dev-panel group and control (Row/Column Spacing, Alternate/
  Progressive Row Offset + mode checkbox, Camera X/Y/Z, Rim Light
  Intensity, Use OutlinePass, etc.) is present and reachable in the live
  panel (confirmed via `find` against their exact labels).
- Mobile touch-drag was exercised under 375x812 viewport emulation and
  produced a correct pan -- but NOT confirmed as a genuine touch-event
  path: this session's own browser-automation tool documents that its
  drags "still arrive as mouse clicks" even under mobile emulation, so
  this only confirms the pan logic is viewport-size-agnostic, not that
  real on-device touch/pinch gestures work. OrbitControls' `touches` config
  is a proven three.js library feature, which lowers but doesn't eliminate
  that residual risk.
- Separately identified during this verification (NOT a page bug, and NOT
  what the self-healing fix above addresses): this session's own browser
  tool reports `document.hidden === true` and `window.innerWidth === 0`
  whenever queried via its JS-execution path, confirmed even on a freshly-
  created, explicitly-foregrounded tab -- while a `screenshot` call on the
  identical tab at the identical moment renders correctly at full
  resolution. Documented as a tool characteristic in CODE_SUMMARY.txt's
  GOTCHAS so a future session doesn't mistake it for a real page defect.

Disclosed interpretation call: "Alternate Row Offset in perpendicular
direction" was ambiguous -- implemented as the standard brick/hex-pattern
reading (shift along the column axis), not a Z-depth stagger. Flagged to
the user rather than silently assumed; a 1-line change in `relayoutField()`
if the other interpretation was actually meant.

2026-09-12 (continued again)  -  ~08:19 AM - 08:29 AM EDT

3 more requests: a Camera Zoom slider 2-way synced with scroll/pinch;
baking the user's own pasted live-tuned dev-panel settings dump in as the
new defaults; render-order stacking driven by distance from the cursor
(farthest hands on top, nearest at the bottom). Plus: create a GitHub repo
and push (see next entry).

Added:
- "Zoom (Distance To Pan Target)" slider in the Camera group --
  `setCameraDistance()` moves the camera along its existing camera-to-
  target line to a new distance (the same axis OrbitControls' own scroll/
  pinch dolly already moves it along); `syncCameraPanelFromLive()` reads
  `camera.position.distanceTo(controls.target)` back into the slider every
  frame. Confirmed both directions live: scrolling moved the slider
  259 -> 172; setting the slider to 400 moved the live camera distance to
  exactly 400.
- Cursor-distance render ordering: every frame, each hand's
  `skinnedMesh.renderOrder` is set to its own live distance from the
  cursor target (farther = higher = drawn later = ends up on top); its
  `outlineMesh.renderOrder` is that value minus a small epsilon so a
  hand's own outline always draws just under its own fill. Required
  `depthTest: false` on both hand materials (kept `depthWrite: true`) --
  three.js's normal depth-tested compositing otherwise always lets real
  camera depth win over renderOrder. Disclosed tradeoff: a single hand's
  own internal self-occlusion (finger over finger) now relies on
  front-face culling + triangle order, not a real per-pixel depth test --
  acceptable for this model's mostly-convex static bind pose.
- Every dev-panel default baked directly from the user's own pasted
  live-tuned settings dump (a raw `devPanel.js` `values`/`order` JSON
  export): grid grew from 6x9/54 hands to 17x30/510 hands; Row/Column
  Spacing 10/11.5; Alternate Row Offset -5.5; camera positioned at
  (-1.80, -1.30, 259.09), FOV 35; Lighting/Toon/Outline/Background all
  retuned (white background, ambient off, key light white at full
  intensity from azimuth 231/elevation 28/aim height -100%, 2-step toon
  shading, outline off by default). Also widened `fieldRows`/`fieldCols`
  slider max (20/24 -> 40/40) since the new defaults (17/30) needed the
  room.

Verified:
- All 3 features confirmed live (not just visually): `renderOrder` values
  sampled directly via `window.__debug` showed 5 hands' render order
  decreasing monotonically as their distance from the cursor target
  decreased, with each outline's own renderOrder exactly 0.01 below its
  fill's; the Zoom slider's 2-way sync (see Added, above); `depthTest:
  false` confirmed present on the live material.
- Re-confirmed the "hidden tab suspends rendering" environment quirk
  (documented in the prior round) independently: at 510 hand instances,
  an un-fronted tab's `requestAnimationFrame` genuinely never fires (a
  frame-counting script itself timed out waiting), which first looked like
  a real performance regression from the much larger hand count until the
  tab was explicitly fronted (`tabs_select`) and rendered correctly at
  full detail on the very next screenshot -- not a new bug, the same
  already-documented tool characteristic applying at a larger scale.

2026-09-12 (continued yet again)  -  ~08:52 AM - 09:04 AM EDT

1 request: a "Reordering Flash" checkbox so 2 overlapping hands' cursor-
distance render order doesn't flip/flash while they're still overlapping,
only once they visibly clear each other.

Added:
- `preventReorderFlash` checkbox (Debug group). When on,
  `updateRenderOrder()` approximates every hand's on-screen footprint as a
  circle (world radius = half the measured hand length x its own scale,
  projected to screen pixels via standard perspective scaling) and runs an
  O(n^2) pairwise screen-space overlap check across all hands. A hand's
  `effectiveRenderOrder` (now a persisted field, not recomputed from
  scratch every frame) only updates from its live cursor-distance when it
  is NOT currently overlapping anything -- while overlapping, it keeps its
  prior order, so 2 fighting hands can't flip mid-overlap; it only
  re-evaluates once that hand clears every overlap it was in. Off by
  default (zero added cost when disabled -- the pairwise check is fully
  gated behind the checkbox).

Verified:
- The full freeze -> stay-frozen-despite-large-distance-change -> resume
  cycle, via a `window.__debug`-driven test in one atomic script: forced 2
  hands to identical positions (guaranteed overlap), captured their order;
  moved the cursor target to a wildly different point (massively changing
  live distance) and re-ran the update -- order stayed byte-identical;
  restored one hand's real grid position (clearing the overlap) and
  re-ran -- order immediately jumped to the new live value. All 3 phases
  confirmed in a single script run, eliminating any ambiguity about
  ordering/timing between separate tool calls.

Debugging note (real time cost, worth recording): initial verification
attempts showed the overlap check always reporting no-overlap and NaN-like
projected screen positions, which looked like a bug in the new code. Root
cause was NOT the new code: this session's Browser pane was not currently
displayed at all (confirmed via `tabs_context`: "The Browser pane is
currently hidden"), which fully suspends `requestAnimationFrame`
(confirmed with a vanilla rAF-counting script having nothing to do with
this project) -- and since the existing camera-resize self-heal fix (added
in an earlier round for the `window.innerWidth`-reads-0-at-boot issue)
lives inside the rAF loop, it never got a chance to run, leaving the
camera stuck with a NaN projection matrix (constructed with
`aspect = 0/0` at boot). Manually repairing the camera via a debug hook
(`camera.aspect = <real ratio>; camera.updateProjectionMatrix()`) before
re-testing resolved it completely -- the overlap-detection code itself was
correct the whole time. See CODE_SUMMARY.txt's GOTCHAS for the durable
writeup.

Not committed to git yet -- this project's own git discipline (CLAUDE.md
§9) requires an explicit push instruction, which wasn't given for this
specific change.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued yet again)  -  ~09:15 AM - 09:23 AM EDT

Bug report: "certain hands are always rendering on top regardless of
clearance" -- the previous round's "Prevent Reordering Flash" checkbox.

Root-caused via direct simulation (not guessing): the original design froze
a hand's ENTIRE render order the instant it overlapped ANYTHING, resuming
only once every overlap cleared. A 240-frame simulated sweep at the
default zoom showed 0% overlap (fix never even engaged) -- but the SAME
sweep at a closer zoom (matching how a user would actually zoom in to
inspect this exact behavior) showed ALL 510 hands overlapping something
in EVERY frame, meaning none of them ever got a single overlap-free frame
to unfreeze on. The whole field froze solid, permanently, the instant the
checkbox was turned on in anything but a sparse view -- exactly the
reported symptom (whichever hands happened to be on top at that instant
stayed there forever).

Fixed:
- Replaced the per-hand freeze with a per-PAIR lock (`pairLocks` Map).
  Every hand's `effectiveRenderOrder` is now ALWAYS its own live cursor
  distance -- never frozen. For each currently-overlapping PAIR, a lock
  remembers which of the 2 should stay underneath (decided once, from
  their live order when the overlap began); each frame, only a pair whose
  live order would violate its own active lock gets a small corrective
  nudge, and the lock is dropped the instant that pair stops overlapping.
  A hand can never fully disconnect from reality, so a scene-wide freeze
  is no longer structurally possible.
- Fixed a real TDZ crash introduced while relocating this logic:
  `pairLocks` was declared right next to `updateRenderOrder()`, further
  down the file than `animate()`'s own call site -- and `animate()`'s
  first, synchronous invocation reaches `updateRenderOrder()`'s `else`
  branch (which reads `pairLocks.size`) before the rest of the module's
  top-level code, including that declaration, has run. Moved `pairLocks`
  (and `projectScratch`) up next to the other early module-level state.

Verified:
- Re-ran the EXACT repro (240-frame sweep, camera zoomed to distance 60):
  0 hands showed zero order-changes (the previous bug's signature) --
  every single hand updated its order on nearly every frame (239/239
  possible changes each).
- Confirmed the actual anti-flash behavior still works post-fix: forced 2
  hands into a sustained, moving screen overlap across 100 frames of live
  cursor movement -- their relative order (which one's value was larger)
  never flipped once, while their absolute values kept tracking live
  distance (unlike the old frozen-solid version).
- Both tests driven via `window.__debug` state manipulation, not a live
  screenshot -- this session's Browser pane was again not displayed
  (see the prior round's own GOTCHAS entry for this recurring tool
  characteristic).

Not committed to git yet -- awaiting an explicit push instruction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~09:33 AM - 09:46 AM EDT

Bug report against the immediately-preceding round's own fix: "its better
but not fully resolved i still see some hands that are incorrectly
rendring above a hand further than the cursor" + "the previous build had
no flashes, but it now does."

Root-caused via direct measurement, not guessing. Simulated a realistic
cursor sweep and measured 2 things directly: (1) among ~87,000 random
hand pairs whose TRUE cursor distances differed by more than 15 world
units (a large, unambiguous gap), how often the ASSIGNED render order
still put the closer one on top; (2) among hands actually overlapping on
screen in consecutive frames, how often their relative order flipped.
The prior round's per-pair "unconditionally pin to partner+epsilon"
design scored 21% on test (1) -- worse than doing nothing -- because in
this dense field, chains of simultaneous locks (A-under-B-under-C...)
compound: every hand in a long chain collapses toward whichever hand
anchors the chain, discarding real distances almost entirely. That's
exactly the reported regression.

Fixed:
- Replaced the entire lock/pin system with per-hand exponential
  smoothing. A hand not currently overlapping anything snaps straight to
  its own live cursor distance (fully responsive, no lag). A hand that IS
  overlapping something blends toward its own live distance instead of
  snapping (`effectiveRenderOrder += (live - effectiveRenderOrder) * 0.15`
  per frame) -- critically, NEVER derived from another hand's value, so
  the chain-collapse failure mode (and the earlier freeze-forever failure
  mode, 2 rounds ago) are both structurally impossible now, not just
  patched around.
- Fixed a stray `#` typo (should have been `//`) introduced while writing
  this fix's own comment block, which would have been a hard syntax error.

Verified:
- Re-ran the same 87,000-pair "clearly different distance" test against
  the new design: 0.09% wrong-order rate (vs. the previous round's 21%).
- Re-ran the 240-frame stuck-hand check: 0 hands frozen (matches the
  already-fixed permanent-freeze bug from 2 rounds ago -- confirms this
  rewrite didn't reintroduce it).
- Measured the frame-to-frame flip rate among hands actually overlapping
  on screen: ~0.6%, roughly half of what the SAME scenario measures with
  the checkbox off entirely -- confirms the feature still does something
  real, not just "stopped breaking things."
- Did NOT get a clean live visual confirmation this round either (see
  GOTCHAS) -- all 3 measurements above were taken via direct
  `window.__debug`-driven simulation of realistic and dense/close-zoom
  cursor movement, not a screenshot.

Disclosed trade-off: this is no longer a literal "won't change until they
visibly clear" hard freeze, as originally specified -- it's a fast blend
that mostly prevents instant pops. 2 different literal-freeze designs
(this round's predecessor and the one before it) each produced a worse,
concrete bug once tested against this field's actual density, so this
round deliberately trades the literal spec for a measurably more robust
mechanism rather than attempting a 4th variation on hard locking.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~10:19 AM - 10:25 AM EDT

Follow-up bug report against the smoothing fix: "still see flashing...
the thumb of the hand doesnt seem to be accounted for... only happens
when I move the cursor left and right, not up and down."

Root-caused via direct measurement of the actual mesh geometry (not
guessing): the reordering-flash overlap check approximated each hand's
on-screen footprint as a circle centered at `wrapper.position` with
radius = half the wrist-to-fingertip bone distance (`handLengthRaw`).
Measured the ACTUAL rendered mesh's bounding sphere directly
(`THREE.Box3.setFromObject()` + `.getBoundingSphere()`) and found: the
real sphere's radius is ~23 world units vs. the assumed ~8 (undersized by
~5x), and its true center sits ~13 world units from `wrapper.position` --
because `Hand2.glb`'s visible mesh includes a full forearm below the
wrist that the bone-distance measurement never accounted for. The
mis-centered, undersized circle missed the thumb (and most of the
forearm) at many rotations, meaning those overlaps got ZERO flash
protection at all -- explaining both symptoms exactly: the "pop" is the
thumb entering a screen region the algorithm never knew was part of the
hand, and it's specifically a left/right (yaw) symptom because yawing
swings that off-center mass much further across the screen than
pitching does.

Fixed:
- Measure the real mesh bounding sphere ONCE at load time
  (`handBoundsCenterLocal`/`handBoundsRadiusLocal`), in the same
  identity-transform frame `alignQuat`/`handLengthRaw` already use.
- Each frame, transform the sphere's LOCAL center through the hand's
  actual live transform chain (scale -> alignQuat -> live wrapper
  rotation -> wrapper position) instead of assuming `wrapper.position`
  directly -- a sphere's radius only needs scaling, not rotating, so only
  the center needed the full chain.
- Deliberately left `handLengthRaw` itself untouched (it still drives
  grid scale-fitting and outline thickness) -- this fix is scoped only to
  the reordering-flash overlap circle.

Verified:
- Re-ran the same grid-neighbor overlap-observation count from the prior
  round's own test: 975,875 overlap-observations across a 150-frame sweep
  with the corrected circle, vs. 326,012 with the old one at the same
  positions -- roughly 3x more genuine overlaps are now actually detected
  and protected (this is the concrete, measured fix for "the thumb wasn't
  accounted for").
- Re-ran the same 3-metric validation suite from the prior round (0 stuck
  hands; wrong-order rate for clearly-different-distance pairs; flip rate
  for actually-overlapping pairs) -- all held steady or improved slightly
  with the corrected geometry, confirming this change didn't regress the
  design fixed 2 entries ago.
- All measurements taken via `window.__debug`-driven simulation, not a
  live screenshot (Browser pane still undisplayed this session).

Not committed to git yet.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~10:35 AM - 10:38 AM EDT

Investigation only, no code change. Follow-up: "when i move the cursor
left and right, i still see flashing above and below it on that vertical
axis... its mostly the thumb of one hand popping ontop of the hand next
to it."

Traced a specific vertically-adjacent hand pair (same column, adjacent
rows) through a horizontal-only cursor sweep, frame by frame. Found: the
gap between their smoothed render-order values shrinks perfectly cleanly
and monotonically, frame over frame, with zero discontinuity anywhere
(-1.24 -> -0.92 -> -0.58 -> -0.23 -> +0.11 -> +0.43 across 6 consecutive
frames) -- yet the instant that gap crosses zero, the actual draw order
(and therefore the visible stacking) flips completely in a single frame,
identical to what would happen with no smoothing at all.

Root cause, structural rather than a further bug: `renderOrder` is a
discrete sort key. A hand is drawn strictly before or after another, with
no partial/blended state in between -- this whole feature exists because
`depthTest: false` removes the GPU's own continuous, per-pixel depth
resolution. Smoothing the underlying NUMBER (which is what every fix so
far has adjusted) cannot smooth the resulting binary SWAP. The prior 2
rounds' fixes (geometry-correct overlap detection, then per-hand
smoothing) are still both doing real, measured work -- they fix wrong
overall ordering and damp noise-driven spurious crossings -- but neither
one, nor any further tuning of the same mechanism, can eliminate the
instant pop that happens at a GENUINE crossing (the cursor's own movement
actually causing 2 hands' true relative order to swap).

Not fixed this round -- flagged to the user as a scope/architecture
decision rather than attempted unilaterally, since a real fix (e.g. an
alpha cross-fade between the 2 competing hands during the crossing
window) is a materially bigger change than anything else in this
feature's history and conflicts with the current opaque/`depthTest:false`
approach.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~10:40 AM - 10:43 AM EDT

Request: "make it so that row and column spacing is not tied to hand
scale. changing one should not affect the other."

`computeBaseScale()` previously derived the auto-fit scale factor from
`Math.min(rowSpacing, columnSpacing) * 0.8`, so dragging either spacing
slider silently rescaled every hand too. Replaced with a fixed world-unit
reference length (`HAND_SCALE_REFERENCE_LENGTH = 8`, chosen to match what
that formula evaluated to at this project's own original spacing defaults
so existing settings don't visually jump) -- `cfg.handScale` is now the
ONLY thing that scales hands; Row/Column Spacing only reposition them.

Verified directly: forced Row Spacing 10->30 and Column Spacing 11.5->2
via live slider events and confirmed `computeBaseScale()`'s result (and
the actual applied `hand.clone.scale.x`) stayed numerically IDENTICAL
across both changes; then changed Hand Scale 1.2->2.0 and confirmed the
scale DID change (0.597->0.995) -- the 2 controls are now fully
independent. Also confirmed hand positions still update correctly with
the new spacing (the position delta between 2 adjacent hands matched the
new Column Spacing value exactly).

Not committed to git yet.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~10:47 AM - 10:53 AM EDT

Investigation only, no code change. Bug report with screenshot: "I set
the toon shader to 2 toon steps... in certain shaded areas, the surface
is quite noisy looking instead of looking like a flat tone."

Isolated to a single, non-overlapping hand first (rules out any
`depthTest:false`/render-order interaction from recent rounds). Tested 4
separate hypotheses directly, live in the browser:
- Removed the diffuse texture map entirely (`material.map = null`) --
  noise unchanged. Rules out the texture.
- Called `geometry.computeVertexNormals()` to fully re-derive smooth
  normals from scratch, ignoring whatever the source file shipped --
  noise unchanged. Rules out bad/hard-split source normals.
- Raised `keyElevation` from the default 28 degrees (fairly grazing) to
  80 (near-overhead) -- noise area shrank noticeably (a small patch
  remained near the thumb/wrist joint). Confirms light ANGLE is a real
  factor.
- Raised `ambientIntensity` from 0 -- the facet pattern got MORE visible,
  not less. Confirms why: `MeshToonMaterial` only routes DIRECT lights
  through the gradient-map toon banding; ambient/hemisphere (indirect)
  light contributes smoothly, un-quantized, directly revealing whatever
  fine curvature the mesh actually has.

Conclusion: this is real, fine geometric detail in `Hand2.glb`'s own mesh
(likely near the wrist-thumb joint/seam), not a shader or material bug.
A raking light angle -- and any non-zero ambient regardless of angle --
will reveal it, independent of toon step count (also confirmed:
increasing toonSteps to 8/16 did not reduce the noise, since the
underlying normal variation is the same regardless of how many bands the
lighting gets quantized into).

Not fixed with a code change -- flagged to the user as a lighting/
settings characteristic (keep key light closer to overhead, keep ambient
low) rather than something further shader tuning can eliminate for this
specific asset.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~11:08 AM - 11:15 AM EDT

CORRECTION to the 10:47-10:53 AM entry above. Investigation only, no code
change. User directly challenged that entry's conclusion: "but that noise
doesnt occur in HANDO" -- a decisive counter-example, since HANDO renders
the identical `Hand2.glb`.

Re-investigated. First tried isolating a single hand and toggling
`depthTest` true/false directly (suspecting self-occlusion within a
single hand's own complex geometry, since that material is the one real
difference from HANDO's own toon material found by re-reading HANDO's
source). Ruled this out by direct measurement: connected-component
analysis of a 100x100px live sample region found the island count for the
minority toon tone changed by only ~8% (78 islands at `depthTest:false`
vs. 71 at `depthTest:true`, out of ~800 lit pixels) -- nowhere near
enough to explain the effect.

Found the real cause by diffing this project's baked toon/lighting
defaults directly against HANDO's own (`HANDO/src/main.js` DEV_GROUPS):
this project bakes `ambientIntensity:0` with a full-contrast
`toonSteps:2` / `toonShadowFloor:12` / `toonLightCeiling:100` (from the
user's own earlier live-tuned settings dump), while HANDO defaults to
`ambientIntensity:0.7` with a narrower `toonSteps:3` / `13` / `81`.
Confirmed directly: applying HANDO's recipe live to the same sample
region turned a stark 2-value luminance spread (132/255, a 123-unit
jump, in 78 small scattered islands) into a smooth 58-value gradient
(136-232) with a comparable island count -- i.e. the real geometric
variation identified in the prior entry is genuinely there either way,
but HANDO's smooth ambient fill plus narrower/softer toon contrast hides
it, while this project's zero-ambient/full-contrast recipe amplifies the
exact same variation into stark black-or-white speckle.

The prior entry's individual test results are still accurate as far as
they went (ambient raised ALONE, without also narrowing the toon range,
does make it worse) -- what was missing was testing HANDO's actual
combined recipe, not just one variable in isolation. Corrected
`CODE_SUMMARY.txt`'s GOTCHAS entry to reflect this.

Not fixed with a code change -- this is a lighting/toon-defaults tuning
decision (which new `ambientIntensity`/`toonSteps`/`toonShadowFloor`/
`toonLightCeiling` values to bake), flagged to the user rather than
changed unilaterally, since the current values were explicitly baked
from the user's own earlier live-tuned settings dump.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~11:19 AM - 11:39 AM EDT

2nd CORRECTION to the toon-shading-noise investigation, and the actual
fix. The user directly disproved the immediately-preceding entry's
ambient/toonSteps theory: "In Hando, even with 0 ambient light and 2 step
toon shader it still doesnt have that noise." Re-investigated a 3rd time.

First re-tried the original depthTest hypothesis via a rotation sweep (44
orientations of a single isolated hand, sampling for spatial speckle
signatures at each) -- found one genuinely anomalous angle (Y=180, viewing
the back of the hand: 25 islands from only 1167 shadow pixels, vs. ~5
islands from 2-4x as many pixels at other angles), but the user
interjected mid-investigation: "its not tied to hand rotation or position"
-- correctly heading off a 3rd wrong turn before it was fully chased.

Asked the user directly what they observed. Answer was the decisive clue:
"The noise is only present in areas where the hand is overlapping itself.
So I can somewhat see through the hand itself and the noise represents
the obscured surfaces behind the frontmost surface" -- plus "ive used many
hand poses in Hando" (and it never shows this, across any of them). That
is the textbook signature of `depthTest:false` self-occlusion bleed-
through -- the same mechanism the FIRST correction entry (11:08-11:15 AM)
had already tried and dismissed, but via a coarse whole-canvas island-
count metric that diluted the signal across all 510 hands and happened to
sample a region with no actual self-overlap.

Redid the test properly: isolated a single hand in its bind pose (fingers
naturally curl over the palm -- a real self-overlap), and did a per-pixel
diff of that exact region between `depthTest:true` and `depthTest:false`.
Result: 8.2% of pixels differ, spatially clustered precisely on the
overlap, with `depthTest:false` showing bright/lit (255) where
`depthTest:true` correctly shows shadowed (132) -- confirming a wrongly-
drawn, differently-lit back surface bleeds through the front one when
depth testing is off. This is what the user was describing.

Fix (approved by the user before implementing, since it changes the
render-order architecture): restored `depthTest:true` (the default) on
both hand materials (`createToonMaterial()`, `ensureOutlineMaterial()`).
To keep cross-hand cursor-distance stacking working without real depth
testing between hands, gave each hand's own fill mesh AND outline mesh an
`onBeforeRender = (renderer) => renderer.clearDepth()` hook (added where
each hand instance is built, in `rebuildField()`) -- this wipes the depth
buffer immediately before that hand's own draw call, so: within a hand,
triangles depth-test correctly against each other (self-occlusion fixed);
between hands, whichever one draws LATER (per `renderOrder`, unchanged --
still set every frame from cursor distance) always wins the overlap,
since it always starts from a freshly-cleared depth buffer. Attached to
BOTH meshes (not just fill) because the outline mesh is sometimes
invisible and `onBeforeRender` never fires for an invisible object.

Verified live, directly:
- The same finger-over-palm region that showed the 8.2% bleed-through
  split now reads a single clean luminance value (132) across every
  sampled pixel in that region -- self-occlusion confirmed fixed.
- Positioned 2 isolated hands at the identical screen position but 60
  world units apart in real camera depth; gave the PHYSICALLY FARTHER one
  the HIGHER `renderOrder` and (to remove any ambiguity from the shared
  toon material's own custom tint-uniform machinery, which made an
  earlier attempt at this same test misleadingly inconclusive)
  temporarily swapped it to a plain `MeshBasicMaterial` red tint --
  confirmed the farther-but-later-drawn hand visually wins 100% of 25
  sampled overlap pixels despite being physically behind, proving cross-
  hand render-order stacking survived the change.
- 0 console errors on a fresh reload; `renderOrder` still correlates
  perfectly with cursor distance across all 510 hands (0 inversions
  across 509 sorted pairs); full-field render unchanged in overall
  appearance from baseline.
- Screenshot verification was not possible this round (the Browser pane's
  own screenshot pipeline timed out repeatedly, a known recurring
  environment quirk) -- all verification above was done via direct WebGL
  `readPixels()` pixel/histogram/diff analysis instead, which is more
  precise than a visual screenshot for this specific class of bug anyway.

Corrected `CODE_SUMMARY.txt`'s GOTCHAS section in 3 places: the noise
entry itself (now describes the real cause and fix, not either of the 2
disproven theories), and 2 architecture-description entries (#7, #9, and
a 3rd standalone entry) that described the old `depthTest:false`
architecture as current.

One known residual trade-off, disclosed to the user as part of proposing
this fix: if 2 hands' `renderOrder` values land extremely close together
(the same near-tie case "Prevent Reordering Flash" already exists to
soften), their draw sequence could in principle interleave in a way that
briefly disrupts the per-hand depth-clear grouping -- a minor, transient
edge case in the same spirit as the already-documented reordering-flash
residual pop, not separately reproduced or treated as a new bug class.

Not committed/pushed yet.

2026-09-12 (continued once more)  -  ~11:56 AM - 12:24 PM EDT

"Add the pose settings from Hando. For now, the pose settings apply to
every hand. Be sure to provide the Hide Wrist (%) setting input. The main
thing is that a wrist that is cut will be shifted such that the cut wrist
base should still correspond to the Field Layout points."

Ported HANDO's own Pose dev-panel group (identical rig/bone names, same
`Hand2.glb` asset): 25 finger sliders (Curl/Splay/2nd-Segment-Splay/
Curl-Bias/Tip-Twist x5 fingers), Wrist Bend/Splay, Whole-Hand Rotation
X/Y/Z, and Hide Wrist -- applied identically to every hand (a shared
pose, per the user's own "for now" scoping; no per-hand override yet).
Explicitly did NOT port HANDO's own Saved Poses list-picker / pose-tween
system, since the request was about the pose CONTROLS, not the preset-
management layer on top of them -- flagged as a scope boundary rather
than silently omitted.

Added:
- New "Pose" dev-panel group (between Cursor Tracking and Camera), 31
  controls total, defaults copied verbatim from HANDO's own.
- `boneRestQuat` -- captured once, right after load, from the ORIGINAL
  un-cloned skeleton's bind pose, BEFORE `rebuildField()` ever clones it.
  Shared across every hand's own distinct skeleton clone (simpler than
  HANDO's own per-model `restQuatMap`, since this project clones ONE GLB
  N times rather than supporting 2 different model files).
- `applyCurlToSkeleton()`/`applyCurl()`, `applyWristPoseToSkeleton()`/
  `applyWristPose()` -- ported near-verbatim from HANDO's own identical
  functions (including all of its live-measured per-finger axis/sign/
  magnitude tables: `FINGER_CURL_AXIS`, `FINGER_SPLAY_AXIS`,
  `FINGER_SPLAY2_AXIS`, `FINGER_MAX_DEG`, etc.), adapted to loop over
  every hand in `hands` (each with its own skeleton) instead of HANDO's
  1-2 `posableModels`.
- `updateClonePoseTransform()` -- new, this project's own (no HANDO
  equivalent needed one): computes `cloneBaseQuat` (`alignQuat` composed
  with the live Whole-Hand Rotation sliders) and each hand's
  `clone.position`, both identical across every hand, applied once per
  slider change (not per frame -- `wrapper`'s own per-frame cursor-
  tracking rotation is untouched by any of this).
- `updateWristClipPlaneForHand()` + a shared `wristClipPlane` -- Hide
  Wrist's own GPU clipping plane, ported from HANDO's own `wristClipPlane`
  but recomputed PER-HAND (via `onBeforeRender`, the same mechanism
  already used for the depth-clear technique) rather than once globally,
  since every hand here faces a different direction. `material.
  clippingPlanes` (requiring `renderer.localClippingEnabled = true`) was
  used instead of HANDO's own `renderer.clippingPlanes` specifically so
  the clip never affects the cursor target marker/grid helper.

Fixed 3 real bugs this round, all found via direct empirical live
testing (not caught by code review alone):

1. **Hide Wrist + Whole-Hand Rotation combined drifted off the grid
   point.** First implementation ported HANDO's own palm-center rotation
   pivot math (generalized with an extra baseline term for Hide Wrist) --
   passed EVERY isolated test (Hide Wrist alone: exact; Whole-Hand
   Rotation alone: exact) but a COMBINED test (Hide Wrist 30% + Whole-Hand
   Rotation Y 90°) measured a real 0.27-world-unit drift off the grid
   point. Root cause: preserving 2 different fixed points (palm center
   AND the cut point) under one rotation is only possible if the rotation
   axis happens to pass through both, which a general slider combination
   won't. Resolved in grid-alignment's favor (the user's own explicit
   "main thing") by dropping the palm-pivot preservation entirely and
   solving directly for whatever position keeps the CURRENT cut point at
   the grid point for the CURRENT rotation -- a real, disclosed behavior
   difference from HANDO (Whole-Hand Rotation now pivots around the cut
   point, not the palm center, even at Hide Wrist=0%).
2. **A direction+distance pair silently isn't an absolute position.** The
   same first attempt stored `armDownDirRaw`/`armToWristDistRaw` (a unit
   direction and a scalar) and used `direction*distance*t` as if it were
   the cut point's own absolute raw-frame position -- it's a displacement,
   not a position, and treating it as one implicitly (and wrongly)
   assumed the wrist bone sat at the raw content-space origin. It looked
   correct in ISOLATED testing (masked by the fact that the actual origin-
   coincident bone is the FOREARM, not the wrist, confirmed via a live
   measurement with the whole ancestor chain zeroed out: `forearmPosRaw ≈
   (0,0,0)`, `wristPosRaw ≈ (0, 14.63, 0)`) but broke once rotation was
   combined in (bug #1 above). Fixed by storing the actual ABSOLUTE raw
   positions of both bones (`wristPosRaw`, `forearmPosRaw`) and computing
   the cut point as `forearmPosRaw.lerp(wristPosRaw, hideT)` directly.
3. **Finger curl axes leaked each hand's own current cursor-facing
   direction into what should be a purely anatomical rotation.** HANDO's
   own `applyCurlToSkeleton()` pre-rotates its fixed WORLD_X/Y/Z curl
   axes by `modelRoot.quaternion` (its single whole-hand orientation)
   before converting via the bone's full world quaternion -- correct for
   HANDO, which has no OTHER rotation layer. This project's hands have an
   extra one: `wrapper.quaternion`, the per-hand, per-frame cursor-
   tracking look-at. Confirmed directly, live: 2 different hands, facing
   2 different directions at that moment, produced 2 DIFFERENT local bone
   quaternions for the IDENTICAL slider values on the identical finger --
   proof the ported axis math wasn't actually anatomically fixed the way
   it is for HANDO. Fixed by extending `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` with an
   optional `excludeQuat` parameter (that hand's own `wrapper.quaternion`),
   factored out of the bone's world quaternion before the axis conversion
   -- the quaternion algebra shows this exactly cancels the existing
   pre-rotation by `cloneBaseQuat`, leaving the axis fixed relative to the
   mesh's own raw bind-pose frame regardless of either rotation. Does NOT
   apply to Tip Twist's own axis (computed live from 2 bones' actual
   current world positions -- already correct, excluding anything from it
   would be wrong).

Verified directly, live, after each fix (not just code-reviewed):
- Grid-alignment: isolated Hide Wrist (60%, 30%), Hide Wrist + single-axis
  Whole-Hand Rotation, Hide Wrist + all 3 rotation axes combined, across
  multiple hands (index 0/50/200/300/400) at different grid positions and
  different live cursor-tracking orientations -- every case measured
  within floating-point precision (~1e-14 to exactly 0) of the grid point.
- Finger-curl anatomical consistency: after the fix, 5 hands (indices 0,
  50, 150, 300, 450) with genuinely different live wrapper orientations
  produced byte-identical local bone quaternions for the same slider
  values (curlIndex=150, splayIndex=80) -- confirmed via direct quaternion
  comparison, not visual inspection.
- A full combined stress test (Hide Wrist 40%, Whole-Hand Rotation Y 60°,
  Curl Index 150%, Wrist Bend -30° all active at once) still measured
  exact grid-alignment (~1e-16 to ~1e-17).
- 0 console errors across every reload this round; a live screenshot
  confirmed the field renders the new default pose (asymmetric per-finger
  curl, matching HANDO's own defaults) with no visual regressions.

Corrected `CODE_SUMMARY.txt` (new architecture entry #11 for Pose, plus 4
new GOTCHAS entries documenting the 3 bugs above and the fix) and
`PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`. Took the opportunity to consolidate
`PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`'s own "Recently completed" section (which had grown
into exactly the session-by-session historical narrative its own header
warns against) into a shorter current-state summary -- the full detail
this consolidation replaced remains permanently in this file's own prior
entries, nothing was lost, only de-duplicated from the live-document copy.

Not yet committed/pushed.

2026-09-13  -  ~2:40 AM - 4:04 AM EDT

User tested the pushed Pose/Hide Wrist build and reported 2 problems: "1.
theyre not cropping correctly when the app is actually following the
cursor. 2. When cropped, the hand should be placed in the grid from the
crop point, not the original base of the 3d model." Extensive
re-verification of the pushed code (both on `localhost` and, once the
user shared it, the actual deployed `handy-dandies.vercel.app` URL with
`?dev=1`, live mouse movement, and the exact same measurement approach
already proven earlier) found the code correct in every test -- grid-
alignment exact, clipping genuinely removing geometry, `armToWristDist`
identical across hands. Confirmed the deployment WAS serving the latest
code (`main.js?v=19`, matching `25d47ce`), ruling out a stale-cache
explanation. Asked the user for a screenshot/recording rather than keep
guessing blindly.

User provided a screen recording (saved to
`data/SCREENCAP/Recording 2026-09-13 032926.mp4`). Extracted 10 frames
directly via a local OpenCV script (no `ffmpeg` available in this
environment) rather than trying to infer the issue from description alone
-- immediately visible: hands in different parts of the same frame showed
dramatically different amounts of visible forearm, some looking almost
fully uncropped. Root-caused by direct measurement, not further guessing:
sampled 11 hands scattered around the field at that exact moment --
`armToWristDist` (the TRUE 3D length of the remaining segment) was
`14.630384832518` world units for every single one (differing only in the
15th decimal place), and grid-alignment held exactly for all of them too,
including the specific hands that looked least cropped. What varied was
the SCREEN-PROJECTED length of that same fixed 3D segment -- 17px to 56px,
over 3x -- purely because each hand independently tracks the cursor and
so points in a different 3D direction relative to the camera at any given
moment; a hand facing more edge-on to the camera shows the segment near
its true length, one facing more toward/away from the camera shows it
heavily foreshortened. **Not a bug** -- reported the exact measurements to
the user rather than a vague "looks fine to me," and explained plainly
that "cropped equally at all times" as a literal on-screen appearance
isn't achievable with a fixed-fraction 3D clip on independently-rotating
objects.

User's actual follow-up request, once this was clear: stop thinking of it
as a crop and think of it as arm length instead -- both short and long
variants still spawn from the same grid plane, but the amount should react
to live distance from the cursor (closer = shorter arm), with 4 new
controls specified via this project's own `*D*` shorthand:
- **Min / Max Arm Length** -- "a slider that can take multiple toggles,
  similar to the color gradient slider in DickoClicko. Left side is 0 and
  right side is 1. 1 is fully cropped, 0 is none." DICKOCLICKO's own
  `type:'gradient'` control (a Photoshop-style draggable bar,
  `cfg[key]` = `[{pos,color}]`) lives in ITS OWN monolithic index.html, not
  the shared `devpanel.js` engine this project reuses verbatim from HANDO
  -- ported the UI PATTERN (a draggable bar, values persisted through the
  existing control machinery), not the code, since this needs exactly 2
  numeric handles (min/max crop %), no color picker.
- **Length Scaling Curve** -- "one of those input ui things where its like
  a 2 axes graph and theres a curve showing the relationship between
  distance and length, and i can manipulate the curve." No prior art for
  this in the workspace -- built fresh: a small SVG plot, X=normalized
  cursor distance (0-1), Y=raw crop fraction (0-1), draggable points
  connected by straight segments (piecewise-linear, not a smoothed spline
  -- a deliberate simplicity tradeoff, flagged to the user), click empty
  space to add a point, double-click to remove (floored at 2, the 2
  domain-spanning endpoints always present, only their Y draggable).
- **Reactive Arm Length** checkbox, and **Default Arm Length** (the
  original Hide Wrist slider, relabeled, used when Reactive is off) --
  both straightforward.

Neither new widget is a `devpanel.js` control type -- per this project's
own explicit convention ("do not fork it, add controls via main.js's own
DEV_GROUPS instead"), both are built as custom DOM/SVG laid directly over
their own control's existing (hidden) generic `type:'text'` input, driven
via a `commitTextControl()` helper that sets `.value` and dispatches a
real `'input'` event -- routing through devPanel.js's own existing
`commit()`/localStorage-save path exactly as if the user had typed into a
plain text box, so Copy/Save/Reset all keep working without either widget
knowing anything about devPanel.js internals.

Architecture change: arm-length compensation (`applyHandArmLength()`,
the direct descendant of the prior round's `updateClonePoseTransform()`)
moved from a ONE-TIME, SHARED, slider-triggered calculation to PER-HAND,
PER-FRAME -- necessary since Reactive mode means every hand can have a
genuinely different crop value that changes continuously as the cursor
moves. Reuses the live cursor-distance value `updateRenderOrder()` already
computes for render ordering (no new per-frame distance calculation
needed) rather than adding a redundant one. `cloneBaseQuat` (Whole-Hand
Rotation's own shared quaternion) stays event-driven, computed only when
those sliders change (renamed to `updateCloneBaseQuat()`, position-only
logic split out of it).

Hit and fixed 1 real bug during implementation, caught immediately on the
first live reload rather than shipped: a TDZ crash
(`ReferenceError: Cannot access 'armLengthWidgetResyncs' before
initialization`) from declaring 3 new cached-state variables
(`armLengthRangeParsed`, `armLengthCurveParsed`, `armLengthWidgetResyncs`)
next to the functions that use them (well after `initDevPanel()`) while
calling 2 of those functions immediately after `initDevPanel()` -- before
the module's own top-to-bottom evaluation ever reached those
declarations. Same crash class as this project's own documented
`initDevPanel()`-detach-onChange gotcha. Fixed by moving all 3 to this
file's own early shared-state block instead.

Also added a small per-frame resync poll (`armLengthWidgetResyncs`,
checked once per frame from `animate()`) so either widget's own displayed
state stays in sync if devPanel.js's Reset/Copy-restore path ever writes
a new value into the underlying hidden input directly (which it does,
without firing an `'input'` event) -- couldn't directly verify this ONE
mechanism firing live in this session's own test environment, since a
pre-existing, already-documented tool quirk suspends
`requestAnimationFrame` entirely whenever the Browser pane isn't
displayed (confirmed directly with a vanilla, page-independent rAF
counter: 0 ticks in 500ms even with `document.hidden` reporting false) --
its logic is code-reviewed and pattern-matches the already-verified
drag-commit path, but flagged as worth a real on-device check rather than
claimed as fully verified.

Verified directly, live, working around that same rAF-suspension quirk by
manually invoking `updateRenderOrder()` via the debug hook wherever a real
animation frame couldn't be relied on to have run naturally:
- Dragging the Min/Max range handles and the curve's points both update
  `cfg.armLengthRange`/`cfg.armLengthCurve` correctly (confirmed via
  direct pointer-event simulation against the real DOM elements, not just
  code reading).
- With Reactive on and the cursor moved: the closest-to-cursor hand
  (distance 111.08) measured crop T=0.660; the farthest (distance 216.49)

- Grid-alignment held within floating-point precision of exactly 0 (2.7e-15
  and 6.2e-17) for BOTH of those hands despite their genuinely different,
  continuously-varying crop values -- the "same plane" requirement
  survives Reactive mode intact.
- 0 console errors on live reload after the TDZ fix; a screenshot with
  Reactive on and the cursor positioned near one corner of the field
  showed the expected visual gradient (shorter arms near the cursor,
  longer arms farther away).

2026-09-13 (continued once more)  -  ~7:31 PM - 8:03 PM EDT

4 bug reports plus 2 new feature requests, arriving together: "1. Dev
Panel minimize doesnt work. The settings dissappear, but the full panel
is still there. 2. *D* Crop Wrist Checkbox. To turn the cropping on and
off. 3. Wrist cropping min max doesnt seem to work. I set the default
and max at .5 or less but i still see full arms. 4. Why is it that
sometimes when i place the cursor far away, the hands furthest from the
cursor start dissappearing, even though i have a min length of more than
0." Then, mid-turn: a mouse-tracking log request, and (from a reference

curve, not angled line segments.

Investigated items 3/4 first via 2 more user-provided screen recordings
(`data/SCREENCAP/Recording 2026-09-13 193115.mp4` and
`...193218.mp4`), extracted to frames directly via the same local OpenCV
approach as the prior recording -- visually confirmed a real, dramatic
disparity in visible arm length across the field at a single moment,
worth root-causing rather than guessing at.

1. **Dev Panel minimize bug** -- root-caused directly in `devpanel.js`
   (the shared, reused-verbatim engine, not this project's own code):
   `.dp-collapsed`'s CSS only hides `.dp-body`/`.dp-resize`; it can't
   shrink the panel itself, because `panel.style.height` is an explicit
   inline px value (for resize-drag persistence) that always wins over a
   stylesheet rule, AND `.dp-panel`'s own `min-height: 140px` applies
   unconditionally too -- confirmed live in 2 steps: fixing `height`
   alone still left the box floored at 140px (measured
   `getBoundingClientRect().height` directly), only overriding BOTH
   `height` and `minHeight` on collapse (and restoring both on expand)
   actually shrunk it to the header's own height (confirmed: 36px
   collapsed vs. 677px expanded).
2. **Crop Wrist checkbox** -- new `cropWristEnabled` master on/off,
   gating the entire Arm Length system (`computeArmLengthT()` returns 0
   directly when off, before even checking Reactive/Default). Verified
   live: off + a high Default Arm Length value still produced crop 0.
3. **The ".5 still shows full arms" report was a real, confirmed unit-
   scale mismatch, not a code bug.** Typed "0.5" into the live Default
   Arm Length control and confirmed directly: `cfg.hideWrist` became
   exactly `0.5` (0.5% crop), not `50`. `hideWrist` is a plain 0-100
   slider matching every other percent control in this project -- 0.5 IS
   a valid point on that scale, just not the one the user meant. Root
   cause of the CONFUSION (not the value itself): the Length Scaling
   Curve widget's own axis captions read "(0-1)" at the time, which
   likely primed the "type a fraction" instinct for this OTHER,
   differently-scaled control. Fixed by relabeling the curve's own
   captions to read in percent too ("Distance From Cursor (%)... Crop
   (0%=None, 100%=Full)"), so every control in this feature speaks the
   same units -- not by changing any control's actual scale.
4. **The far-cursor "disappearing" report was a real, confirmed bug in
   the Reactive normalization math.** `computeArmLengthT()` used to
   normalize each hand's cursor distance against a FIXED reference
   (`sceneState.fieldRadius * ARM_LENGTH_DISTANCE_NORM`) -- measured
   directly: with the cursor placed far outside the whole field (2000,
   2000), BOTH the nearest hand (distance 2654) and the farthest hand
   (distance 3003) in the field landed on the IDENTICAL crop value
   (0.2, the Min bound) -- once every hand's distance exceeds the fixed
   reference, all relative variation collapses to the same extreme.
   Fixed by adding a cheap pre-pass in `updateRenderOrder()` that finds
   the CURRENT field's own live min/max distance-to-cursor each frame,
   normalizing against THAT range instead of a fixed constant. Re-
   verified with the cursor placed even farther away (3000, 3000,
   distances ~4000-4400): nearest hand measured crop 0.9 (Max bound),
   farthest measured 0.3 (Min bound) -- the full gradient held instead
   of collapsing.

Also shipped, from the mid-turn requests:
- **Mouse Tracking Log** (Debug group): a click log describing what each
  click actually triggers in this app (Dev Panel interaction, or Camera
  Pan/Dolly via OrbitControls, based on `e.target`/button -- an honest
  description of this app's real click semantics, not a placeholder),
  a regular interval-driven cursor-position log, an interval slider
  (100-5000ms), and an on/off checkbox for the regular log specifically
  (the click log always runs). A plain scrollable `<pre>` appended
  directly to the Debug group's own DOM, session-only (not persisted --
  nothing meaningful to restore in a log). Verified live: dispatched
  clicks with different buttons/targets and confirmed each produced the
  correct trigger description; confirmed the interval timer fires
  correctly at a shortened 100ms test interval.
- **Length Scaling Curve is now a real smooth spline** (Catmull-Rom,
  C1-continuous, passes exactly through every point), per direct
  correction with a Photoshop/Lightroom tone-curve-editor reference
  image ("ignore the colors in that image"). The SVG widget draws the
  SAME `evaluateArmLengthCurve()` function the real per-hand math calls,
  fine-sampled at 48 points -- not a separately-approximated visual.
  Verified live: added a 3rd point and confirmed the sampled path's own
  rate-of-change varied smoothly through it (no linear slope break),
  confirming genuine curvature.

2 more small bugs caught and fixed live before shipping, neither
originally reported:
- Dragging either new widget while its own Pose group was COLLAPSED
  corrupted the stored value to `{"min":null,"max":null}` --
  `getBoundingClientRect()` on a hidden element returns an all-zero
  rect, dividing by 0 producing `NaN`, which `JSON.stringify` silently
  turns into `null`. Fixed with a zero-rect guard in both widgets' own
  drag handlers.
- The new click handler's `e.target.closest('.dp-panel')` threw when
  `e.target` wasn't a real Element (confirmed via a synthetic test event
  dispatched directly on `document`) -- fixed with optional chaining.

User reported, with a new screen recording (`Recording 2026-09-13

you fixed didnt seem fixed in terms of the wrist cropping." Investigated

Correlated the recording's own file timestamps (Modify 20:26:44.42,
36.27s duration -> start ~20:26:08) against the click log's wall-clock
timestamps to determine the recording's own timeline: Crop Wrist (Master
On/Off) was ON for roughly the first 21s of the recording, then the user
clicked it OFF (log: "Setting changed: Crop Wrist (Master On/Off) =
false" at 8:26:29 PM) for the remainder.

This environment's own documented `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight === 0`
quirk (CLAUDE.md GOTCHAS) blocked the first attempt at live cursor-target
raycasting -- diagnosed directly (`rayDir` came back `[null,null,null]`
because the NDC computation divided by 0) and worked around by using
`resize_window` to set a real 1536x864 viewport, then manually replaying
this project's own `applyRendererSize()` logic (camera aspect/projection,
renderer/composer/outlinePass size) since the render loop's own self-heal
for this never got a frame to run in this same environment.

With that fixed, 2 direct measurements:
1. Crop Wrist OFF: confirmed via `hand.currentArmLengthT` that EVERY one
   of 510 hands reads exactly 0 (no crop applied, code path returns 0
   immediately when `cfg.cropWristEnabled` is false, as designed).
   Reproducing the video's own Crop-OFF cursor position (bottom-left,
   `~(60,855)`) and measuring the exact same `rForearmBend`->`rHand` bone
   segment used by the clip-plane math: every hand in the visible column
   shares an IDENTICAL true 3D length (14.63 units -- the same figure
   measured in the original foreshortening investigation), yet that
   segment's own on-screen projected length still ranged ~11-27px within
   just a 20-hand sample (and further down the same column in the full
   video). Pure perspective/orientation foreshortening, not cropping --
   confirmed with T=0 for every one of them.
2. Crop Wrist ON + Reactive ON: cursor placed at a fixed screen position,
   confirmed the crop math itself is correctly monotonic by live cursor
   distance -- nearest-to-cursor hand measured T=0.9 (max crop, per the
   default 30-90 range and default linear curve), farthest measured
   T=0.3 (min crop), exactly as designed.

Conclusion, reported to the user rather than guessing at another patch:
the Arm Length/Crop feature's own logic is verified correct in both
states. What's still visible in the recording as "cropping isn't fixed"
is the SAME class of pure perspective/orientation foreshortening already
diagnosed and reported as not-a-bug earlier this project (see the
2026-09-13 03:43 AM entry) -- inherent to any hand rotating to face a
moving cursor under a perspective camera, present with or without any
cropping at all, and not something the Arm Length feature itself causes
or can fix. No code changed this round. Corrected `PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`
with the finding and the open architectural question (accept as
inherent vs. reduce camera perspective distortion, e.g. toward an
orthographic projection -- a real architectural change needing the
user's own approval per CLAUDE.md §0a) for the user to weigh in on.

Presented the finding directly to the user with 3 options (leave as-is /
move toward an orthographic camera / something else is still wrong).
**User chose to leave it as-is.** Recorded the decision in
`PROJECT_PROGRESS.md` -- no further action planned on this item.

2026-09-13 (continued once more, correcting the round above)  -  ~9:09 PM - 9:55 PM EDT

Immediately after the round above shipped ("leave the foreshortening
as-is"), the user pushed back hard: "but bro. dont you see in the video
arms dissappearing completely?" Re-examined the same recording's actual
pixels (not just the downsampled frame view) and found genuine 2-4px
fragments where a hand had been -- more severe than the "looks shorter"
framing the prior round's own AskUserQuestion had used. Re-opened the
decision with the user rather than assuming the prior "leave as-is"
answer still applied to this more severe picture.

First hypothesis (extreme edge-on orientation, same class as the
foreshortening finding, just more severe) did not survive contact with
the user's own counter-evidence: "no its not the dissappearng happens
when my mouse is very far from the grid. The furthest hands dissappear...
The furtherst hands should be pointing at the cursor. so they would not
be pointing at the camera" -- a correct, decisive rebuttal (a hand
rotating to face a genuinely distant point can't simultaneously be
edge-on to a camera pointed at the field's center).

Reproduced the exact video moment (8-10s and 14-16s, per the user's own
timestamps) via OpenCV frame extraction, then asked for and received the
user's own Copy Settings JSON dump (17x17 field, camera FOV 35 at
Z=352.46, `cropWristEnabled`/`reactiveArmLengthEnabled` both FALSE the
entire time -- a fact the mouse-tracking log had already shown, since
neither setting was ever toggled during the recording) to reproduce their
EXACT scene rather than guessing at settings. Loaded it into a fresh
browser's `localStorage` under `handyDandies.devSettings` (the exact key
`initDevPanel({storageKeyPrefix:'handyDandies'})` reads) and reloaded --
confirmed live that the reported cursor position (49,47) reproduced the
video's own narrow-column collapse exactly, with Crop Wrist definitively
OFF, ruling out the Arm Length feature entirely as a cause.

Diagnosed via direct pixel-readback (`gl.readPixels`) cross-referenced
against each hand's own verified world/screen position (confirmed correct
by temporarily rendering a single isolated hand in solid red and matching
its expected vs. actual screen position): only 2 of 289 hands actually
rendered anything at their own calculated position; the other 287 showed
pure background color there, even though their positions, rotations, and
render-order values were all individually confirmed correct. Ruled out,
in order, frustum culling (forcing `frustumCulled=false` on every mesh
changed nothing), the EffectComposer/OutlinePass pipeline (bypassing it
entirely with a direct `renderer.render()` call reproduced the identical
failure), and stale camera matrices (`updateMatrixWorld(true)` before
projecting changed nothing).

Root cause: `clippingPlanes` (for Hide Wrist/Arm Length) lives on the
MATERIAL, not the mesh, but every field hand's fill mesh AND outline mesh
shared ONE material instance with ONE shared `wristClipPlane`, mutated
in place per-hand via `onBeforeRender` right before that hand's own draw
call -- the original design's own comment asserted this "works correctly
even though the fill/outline materials are shared across all 510 hands,"
which this investigation disproves directly. Three.js does not re-resolve
a shared material's clipping-plane uniform per individual draw call the
way that assumed; with 289 hands sharing one material, almost every hand
ended up clipped against a stale/foreign plane (whichever hand's own
`onBeforeRender` last wrote to it before the GPU actually consumed the
uniform), discarding its entire mesh. Confirmed via a direct falsification
test: setting `material.clippingPlanes = []` on the shared material,
with nothing else changed, immediately took visible hands from 2/289 to
288/289. This explains every symptom reported across both this round and
the prior one -- happens with Crop Wrist on OR off (the clip-plane update
runs unconditionally for every hand regardless of `cropWristEnabled`);
happens specifically for far-cursor/farthest-hand cases only insofar as
whichever hand's plane "wins" the race for a given render happens to
discard geometry differently depending on scene state, not because of
any actual distance-based logic; and was never a foreshortening effect
at all, despite superficially resembling one in the recordings examined
so far (real foreshortening likely still contributes some visual
variation on top, per the earlier investigations, but is not the
"disappearing" mechanism).

**Fix implemented** (`main.js`): every field hand now gets its own
`.clone()` of the toon material AND the outline material inside
`rebuildField()`, each with `clippingPlanes: [hand.wristClipPlane]` -- a
genuine per-hand `THREE.Plane` instance stored on the hand object, not a
shared module-level one. `updateWristClipPlaneForHand()` now mutates
`hand.wristClipPlane` instead of a shared plane. Added
`toonMaterial.customProgramCacheKey = () => 'handToonMaterial'` so all
289+ clones (plus the template) still share ONE compiled WebGL program --
only the clip-plane uniform VALUES differ per clone, not the plane count
or shader structure, so without this three.js would otherwise recompile
the same shader hundreds of times. Added 2 helpers, `forEachToonMaterial()`
and `forEachOutlineMaterial()`, and rewired every existing "mutate the
shared material" control (Toon Base Tint, Show Wireframe, Outline Color,
Hull Outline Thickness, `rebuildGradientMap()`'s own toon-gradient
rebuild) through them, so every one of those dev-panel controls still
correctly updates all per-hand clones plus the template -- the template
itself stays directly assigned to the single Pose Preview hand, which has
no per-hand clipping conflict to begin with (only one instance uses it).
Corrected 2 stale comments that had asserted the shared-plane approach
worked (in `createToonMaterial()` and `updateWristClipPlaneForHand()`'s
own doc comments) and one unrelated pre-existing stale comment in
`CODE_SUMMARY.txt` claiming this project had "no clippingPlanes support"
(predates the Hide Wrist feature, never updated when it shipped).

Verified live: reproduced the user's own exact failing scenario (their
real settings dump, cursor at their own logged (49,47)) before and after
the fix -- 2/289 visible hands before, 276/289 after (the remaining 13
are plausible legitimate off-screen/dense-grid-overlap cases in a 17x17
field, not separately investigated). Re-confirmed the Arm Length feature
itself (Crop Wrist ON + Reactive ON) still produces the correct monotonic
crop gradient afterward (nearest T=0.9, farthest T=0.3, matching every
prior verification) -- the bug was entirely in the SHARED clip-plane
mechanism, never in the per-hand T-value math. Corrected `CODE_SUMMARY.txt`
(new GOTCHAS entry, and the #7 architecture-summary item) and
`PROJECT_PROGRESS.md` (removed the now-incorrect "leave as-is" foreshortening
resolution, replaced with this fix in Recently Completed). Cache-buster
bumped `index.html`'s `src/main.js?v=26` -> `?v=27`.

2026-09-14  -  ~12:06 AM - 12:35 AM EDT

Direct user report, same day as the wrist-clip-plane fix: "Keylight color
no longer seems to work. When I set it to white, I still see color being
rendered on the hands. Though when I set it to red, they do turn red."
Investigated as a likely regression from that same fix's own
`customProgramCacheKey` addition, since it was the one change that
specifically affects how the toon material's `onBeforeCompile`
customization gets wired up across the newly-cloned per-hand materials.

First fix attempt (remove `customProgramCacheKey` entirely) did NOT
resolve it -- confirmed live by reading back a field hand's own actual
compiled fragment shader source (`gl.getShaderSource()` via
`renderer.properties.get(material).currentProgram.fragmentShader`)
before and after: neither contained the `toonLuma`/duotone-blend code
`createToonMaterial()`'s own `onBeforeCompile` is supposed to inject.
Tried forcing a GENUINELY unique cache key per material
(`material.uuid`) as a second attempt, specifically to rule out any
cross-material program-sharing explanation entirely -- still no effect,
proving the bug was never actually about program caching at all.

Real root cause, found by inspecting the clone's own `onBeforeCompile`
property directly: it read back as three.js's stock
`Material.prototype` no-op (`onBeforeCompile(){}`), not the custom
function assigned in `createToonMaterial()`. **`Material.clone()`/
`.copy()` does not carry over a custom `onBeforeCompile` override** --
it isn't part of the fixed property list three.js's own `.copy()`
implementation handles. Every `toonMaterial.clone()` added in the
previous round's wrist-clip-plane fix silently lost this customization
the moment it was cloned, reverting every field hand to plain,
unmodified texture-mapped `MeshToonMaterial` rendering: rim lighting and
the toon-tint/texture duotone blend gone entirely, while direct lighting
(Key Light Color) kept working normally since that's a separate,
standard three.js mechanism the clone bug never touched -- exactly
matching the reported symptom (white light doesn't neutralize an
already-colored texture; only the broken duotone override could have,
while red light still visibly tints whatever's there regardless).

**Fix implemented:** explicitly re-assign
`skinnedMesh.material.onBeforeCompile = toonMaterial.onBeforeCompile`
immediately after `.clone()` in `rebuildField()`. Also removed
`customProgramCacheKey` for good (confirmed not the cause, and not worth
reintroducing) -- three.js now compiles one WebGL program per hand's own
material, same cost it always would have without that speculative
optimization.

Verified live: sampling the same hand's own rendered pixel while
toggling Key Light Color between white and red now gives neutral gray
`(132,132,132)` and red-tinted `(132,0,0)` respectively, fully
reversible in both directions. Re-confirmed the wrist-clip-plane fix
from the round above still holds after this change: 274/289 hands
visible at the same test cursor position used in that round's own
verification (consistent with its 276/289 measurement, the small
difference plausibly ordinary render-order/overlap variation between
runs, not re-investigated separately). Confirmed via a genuinely fresh
browser tab that no console errors remain (the errors seen during the
investigation itself were from the 2 live, in-browser experimental
attempts, not from the final committed code).

Corrected `CODE_SUMMARY.txt` (new GOTCHAS entry immediately following
the wrist-clip-plane one, plus a correction to that entry's own now-
inaccurate `customProgramCacheKey` claim). Cache-buster bumped twice
during the investigation (`?v=27` -> `?v=28` for the first, ultimately
insufficient attempt; `?v=28` -> `?v=29` for the real fix) -- `?v=29` is
the version actually deployed.

2026-09-14 (continued)  -  ~1:21 AM - 1:40 AM EDT

Direct user report: "when i open the app on desktop, the dev panel seems
to default to Landscape tab. It shouldnt." Root-caused in
`src/devpanel/devPanel.js` (the generic dev-panel engine, "reused
verbatim from HANDO" per this project's own CLAUDE.md file map -- see
Open questions below for the cross-project implication).

`realDeviceClass()` (the function that classifies the current viewport
as desktop/mobile/landscape) is called exactly once, synchronously, at
the very top of `initDevPanel()`, to set the panel's initially-active
tab (`editingDevice`). This project already has a DOCUMENTED gotcha that
`window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` can read wrong at that exact early
script-parse moment (main.js's own renderer has a frame-by-frame self-
heal specifically because of this) -- but the dev panel's own tab-
selection had no equivalent correction: if the viewport misread once at
that moment, the wrong tab stayed "active" for the rest of the session,
since the existing `resize` event listener only re-syncs per-device
VALUES for a genuine later resize, never which tab is shown as active,
and doesn't fire at all if the viewport was simply wrong once at load
with no resize event following it.

Reproduced the exact mechanism live: this environment's own browser-
automation tool has its own separate, previously-documented quirk where
`window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` read as 0 via its JS-exec query path
immediately after a fresh navigation (a tool characteristic, not a page
bug) -- used this to confirm a fresh load's `dp-tab-active` class was
briefly absent from every tab (consistent with `realDeviceClass()`
having computed something unexpected from `w=0,h=0` at the moment
`initDevPanel()` ran), before the page's own real dimensions became
available a few seconds later.

**Fix implemented:** added a short self-heal window (up to 30
`requestAnimationFrame` ticks, ~0.5s) right after the panel is built --
re-checks `realDeviceClass()` each frame and corrects `editingDevice`
(plus the visible active-tab styling) once if it differs, but stops
immediately the moment the user manually clicks a tab themselves (a new
`userSwitchedTab` flag), and stops after the frame budget regardless --
this is specifically a load-time correction, not a standing "tab always
follows window resize" behavior (which would break a legitimate
workflow: manually viewing Mobile settings, then resizing the desktop
browser window while doing so, should NOT snap back to Desktop).

Verified live: a fresh load on a genuine 1024x768 viewport correctly
shows the Desktop tab active; manually clicking Landscape afterward
switches and STAYS switched (checked again 600ms later, well past the
self-heal window) -- the fix doesn't fight a real user selection.
Bumped `devpanel.js`'s own cache-busting import query in `main.js`
(`?v=4` -> `?v=5`) and `index.html`'s (`?v=29` -> `?v=30`).
