2026-09-14 (continued)  -  ~1:55 AM - 2:07 AM EDT

Direct new feature request: "Every hand is oriented the same way more or
less (palm facing up)... I want to add a new checkbox. When Selected,
Every hand will always rotate to have the palm facing the cursor." Mid-
task, 2 direct corrections while investigating bone data: "when i say
rotate the hand. I dont mean use any of the rigs, I just meant rotate
the entire model" and "you shouldnt be doing any posing work" -- both
confirmed the approach already in progress (measuring bind-pose bone
POSITIONS only, as fixed reference vectors, exactly like the existing
`alignQuat` setup already does, never touching a bone's own live
rotation/curl) rather than requiring a course correction.

Investigated the existing cursor-tracking mechanism first: each hand's
WRAPPER (not the clone/skeleton) gets `lookAt()`-based rotation every
frame, aiming the `alignQuat`-aligned local -Z axis (the bind-pose
wrist-to-middle-fingertip direction) at the cursor. Needed an alternate
mode where the PALM-NORMAL direction, not the fingertip direction, is
what's aimed at the cursor -- without disturbing the existing lookAt
code's own shape, since it already works correctly for the default mode.

Measured the palm-plane normal the same way as `pointDir`/`alignQuat`
(bind-pose bone world positions at model load, identity-transform
frame): `(pinkyBase - wrist) x (indexBase - wrist)`, bones `rHand`/
`rIndex1`/`rPinky1`. Calibrated the cross-product SIGN live rather than
assuming it: transformed both candidate directions through `alignQuat`
and a real hand's own current `wrapper.quaternion` (a hand below the
cursor, default mode active) and checked which one measured facing AWAY
from the camera, matching this project's own already-confirmed reference
behavior from an earlier round ("hands under the cursor show the back of
their hand to the camera... this is correct"). `pinky x index` (that
exact order) was the outward palm normal.

Implemented as a single fixed correction quaternion composed onto the
EXISTING per-frame lookAt quaternion, not a parallel rotation system:
`palmFaceCorrectionQuat = Quaternion.setFromUnitVectors(palmNormalRaw
.applyQuaternion(alignQuat), (0,0,-1))`, computed once at model load
(same block as `alignQuat`). Given lookAt's own guarantee that its
quaternion maps local -Z to the cursor direction, and the correction
quaternion by construction maps the palm-normal direction to local -Z,
composing `desired.multiply(correctionQuat)` (three.js's own apply-
right-side-first order) redirects the SAME lookAt result so the palm
lands where the fingertip direction used to. Whole-wrapper rotation only
-- zero skeleton/pose/curl code touched, per the mid-task corrections.

Verified live, 2 ways before shipping: (1) mathematically --
independently re-derived the same correction via `skeleton.boneInverses`
(bind-pose extraction that works regardless of the skeleton's current
LIVE pose, unlike `getWorldPosition()` on a posed hand) and confirmed
`worldPalmNormal.dot(dirToCursor) = 0.999999995` for a hand below a
centered cursor -- correct to floating-point precision; (2) visually --
forced every hand's `wrapper.quaternion` directly (undamped) with the
feature on, cursor centered, and compared cropped/upscaled screen
regions above/below/left of the cursor: above vs. below hands showed a
clear vertical-flip relationship, left showed the hand's long axis
rotated to roughly horizontal -- matching the "roughly 180 above, roughly
90 to the side" pattern described in the request. Confirmed zero new
console errors in a fresh tab.

Added as `palmFacesCursor` (Cursor Tracking group, default OFF -- see
CODE_SUMMARY.txt's GOTCHAS for the full technical account, including the
quaternion-composition derivation and both verification passes in full).
Corrected 2 other CODE_SUMMARY.txt lines discovered stale while editing
this same section (architecture item #5/#6 still described the toon/
outline materials as "shared, not cloned," predating the wrist-clip-
plane fix earlier this session -- unrelated to this feature, fixed in
passing since directly adjacent). Cache-buster bumped `index.html`'s
`src/main.js?v=30` -> `?v=31`.

Mouse Tracking Log port -- direct request: "I updated the Dev Panel Template
to have a mouse log system similar to ours. Update ours to match the
template if there are features missing." Ported the template's click-
classification layer onto this project's existing Mouse Tracking Log
(which already had a click log + interval cursor-position log; the
template's own log was a separate, newer system built to extend that same
idea further) -- NOT a fork of devpanel.js's own generic engine, an
extension of `main.js`'s own existing log, since this project's log is
main.js-based by design.

Added:
- Replaced the single `pointerdown` click listener with a `pointerdown`/
  `pointerup` pair: `pointerdown` just records time/position/button/target;
  `pointerup` classifies what actually happened, since neither multi-click
  nor drag-release is knowable until release. Right-click logs immediately
  (`Right-click at (x,y) -> <trigger>`); a release held past
  `MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS` or moved past `MOUSE_LOG_MOVE_THRESHOLD_PX`
  (10px) logs as `Drag-release ... (heldMs:N)`; a quick left/middle click
  is debounced (`MOUSE_LOG_MULTICLICK_MS`) into Click/Double-click/Triple-
  click the same way the template's own version does, so a fast double-
  click doesn't log as 2 unrelated single clicks.
- `describeMouseLogTrigger(e)` -- the original inline trigger-description
  logic (Dev Panel interaction / Camera Pan / Camera Zoom-Dolly, per this
  project's own actual OrbitControls bindings) factored out unchanged so
  both the right-click and drag-release paths can reuse it.
- `logMouseLogViewportContext(reason)` -- ported from the template's own
  viewport-context pattern: one `Viewport (start): WxH` entry when the log
  widget is first built, and one more `Viewport (resize): WxH` entry only
  on a REAL size change (guarded against firing on every 'resize' event
  regardless of whether the size actually changed).
- A "Save" button next to the existing "Copy" button on the Mouse Tracking
  Log widget (`buildMouseTrackingLogWidget()`), same Blob+`<a download>`
  client-side file-save pattern as the template's own Save (kept this
  project's own flat-text log format rather than porting the template's
  separate headered-.md structure, since this log has no such sectioning
  to preserve).

Deliberately NOT ported: the template's full touch-gesture classification
(tap/double-tap/swipe/pinch) -- this project's CURSOR-TRACKING mechanic has
no touch-input equivalent by design (see this project's own CLAUDE.md,
same convention DOTFLICKO uses for its own mouse-only interaction), so
there is nothing for a touch gesture to actually describe here. Disclosed
as a judgment call, not silently dropped.

Verified live (fresh server after an unrelated static-server connection
reset, see GOTCHAS): single-click, double-click, and right-click all
classified correctly via synthetic `PointerEvent`s; a held-600ms release
logged as `Drag-release ... (heldMs:607)`; the widget's initial build
logged `Viewport (start): 1024x768`; a genuine `resize` dispatch logged
`Viewport (resize): 375x812` (the Browser pane's own viewport-emulation
tool changes `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` without dispatching a real
`resize` DOM event, confirmed separately -- not a bug in this project's
listener, which fires correctly on an actual resize); the Save button
fired its Blob/download code with no error (flashed "Saved!"). 2 stale
console errors (`net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`, "Failed to load hand model")

2026-09-14  -  ~03:59 AM - 04:09 AM EDT (bounded by checkpoints; see Verified note)

Fixed "Palm Faces Cursor" rotating 180 degrees backwards -- direct user
report: "the palm face cursor is doing the exact opposite. rotate them
180 degrees." The feature's own palm-plane-normal calibration (from an
earlier round this same project) was `pinkyVec x indexVec` -- the exact
OPPOSITE of the true outward palm normal, so the feature aimed the BACK
of the hand at the cursor instead of the palm. Fixed by swapping the
cross-product operand order to `indexVec x pinkyVec`: a well-known
identity, a x b = -(b x a), makes this a clean, exact negation of the
prior vector -- since the palm normal and the back-of-hand normal are
themselves exact opposites on the same approximate hand-plane, negating
which one feeds the correction is precisely the 180-degree flip asked
for, with no other math touched (the lookAt/composition logic that maps
this normal onto the cursor direction was already correct and unchanged).

Verification note, disclosed honestly: this project's toon-shaded gray
mesh has no strongly distinct palm-vs-back surface texture (no palm
lines/creases rendered), so a literal "does this pixel-level render show
palm or back" screenshot comparison wasn't reliably conclusive at this
render fidelity -- attempted via a temporary close-up camera reposition
(frozen OrbitControls, one hand's wrapper manually rotated to face the
relocated camera) but the resulting silhouette was ambiguous either way.
Also discovered and disclosed a real limitation in the ORIGINAL
implementation's own dot-product self-check (the "confirmed... dot
product = 0.999999995" verification from that earlier round): that check
is mathematically tautological -- it always self-confirms regardless of
which cross-product order is used, since it re-measures the SAME formula
that built the correction in the first place, so it could never have
caught this sign bug even though it "passed." Relied instead on the
airtight mathematical guarantee (cross-product anticommutativity means
this change is a true, exact negation, not an approximation) plus the
user's own direct, specific report of the symptom and the exact fix
needed. Confirmed a clean, isolated diff (git diff reviewed: only the
comment and the one operand-order line changed) and a normal fresh-tab
load afterward (510/510 hands, zero new console errors).

Cache-buster bump: index.html's own main.js?v=36 -> ?v=37.

2026-09-14  -  ~04:11 AM - 04:14 AM EDT (bounded by checkpoints; see Verified note)

Added "Palm Face Rotation (Deg)" slider (Cursor Tracking group, -180 to
180, default 0) -- direct follow-up to the 180-degree flip fix above:
"Provide me a slider to control that rotation actually." Rather than
trusting a fixed, code-baked calibration to be right (which just proved
wrong once already), the palm-facing correction is now a LIVE, per-frame
computation (`computePalmFaceCorrectionQuat()`) incorporating this
slider, so a future bad calibration (on this rig or a different model)
can be corrected from the panel instead of needing another code change.

Rotates the calibrated palm-normal direction around the hand's own
pointing axis (local -Z in the aligned frame, the same axis `pointDir`
itself sits on) before aiming it at the cursor -- perpendicular-ish to
the palm normal for a roughly-flat hand, so the slider sweeps the aimed
direction continuously from the palm (0 degrees) through edge-on (+-90)
to the exact opposite, the back of the hand (+-180), rather than an axis
that would leave the correction unchanged. Refactored the module-level
`palmFaceCorrectionQuat` (a fixed value computed once at model load) into
`palmNormalAligned` (the raw calibrated direction, still computed once)
plus a new `computePalmFaceCorrectionQuat()` function called once per
frame (not once per hand -- identical for every hand, like `alignQuat`
itself) inside the tracking block, only when Palm Faces Cursor is on.

Verified live: slider row renders with the correct key/range/default;
toggling the checkbox + slider through several values for ~100ms while
tracking ran produced no errors, 510/510 hands still updating normally.
Also ran a genuine INDEPENDENT cross-check (not the tautological kind
flagged in the entry above) confirming the roll-axis choice is
mathematically sound: built the OLD (pre-fix) formula's correction
quaternion completely separately, then built the NEW formula's output at
offset=180 degrees, and confirmed the two are the same rotation
(quaternion dot ~0.999966, i.e. ~1.0) -- proving the slider's 180-degree
end genuinely reproduces the exact old (wrong) behavior, and by
extension that 0 degrees genuinely gives the new (correct) one. Zero new
console errors in the final fresh-tab check.

Fixed Palm Face Rotation slider's roll axis -- direct follow-up: "The

made without an explicit spec at the time. The real spec is the WRIST

essentially perpendicular), which is what makes the slider sweep the

Each hand now rotates, around the SAME wrist-crop-plane axis the Palm
Face Rotation slider already used, by an angle computed fresh per hand
per frame from THAT hand's own position relative to the live cursor
target, in the field's own world XY plane: `computeRadialRollDeg(hand,
cursor) = atan2(dx, -dy)` where `dx = hand.x - cursor.x, dy = hand.y -
cursor.y`. This exact formula was derived directly from the user's own
5 stated reference points before writing any code, then verified to
reproduce every one of them precisely, plus the 3 implied diagonals
(45/-45/-135 degrees) the user didn't state explicitly but which follow
the same pattern. The Palm Face Rotation slider now adds directly onto

that this simpler 2D-rotation spec doesn't need at all.

Verified live in 2 layers, since the Browser pane's own hidden-pane
rAF-suspension (documented 2 rounds ago) makes a real live-render check
unreliable: (1) the bare formula against all 8 of the user's
above/below/left/right/diagonal reference points, all exact; (2) the
FULL render-loop composition, externally reconstructed and checked via
quaternion dot products -- a hand directly below the cursor produces a
rotation identical to the pre-existing default lookAt (dot=1.0,
confirming "facing up, as it is currently" is preserved exactly); a
hand to the right of the cursor differs from default by exactly the
mathematical signature of a 90-degree rotation (dot=cos(45)=0.7071);
adding a 30-degree slider offset on top differs from the base case by
exactly the signature of a 30-degree rotation (dot=cos(15)=0.9659).

**3. New "Responsive Wrist Splay" group** -- direct request: "Wrist will
Splay in response to distance from cursor. Provide similar settings as
the Wrist Crop/ arm Length group. call the new group 'Responsive Wrist
Splay'. By default, set minimum to 0 and maximum to -90. The closest
hands will be at -90 and furthest will be at 0." Mirrors Arm Length's
exact structure (master on/off, a Default value for when Reactive is
off, a Reactive-by-cursor-distance toggle, a Min/Max range widget, a
Distance->Value scaling curve widget) as its own separate top-level

Length is). Adds a per-hand EXTRA wrist-splay rotation on top of the
Pose group's own shared `wristSplay` slider (`applyWristPoseToSkeleton()`
gained an `extraSplayDeg` param, default 0 so every existing call site
is unaffected) -- reuses the exact same per-frame live cursor-distance
values (`live`/`minLiveDist`/`liveDistRange`) Arm Length already
computes in `updateRenderOrder()`, not a second distance pass, and
reuses `evaluateArmLengthCurve()` directly (already fully generic
despite the name -- distance in, curve value out, nothing Arm-Length-
specific about it).

The default range (min:0, max:-90) is deliberately NOT numerically
ordered -- "min"/"max" name which END of the distance curve each value
applies to (farthest/nearest, matching Arm Length's own min/max roles),
not a numeric min<=max constraint -- the underlying lerp works correctly
regardless of which endpoint is numerically larger. The new range
widget's own 2 handles do NOT cross-clamp each other the way Arm
Length's own range widget does (which assumes a numerically-ordered
0-100% scale) -- each handle moves independently across a -180 to 180
degree track, with a vertical tick marking 0 degrees since the track
(unlike Arm Length's all-positive one) spans negative and positive
values.

Verified live via a proper end-to-end check: initially measured the
nearest/farthest hands' `rHand` bone rotation via a raw Euler read and
got a constant -22.6-degree offset on BOTH ends (-112.6 near, -22.6 far,
instead of the expected -90/0) -- root-caused immediately (not assumed
correct) to the measurement technique itself: the rig's own bind pose
has a non-identity Z-component on this bone, so a raw Euler reading
conflates the rest pose with the applied rotation. Redid the
measurement correctly as a baseline-delta quaternion comparison
(capture the bone's quaternion with the feature OFF as the true
zero-reference, then compare against the feature-ON quaternion) and got
the exact expected result: nearest hand -90.0 degrees, farthest hand
exactly 0. Called `updateRenderOrder()` directly (exposed on
`window.__debug`) rather than relying on `requestAnimationFrame`, which
the Browser pane's own hidden-pane suspension (documented several
rounds ago) makes unreliable for this kind of live check. Zero new
console errors (one stale `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` from an earlier
failed navigation attempt, confirmed stale via unchanged 510/510 hand
count on the successful reload).

2026-09-14  -  06:15:07 AM - 06:31:19 AM EDT (measured, this entry's own start/finish)

Added "Click Hold-Pose" and "Right-Click Hold-Pose" -- 2 new, fully
independent top-level dev-panel groups (direct request, 2 bundled
features: "when i click and hold, the hands will transition to one of my
saved poses... on release, they transition back to default pose... I can
choose if they all transition together, or if their transition time is
based on distance from cursor," then "On 2nd thought, provide 2 of these.
The 2nd will be right click and hold," then clarified further: "the 2nd
new clickhold pose sets should be their own setting groups").

Each group: a Target Pose dropdown (reads the existing "Saved Poses"
list-picker), Pose Transition Speed (Ms), a Pose Transition Start Time
curve (distance-from-cursor -> start time, 0-1 normalized) with its own
Min/Max Start Time (Ms) range, and matching Retransition Speed/Curve/
Range controls for the release-side animation back to default pose.
Setting a trigger's Min and Max start time equal collapses the stagger to
"every hand transitions together" -- satisfies the request's own
either/or requirement without a separate toggle.

Architecture: a small per-hand-per-trigger state machine (`hand._chp[p]`,
phases idle -> forward -> retransition -> idle). On pointerdown, snapshots
every hand's current Pose-group values (`trig.forwardSnapshot`) and each
hand gets its own forward-phase start delay, computed ONCE from a live
cursor-distance snapshot at that instant (not continuously re-evaluated,
so a moving cursor mid-hold doesn't reshuffle stagger order). On
pointerup, each hand's own last-applied interpolated pose becomes its own
retransition start (correctly handles releasing mid-transition, not just
after reaching the target). Reuses `applyCurlToSkeleton()`/
`applyWristPoseToSkeleton()`'s existing `values` override param (already
proven by Pose Preview) and `evaluateArmLengthCurve()` directly -- no new
posing or curve-evaluation code. mousedown/mouseup listeners are added as
parallel, non-blocking `window` listeners (never call preventDefault/
stopPropagation), matching the Mouse Tracking Log's own established
pattern -- OrbitControls' existing left/right-click-drag camera pan is
completely unaffected and both behaviors work simultaneously by design.

Scope decision (disclosed, not silent): per-hand pose interpolation
excludes Whole-Hand Rotation (modelRotX/Y/Z) -- those 3 keys drive a
single SHARED `cloneBaseQuat` (computed once from `cfg`) used by every
hand's own Arm Length position-compensation math, so making them
per-hand-interpolatable would need a materially larger change (a
per-hand cloneBaseQuat plus touching that compensation math) than this
feature asked for. Checked that every saved pose in the user's own
previously-pasted Copy Settings dump has modelRotX/Y/Z at exactly 0,
making this a low-impact exclusion in practice.

New generic (parameterized) dev-panel widgets: `buildGenericRangeBarWidget()`
and `buildGenericCurveWidget()`, added alongside (not replacing) the
existing project-specific Arm Length/Wrist Splay widget builders -- this
round needed 8 more widget instances (4 controls x 2 triggers), enough
that hand-copying a 3rd/4th/5th/6th time was judged worse than
parameterizing, unlike the single-instance Wrist Splay round 2 entries
above, which deliberately kept its own dedicated (duplicated) widgets.
The curve widget carries right-click-to-delete-point from the start (the
2nd, separate part of this session's own opening request, applied to the
original Arm Length curve widget too -- see that entry's own commit).

2 real bugs caught via live testing (a `node --check` pass alone can't
catch a temporal-dead-zone violation -- both were only visible once the
page was actually loaded in the browser):
- The very first module-load-time setup call (`CLICK_HOLD_KEYS.forEach(...)`,
  positioned near the other widgets' own setup calls early in the file)
  referenced `CLICK_HOLD_KEYS`, a `const` declared much later in the file
  alongside the rest of the state machine -- a ReferenceError, since a
  top-level `const` is not hoisted the way a `function` declaration is.
  Fixed by moving the setup call itself down to right after
  `clickHoldPoseTriggers`'s own declaration (where `buildClickHoldPoseWidgets`
  is defined), rather than trying to hoist the const earlier.
- The same call also invoked `parseClickHoldConfig()`, which reads
  `clickHoldPoseTriggers` -- same TDZ problem, same fix (moving the call,
  not the declaration).

Live-verified via `window.__debug.updateRenderOrder()` (direct calls,
bypassing the Browser pane's own rAF-suspends-while-hidden limitation)
plus real `PointerEvent` dispatch on `window` (exercising the actual
mousedown/mouseup listeners, not a hand-reconstructed simulation):
- Correct idle -> forward -> retransition -> idle phase transitions per
  hand, confirmed via `hand._chp.chp.phase` at each stage.
- Real pose interpolation, not just a state-flag flip: injected 2
  distinct test poses (curlIndex 0 vs. 90) via `cfg.savedPoses`, measured
  the `rIndex1` bone's quaternion after each full transition, and found
  an 81-degree delta between them -- confirms the target value genuinely
  drives the bone, not a fixed animation.
- Distinct per-hand start delays: sampled `forwardDelay` across 6 hands
  mid-hold (299/289/276/262/246/229 ms) -- confirms the distance-based
  stagger computes a real per-hand value, not one shared number.
- The "Saved Poses" list-picker's own Save/Delete/Rename/Overwrite
  actions didn't originally propagate to the 2 new Target Pose dropdowns
  (a `select`-type control's `<option>` list is only rebuilt on an
  explicit `refreshSelectOptions()` call, confirmed by reading
  `devPanel.js`'s own doc comment for that exported function) -- fixed by
  wiring `savedPoses`'s own `onChange` to call
  `refreshSelectOptions('chpTargetPose')` / `refreshSelectOptions('rchpTargetPose')`.
- Zero new console errors after all of the above (3 stale
  `CLICK_HOLD_KEYS`/`clickHoldPoseTriggers`/`ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`
  entries lingered from the 2 TDZ bugs above and an earlier failed
  navigation, confirmed stale by the unchanged 510/510 hand count and a
  fully clean reload afterward).

2026-09-14  -  07:10:07 AM - 07:25:16 AM EDT (measured, this entry's own start/finish)

2 direct user follow-ups on Click Hold-Pose (previous entry): "I dont see
any of the saved poses in the dropdown for Click Hold Pose Target pose
selection. fix that" and "also in both of the new groups, provide a
checkbox to turn that feature on and off."

**Target Pose dropdown fix.** Root cause: a devPanel.js `select` control's
`<option>` list is only ever populated by an explicit `refreshSelectOptions()`
call. The 2 Target Pose dropdowns (`chpTargetPose`/`rchpTargetPose`) were
only wired to refresh reactively, on a FUTURE Save/Delete/Rename/Overwrite
of a saved pose (via `savedPoses`'s own `onChange`) -- never for poses that
already existed in `cfg.savedPoses` at page load. Fixed with a one-time
refresh call placed right after the Click-Hold-Pose state machine's own
`const` declarations (`clickHoldPoseTriggers` etc.) genuinely exist.

**Master on/off checkboxes.** Added `chpEnabled`/`rchpEnabled` (label:
"<Group Title> (Master On/Off)", default off) as the first control in each
group, gating `startClickHoldPose(p)` with a single `if (!cfg[`${p}Enabled`])

**A real, only-partially-root-caused bug found during this round's own
live testing:** calling devPanel.js's `refreshSelectOptions('rchpTargetPose')`
throws inside its own `commit()`/`fillSelectOptions()` -- isolated via
repeated single-key testing in a fresh browser tab (`refreshSelectOptions('chpTargetPose')`
alone always succeeds; `refreshSelectOptions('rchpTargetPose')` alone
always throws, both calls otherwise structurally identical, same control
shape, same code path) but NOT fully root-caused to a specific line inside
devPanel.js despite extensive isolation (disabling the call entirely
fixes the load; the two controls' own definitions and registration are
byte-for-byte parallel). Given the user's own explicit instruction mid-
round ("dont verify, just fix and push") to stop the live-debugging loop,
resolved pragmatically rather than exhaustively: added a
`safeRefreshSelectOptions(key)` wrapper (try/catch + `console.error`) and
routed every `refreshSelectOptions()` call for these 2 keys through it, so
this mystery can never again take down the whole page load regardless of
its true cause. Flagged in PROJECT_PROGRESS.md's "What's next" for a
proper root-cause pass later, since a caught-and-logged crash is a
mitigation, not a fix, and the underlying devPanel.js issue could affect
some other, not-yet-discovered `select` control the same way.

Also found and fixed, during the same live-debugging session, that a test
tab can silently degrade after many repeated reloads under real crash
conditions (WebGL context accumulating `GL_INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION`
errors until Chrome stops reporting them entirely, "too many errors, no
more errors will be reported to the console for this context") -- a
console.log-based debug marker placed in the crashing tab never surfaced,
even though the exact same code verifiably ran fine (confirmed via the
same marker) in a freshly-opened tab. Not a code bug, but a new addition
to this project's own environment-quirks list: a tab that has crashed
and reloaded many times in one debugging session should not be trusted
for a "did my fix work" check -- open a fresh tab instead once a session
has accumulated many failed reloads.

Pose values right after `initDevPanel()` runs (so it already matches

2026-09-14  -  07:59:12 AM - 08:07:37 AM EDT (measured, this entry's own start/finish)

Direct user report: "Works overall. For poses, the thumb pose doesnt
seem correct. I believe it has to do with the rotation that we had
used. Double check in reference to hando to see what the issue is."

**Root cause, found by direct comparison against HANDO (this project's
own reference implementation, sharing the identical rig asset):** this
project's own `applyAllFingerPoses()`, `previewPosePreset()`, and
`applyPoseValuesToHand()` all posed FINGERS before the WRIST in a single
combined call. `rThumb1`'s own PARENT bone is `rHand` -- the exact bone
`applyWristPoseToSkeleton()` rotates. `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` (used by
every finger's own Curl/Splay/Splay2) converts its world axis into the
bone's CURRENT local space via the bone's FULL world quaternion, which
depends on the ENTIRE parent chain's CURRENT `matrixWorld` -- so posing
the thumb before the wrist reaches its own new target rotation reads
whatever wrist rotation was left over from BEFORE that call, not the one
about to be set. The other 4 fingers are unaffected (not parented
directly to `rHand`), which is exactly why only the thumb looked wrong.

**This is the identical bug HANDO already found, diagnosed, and fixed**
(its own CHANGELOG.txt documents the same root cause, found via a direct
user report there too: "From Scissor 1.5 to Scissor 1 - Wrong (thumb
curled/splayed too much). On a second click, its even worse") -- it was
simply never ported here when this project's own `applyCurlToSkeleton`/
`applyWristPoseToSkeleton` were adapted from HANDO's code. Fixed at all
3 combined-call sites by swapping the order (wrist first, fingers
second): `applyAllFingerPoses()` (rebuild, Whole-Hand Rotation changes),
`previewPosePreset()` (Pose Preview / "Use" on a saved pose), and
`applyPoseValuesToHand()` (Click-Hold-Pose's own per-hand application).

**Live-verified with a real, quantitative before/after measurement** (not
just a visual check): with `wristBend=45`/`wristSplay=-20`/`thumbCurl=90`
applied to a freshly-rebuilt (bind-pose) skeleton -- the exact scenario
that triggers the bug, since a fresh skeleton's `rHand` starts untouched
-- captured `rThumb1`'s quaternion right after the rebuild, then
independently re-applied wrist bend/splay and thumb curl via their own
SEPARATE, guaranteed-correct individual slider calls (each one only ever
touches its own bone, no ordering ambiguity possible) to get a reference
quaternion. With the OLD (fingers-then-wrist) order, the rebuild's own
result differed from the reference by 33.0 degrees -- a real, large,
measurable error. Restored to the OLD order specifically to CONFIRM this
number (ruling out "the test just isn't sensitive enough to catch a
real bug" as an explanation for why it might read 0), then re-applied
the fix and re-ran the identical test: exactly 0.0 degrees of error.
510/510 hands still rendering (varied intentionally to 180 via the
Field Layout rebuild trigger used for this test, not a regression), zero
new console errors.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=59` -> `?v=62` (2
intermediate bumps during the before/after live-comparison test above;
`?v=62` is the final, verified, pushed value).

2026-09-14  -  08:19:47 AM - 08:32:10 AM EDT (measured, this entry's own start/finish -- includes an interleaved re-investigation of a separate user bug report, logged as its own entry above)

**Re-investigated the retransition-thumb bug report once more first** (a
NEW, more specific report arrived mid-round: "the thumb error is still
an issue... Startup pose is correct while the retransitioned pose is
incorrect even though they should be exactly the same"). Added
`poseDefaultValues`/`setSelectedPoseAsDefault`/`getSelectedSavedPoseItem`
to `window.__debug` (none were exposed before) and ran the most
realistic test possible: injected a fake selected list-picker row, called
the REAL `setSelectedPoseAsDefault()` button logic, persisted it through
a genuine page reload, then ran a real Click-Hold-Pose trigger/release
cycle varying all 5 thumb-specific keys plus wrist bend/splay together --
0.0 degree delta between true startup and post-retransition pose, same
as every earlier test. User confirmed a hard refresh didn't change the
symptom, ruling out stale cache; per the user's own choice, this was set
aside (not resolved) to proceed with the new feature below -- still an
open item, see PROJECT_PROGRESS.md's "What's next".

**Added "Click Pose" / "Double-Click Pose"** -- 2 new dev-panel groups,
a fire-and-forget variant of Click Hold-Pose (direct follow-up request).
Unlike Click Hold-Pose (hold to transition, release to retransition), a
single click or double-click starts every hand's own FULL sequence at
once: transition to target, PAUSE at the target for a configurable
duration (new "Pause Duration At Target (Ms)" slider -- a single flat
value, not a curve+range pair, per the request's own "*D* pause
duration*"), then retransition back to default -- the whole sequence
then runs to completion per-hand regardless of what the mouse does
afterward. Direct requirement, implemented literally: "Retransition
start time of a single [hand] does not consider whether every hand has
completed their transition. It is independent to that hand specifically"
-- each hand's own pause-end and retransition-stagger are computed from
THAT hand's own live cursor distance at the moment IT specifically
finishes its own pause, never from a shared/global snapshot. Both
triggers sit on the LEFT mouse button, distinguished from each other by
click COUNT (not left/right button the way Click Hold-Pose's own 2
groups are) -- reuses the Mouse Tracking Log's own existing
`MOUSE_LOG_MULTICLICK_MS` (350ms) debounce window for click-vs-double-
click disambiguation, rather than inventing a new threshold. Reuses
every existing piece of Click-Hold-Pose's own machinery directly
(`computeStartDelayMs`, `lerpPoseValues`, `applyPoseValuesToHand`,
`poseDefaultValues`) -- the only genuinely new code is the 3-phase
(forward -> paused -> retransition, one more phase than Click Hold-
Pose's own forward/retransition pair) per-hand state machine and the
click-count trigger detection.

**Also disabled OrbitControls' own Pan while a Click-Hold-Pose trigger
is active** (direct request: "moving the cursor should not trigger any
panning [during a hold]... it should continue holding the held state").
This project's OrbitControls config maps BOTH left and right mouse
buttons to Pan -- the exact same buttons Click Hold-Pose's own
pointerdown/pointerup listeners use, so a hold-and-drag was panning the
camera underneath the pose transition. `controls.enablePan` now toggles
false on `startClickHoldPose()`, restored true on `endClickHoldPose()`
only once NEITHER trigger (chp/rchp) is still active. Confirmed, by
reading `animate()`'s own code (not assumed), that cursor-tracking
rotation (`hand.wrapper.quaternion`) is a fully separate per-frame step
that never went through OrbitControls to begin with -- it already keeps
following the cursor throughout ANY pose-transition state, including
mid-transition, matching the request's own follow-up clarification
("even a hand in mid transition should continue to rotate to follow the
cursor") with zero code change needed for that specific part.

Live-verified: the full forward->paused->retransition->idle phase
sequence, with the target pose's own real values genuinely applied at
each stage (not just a flag flip); click vs. double-click correctly
disambiguated (2 rapid clicks enter only the dblclick phase, click stays
idle); Pan correctly disabled during a hold and restored on release. All
4 new curve/range widgets and the Pause Duration slider confirmed
constructed (SVG/slider elements present in each row). Zero new console
errors, 510/510 hands still rendering.

Cache-buster bumps: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=62` -> `?v=64` (`?v=63`
was the debug-exposure-only intermediate step for the re-investigation
above).

2026-09-14  -  08:37:53 AM - 08:50:24 AM EDT (measured, this entry's own start/finish)

**Finally root-caused the "thumb pose still looks wrong" saga** -- the
user's own follow-up finally pinned it down: "thumb pose of all poses
except startup still looks wrong," and, once asked, "i am using imported
poses from Hando so i cant [re-save a fresh pose to test]." That single
fact reframed the whole investigation: every prior test this session
used SYNTHETIC test poses with modelRotX/Y/Z left at 0, which never
exercised the actual gap.

**Root cause:** Click-Hold-Pose and Click-Pose's own `lerpPoseValues()`
deliberately EXCLUDED modelRotX/Y/Z from interpolation -- a disclosed
scope decision from when Click-Hold-Pose was first built, since those 3
keys drive a single SHARED `cloneBaseQuat` used by every hand's own Arm
Length position-compensation math, and making them per-hand was flagged
at the time as "a materially larger change than requested." A pose
imported from HANDO commonly bakes nonzero Whole-Hand Rotation into the
gesture itself (unlike the user's own native poses, previously checked
and found to universally have modelRotX/Y/Z at 0 -- that check simply
never covered imported poses). During a transition to such a pose, the
finger/wrist articulation correctly animated to the target, but the
finger CURL AXES stayed pre-rotated by the OLD, unchanged SHARED basis
the whole time -- anatomically wrong the instant a pose implies a
different whole-hand orientation than the field's current shared
sliders say. "Startup" (nothing ever transitioning) never showed the
gap since there was nothing for it to diverge from -- confirmed via
direct test: mid-transition, `cfg.modelRotY` measured unchanged (still
0) throughout, while the target pose's own modelRotY was 60.

**User explicitly chose the full fix over 2 smaller alternatives**
(asked directly, since properly fixing this means revisiting the
original disclosed architectural tradeoff): "Make it interpolate
per-hand like everything else," over "snap it instantly, no smooth
transition" or "leave as-is, re-tune poses instead."

**Implementation:** each hand now gets its own `currentBaseQuat` (set at
creation in `rebuildField()`), kept identical to the single shared
`cloneBaseQuat` every frame for any hand NOT currently mid a pose-
transition -- zero behavior change for ordinary Whole-Hand Rotation
usage, verified directly (0.0 degrees of error against an independently
computed reference). `lerpPoseValues()` now genuinely interpolates
modelRotX/Y/Z like every other key (the exclusion is gone); a new
`computeBaseQuatFromValues()` helper (same formula as
`updateCloneBaseQuat()`, just reading a passed-in values object instead
of live `cfg`) computes a per-hand basis from a transition's own
currently-interpolated modelRot, written into `hand.currentBaseQuat`
inside `applyPoseValuesToHand()`. `applyHandArmLength()` now reads the
per-hand basis instead of the shared one directly, accepting a
disclosed ~1-frame lag on the ARM-LENGTH POSITION specifically during an
active transition (it runs before this frame's own pose-override step
updates `currentBaseQuat`) -- imperceptible at 60fps, and avoids
restructuring the per-hand render-order loop's own established bone-
posing sequence just to eliminate a sub-16ms lag on a position, never
the fingers (which read the fresh value the same frame they're posed).

**Live-verified across the full lifecycle:** a target pose with
modelRotY=60 rotates the hand's own ACTUAL rendered orientation
(`hand.clone.quaternion`) by exactly 60.0 degrees during the transition
(previously 0), while the shared field-wide `cfg.modelRotY` stays
untouched the entire time; a hand never touched by any trigger stays
exactly synced to the shared basis (0.0 degrees delta); after a full
retransition cycle completes, a hand's own basis correctly reverts to
match the shared/default orientation again (0.0 degrees); ordinary,
non-transition slider-driven Whole-Hand Rotation was independently
re-verified afterward and measures exactly 0.0 degrees of error against
a freshly, independently computed reference quaternion -- confirming no
regression to the feature this change had to touch. Zero new console
errors, 510/510 hands still rendering throughout.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=64` -> `?v=65`.

2026-09-14  -  09:39:00 AM - 09:53:36 AM EDT (measured, this entry's own start/finish)

Direct user suggestion broke the "thumb still wrong" case wide open:
"maybe check hando for pose settings. maybe there is an extra setting
you dont have." Direct comparison against HANDO's own Pose group found
exactly that: HANDO has a per-finger "Base-Only Curl" slider (5, one per
finger) that this project's own Pose-group port had deliberately omitted
-- disclosed at the time ("don't exist as controls in this project
yet") but never circled back to. A pose imported from HANDO using Base-
Only Curl on the thumb had that data silently dropped -- this project
had no control or key to store or apply it at all.

**Ported Base-Only Curl from HANDO exactly.** Added `baseOnlyCurl{Finger}`
sliders (5, matching HANDO's own `min:-200,max:200,def:0`) in the same
position HANDO uses (right after Curl Bias, before Tip Twist); a new
`FINGER_BASE_ONLY_CURL_KEY` map; the same additive, base-joint-only
rotation in `applyCurlToSkeleton()` HANDO itself uses (a 2nd
`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis` call on joint 0 only, same curl axis, composing
on top of whatever regular Curl+Bias already did); added all 5 keys to
`POSE_PRESET_KEYS` (so saved/imported poses capture and restore them)
and to their own per-finger Pose subgroups. `values[key] || 0` guards
against `NaN` for any older saved pose predating this key.

**Separately, a genuine, correctly-diagnosed interference bug:** direct
report, "for click hold, when i release, it seems to trigger the correct
release, but then it calls it again. I suspect it is click hold
interferring with click triggers." Releasing a Click-Hold-Pose hold
fires the same native `pointerup` Click Pose/Double-Click Pose also
listens for on the left button; that listener had no way to tell a
hold-release apart from a genuine standalone click, so every release
also fired Click Pose's own sequence on top. Fixed with a
`lastPointerupWasHoldRelease` flag set by the (earlier-registered)
Click-Hold-Pose pointerup listener and read by the Click Pose listener.

**That first fix introduced a real regression, caught immediately by
the user:** "i tested the new build and now i cant trigger click nor
double click. i thinkn the click hold trigger probably overrides it" --
correct again. Click-Hold-Pose has no minimum press duration of its own
(`trig.active` goes true the instant the button goes down), so an
instant click -- with both features enabled together -- ALSO left
`.active` true right up until release, making the original fix suppress
EVERY click, not just genuine holds. Corrected by requiring the hold to
have actually lasted a perceptible duration (reusing
`MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS`, the Mouse Tracking Log's own existing "quick
tap vs. real hold" threshold) before treating a release as suppressible.

Live-verified: Base-Only Curl produces a clean, isolated base-joint
rotation (45deg on rThumb1, exactly 0deg on rThumb2, with every other
thumb slider genuinely zeroed); a Click-Hold-Pose transition to a pose
with `baseOnlyCurlThumb` set now measures 0.0 degrees of error against
the "Default" button applying the identical pose; a quick (~50ms) click
correctly enters Click Pose's own 'forward' phase; a genuine sustained
(~700ms) hold still correctly suppresses the extra click. Also
separately investigated a user report of cursor-tracking sticking near
the browser edge -- direct measurement found `cursorTarget` continues
moving smoothly and continuously all the way to the true last-pixel
edge (NDC reaching 0.9984), no sign of a code-level threshold; flagged
to the user as likely a physical mouse/monitor-edge limit or a dev-
panel-position interaction, pending more specifics.

**Despite all of the above, the user reports the thumb is STILL wrong**
for Click-Hold/Click-Pose transition targets and for what a retransition
lands on, even though the identical pose applied via "Default" looks
correct. This is now the 4th round of exhaustive, verified-correct
testing (wrist-order fix, per-hand Whole-Hand Rotation, Base-Only Curl,
each independently measuring exactly 0.0 degrees of error against a
"Default"-equivalent reference) without reproducing what the user is
actually seeing. Genuinely blocked without the user's own actual saved-
pose JSON (Copy Settings) and a screenshot -- see PROJECT_PROGRESS.md's
"What's next" for the explicit ask.

Cache-buster bumps: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=65` -> `?v=68` (2

2026-09-14  -  10:03 AM - 10:54 AM EDT (approximate, spanning several interleaved user exchanges; not independently re-measured per sub-task)

**THE "THUMB POSE LOOKS WRONG" SAGA IS FINALLY, GENUINELY RESOLVED --
root cause found, and it was never a bug in any of the code this
session touched.** Root cause: **Responsive Wrist Splay was enabled**
in the user's own session. That feature adds a live, continuously-
recalculated wrist rotation based on cursor distance, independent of
whatever pose is active -- and since it recomputes every frame during
an active Click-Hold-Pose/Click-Pose transition (while "Default"'s own
one-time application doesn't include it at the instant it's clicked),
any cursor movement between "when Default was set" and "when a
transition runs" changes the live extra splay, producing a real,
visible thumb shift completely independent of which pose was
targeted. Confirmed decisively by the user's own 2 tests: (1) setting
the Click Pose target to the SAME pose as Default still produced an
immediate thumb shift on click -- impossible if the bug were in pose
DATA, since interpolating between 2 identical states is a mathematical
no-op; (2) "every other finger is in the correct pose, its just the
thumb" -- the thumb is the ONLY finger parented to the wrist bone
(`rHand`), so it's the ONLY finger any extra wrist rotation can ever
visibly affect. Disabling Responsive Wrist Splay confirmed the fix
("yeah thats it. good catch.").

**This means every one of the real, verified fixes shipped across this
entire investigation (wrist-before-fingers ordering; per-hand Whole-
Hand Rotation during transitions; HANDO's own Base-Only Curl, ported
after being found missing entirely; 2 rounds of Click-Hold/Click-Pose
interference) were genuine, real bugs, independently confirmed correct,
and NONE of them were the actual cause of what the user kept
reporting.** All 4 remain correctly fixed and still matter for other
scenarios (imported poses that DO use Whole-Hand Rotation or Base-Only
Curl, for instance) -- this entry does not retract any of them, it
closes out the ONE thread that kept resurfacing across all of them.

Also shipped in this same stretch, before the root cause was found:

**Decoupled cursor target X/Y from Cursor Target Depth** (direct
request, after a live-quantified explanation of the old parallax
behavior with real numbers: a corner hand needing to face rightward
despite the cursor being unambiguously further left on screen). X/Y now
always come from a raycast to a plane permanently fixed at z=0 (the
hands' own depth -- "the true cursor xy"); Z is set directly from
`targetDepthFactor * fieldRadius` as a plain scalar, fully decoupled.
`targetPlane` no longer needs `updateTargetPlane()` to track the
configured depth (removed entirely, 2 call sites cleaned up) since both
axes are now computed fresh, independently, every frame. Live-verified:
X/Y at a fixed screen position now exactly matches a raycast to z=0
(previously scaled down by the depth-shift); Z still correctly reflects
targetDepthFactor.

**New, known, ACCEPTED side effect of that same fix (fix deferred,
explicitly grouped with a future round per direct request, "dont do
that right now. group it with the next changes i ask for"):** the
Target Marker (the visual dot showing `cursorTarget`) no longer stays
pixel-aligned with the true mouse position when Target Depth isn't 0 --
because the marker's own position IS `cursorTarget`, and that point is
no longer guaranteed to lie ON the original camera ray now that Z is
set independently of X/Y (previously it always did, by construction,
since every axis came from one single raycast hit). Planned fix
(NOT YET IMPLEMENTED): give the Target Marker its own position, driven
by the TRUE raycast-to-actual-depth hit (always pixel-perfect under the
cursor), fully independent from `cursorTarget` (which keeps driving the
hands with the new decoupled X/Y-depth behavior).

**Click Pose's own 350ms debounce delay removed when Double-Click Pose
is disabled** (direct report: "why is there a delay between the click
and the triggered pose transition, even if the minimum transition time
is set to 0"). Every click used to wait the full multi-click debounce
window before triggering, even with no double-click feature enabled to
disambiguate against -- pure, avoidable latency in that case. Now fires
immediately when `cfg.dblclickEnabled` is false. (Separately explained,
no code change: Click Hold-Pose's own delay source, when present, is
"Pose Transition Min/Max Start Time" -- defaulting to up to 300ms --
which is independent of "Transition Speed" and easy to conflate with
it.)

**Added a "Diagnose" button** (Saved Poses list-picker, next to
"Default") -- a TEMPORARY debugging aid built specifically to get real
data from the user's own actual saved poses rather than this session's
own repeatedly-inconclusive synthetic approximations. Runs the "Default
path" vs. "transition path" comparison directly on whichever pose is
currently selected, reporting each finger's base-joint mismatch via
`alert()`. Not actually needed in the end (the root cause was found
through the user's own 2 decisive manual tests before the button was
ever used) -- left in place for now since it may still be useful for a
future investigation; consider removing it once confirmed unneeded long-
term.

Cache-buster bumps: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=68` -> `?v=71` across
this stretch.

2026-09-14  -  11:2x AM EDT (start not captured -- Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**Responsive Wrist Splay now freezes for the duration of an explicit pose
transition, instead of fighting with it.** Direct follow-up question after
the thumb saga's resolution above: "is there a way around it such that i
can have both functioning correctly." Investigated the actual mechanism
(not just the symptom) before proposing anything: `applyWristPoseToSkeleton()`
already composes the pose's own `wristSplay` and Responsive Wrist Splay's
`extraSplayDeg` into ONE `rotateZ()` call, fully resolved BEFORE any finger
(including the thumb) is posed relative to the wrist -- so "pose the thumb
first, then apply splay" was already mathematically true; reordering it
further couldn't have changed anything. The real cause: `extraSplayDeg`
was being recomputed **live, every single frame**, for the entire duration
of a Click-Hold-Pose or Click-Pose/Double-Click-Pose transition -- so any
cursor movement during the hold/transition kept shifting the wrist target
out from under the pose's own interpolation, visibly as a moving thumb
(the one finger parented to the wrist bone).

Fix: capture `extraSplayDeg` ONCE, at the exact instant a transition
triggers (`chp.frozenSplayDeg` for Click Hold-Pose, set alongside its
existing `forwardDelay` capture in `updateClickHoldPoseForHand()`;
`cp.frozenSplayDeg` for Click/Double-Click Pose, set alongside its existing
per-hand `forwardDelay` in `triggerClickPose()`), then reuse that one
frozen value through every phase of that hand's own sequence (forward,
pause, retransition) instead of calling `computeResponsiveWristSplayDeg()`
again each frame. The per-frame idle-loop path (`updateRenderOrder()`'s own
un-overridden branch) is untouched -- an idle hand keeps tracking
Responsive Wrist Splay fully live, exactly as before. Live-verified in
browser: with Responsive Wrist Splay enabled and a Click Pose target equal
to the current pose (the user's own diagnostic technique from the thumb
saga above), `frozenSplayDeg` was confirmed nonzero and IDENTICAL before
and after moving the cursor far away mid-'forward'-phase, while a
different, untriggered ("idle") hand's own wrist bone quaternion was
confirmed to still change in response to the same cursor move -- both
behaviors now coexist as intended.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=71` -> `?v=72`.

2026-09-14  -  12:0x PM EDT (start not captured -- Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**THE ACTUAL, FINAL ROOT CAUSE of the "thumb pose looks wrong" saga --
found and fixed, independent of cursor movement.** Direct follow-up
report after the previous entry's freeze fix: "doesnt work. thumb still
messed up" -- then, when asked whether it happens with the cursor held
still: "yeah im not talking about moving during the transition. im just
talk about regular static click, duble click or clickhold." This ruled
out the previous entry's own diagnosis (Responsive Wrist Splay
recomputing live DURING a transition) as the explanation, since with a
genuinely static cursor that recompute produces the identical value every
time regardless of freezing -- the freeze fix was real and still correct
for cursor-movement cases, but wasn't the (whole) story.

Found by reading `updateRenderOrder()`'s own idle branch (the per-frame
path for a hand NOT in any active pose transition) line by line: it
re-applies the wrist bone's live Responsive Wrist Splay rotation EVERY
SINGLE FRAME, but never re-applies finger curl -- curl is normally only
recomputed on a slider change or "Default" click
(`applyAllFingerPoses()`), not every frame. `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`
converts a curl's intended WORLD-space axis into a LOCAL bone rotation
using `bone.getWorldQuaternion()` -- the bone's CURRENT parent-chain
world orientation at the exact instant curl is computed (same mechanism
as the original wrist-before-fingers fix, many rounds ago). `rThumb1` is
the only finger bone parented directly to the wrist bone (`rHand`), so
it's the only one whose local rotation goes stale the moment the wrist
keeps rotating afterward -- exactly matching "every other finger is
correct, it's just the thumb," and exactly independent of cursor
movement (a nonzero live Responsive Wrist Splay value sits there
whether the cursor is moving or not). "Default" looks correct for
exactly the one frame it's applied (extraSplayDeg=0 in
`applyAllFingerPoses()`'s own call), then the wrist keeps moving via
live splay every frame after that while the thumb doesn't follow -- and
a hand returning to idle after any Click-Hold-Pose/Click-Pose sequence
shows the identical desync immediately, for the same reason.

Fix: the idle branch now also re-applies the THUMB's curl (only the
thumb -- the other 4 fingers aren't parented to `rHand` and are provably
unaffected, confirmed by the original wrist-ordering fix, so refreshing
all 5 every frame for every hand isn't needed and would cost real
performance at hundreds of hands for no benefit) immediately after the
wrist update, mirroring the same "wrist then fingers, together, every
frame" pattern `applyPoseValuesToHand()` already uses during an active
transition.

**Verified with a genuinely independent double-check, static cursor
throughout (no pointer events at all), reproducing the user's own exact
repro conditions:** built 2 separate reference computations for the
thumb's WORLD-space orientation at a hand with a large (90-degree) live
Responsive Wrist Splay swing -- (A) the real route: apply "Default", then
run 5 real idle frames via `updateRenderOrder()`; (B) an independent
reference: set the wrist directly to its final live value in one shot,
then apply thumb curl fresh against THAT, via the same exposed functions
called directly, not through the frame loop. Result: **0.0 degrees**
of world-space difference between A and B. Confirmed the test was
actually discriminating by reproducing the OLD (pre-fix) behavior the
same way (wrist-only per-"frame" update, thumb curl never refreshed) and
comparing against the same reference B: **12.5 degrees of real,
measurable error** -- a genuine, visually-significant bug, not a
rounding artifact, now fixed.

Also exposed 3 more functions on `window.__debug`
(`applyAllFingerPoses`, `applyPoseValuesToHand`, `cloneBaseQuat` getter,
`triggerClickPose`, `startClickHoldPose`, `endClickHoldPose`,
`updateClickPoseForHand`, `updateClickHoldPoseForHand`, `getOrInitHandCP`,
`getOrInitHandCHP`, `computeResponsiveWristSplayDeg`,
`applyWristPoseToSkeleton`, `applyCurlToSkeleton`, `FINGER_NAMES`,
`FINGER_JOINTS`) to make this and future investigations directly
testable without depending on real pointer-event timing or the Browser
pane's own rAF-suspends-while-hidden limitation.

**Environment note, not a code issue:** this round's own live-browser
verification was repeatedly blocked by the local static file server
(`python3 -m http.server`) intermittently truncating/resetting the
connection for `main.js` and `devpanel.js` specifically (confirmed via
direct `curl` retries outside the browser tool too -- same file,
same intermittent truncation, unrelated to any code change) -- worked
around by starting a completely fresh server instance on an alternate
port (`static-alt2`, 8422) rather than continuing to retry the
already-hammered one.

Cache-buster bumps: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=72` -> `?v=75` across
this round (2 intermediate debug-exposure bumps).

2026-09-14  -  (start not captured -- Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**The thumb-only idle-refresh fix from earlier this same day was real

their own "other fingers" observation as possibly unreliable perception
-- but a direct, independent skeleton inspection (not the user's
perception) settled it: every finger's base joint is parented to its own
"carpal" bone (rCarpal1 for index, rCarpal2 for middle, rCarpal3 for
ring, rCarpal4 for pinky), and every one of those carpal bones is itself
a child of the wrist bone (rHand). The earlier round's belief that "only
the thumb is parented to the wrist bone" (stated by an even earlier
round of this saga and never re-verified) was wrong -- true only in the
narrow sense that the thumb attaches DIRECTLY while the other 4 go
through one intermediate carpal bone each, but every finger is still a
structural descendant of the wrist and therefore depends on the wrist's
current world orientation the same way `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` was
already established to require.

Fix: the idle branch's per-frame finger-curl refresh (added earlier
today for the thumb only) now loops over all of `FINGER_NAMES`.

**Verified live with the user's own real settings already loaded as the
project's defaults** (all 4 triggers enabled simultaneously, matching
their real config, not a simplified single-feature test): 0.0000 degrees
of drift for all 5 fingers across a 60-frame idle "just sitting there"
wait (confirms the idle-loop desync itself is now fully closed), and
0.0000 degrees of round-trip error for all 5 fingers across 11 hands
spread through the field after a real, full click-to-Open-Palm-and-back
sequence.

Performance note, not yet addressed: this refreshes 5 fingers' curl
(instead of 1) per idle hand per frame -- a real cost increase at
hundreds of hands, accepted for correctness; worth profiling later if
frame rate becomes a concern, not attempted this round.

2026-09-14  -  (start not captured -- Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**THE ACTUAL, FINAL, REAL root cause of the entire thumb/finger-pose
saga, found via a decisive cross-project clue.** The user tested the
all-5-fingers idle-refresh fix on desktop and reported it was actually
WORSE than before -- "in Fist pose, every finger is less curled than it
was before when it looked like just the thumb" -- then, critically:
"i actually checked the same issue on Hando and it occurs there too. so
its not just a problem with our project. Its not resolved in hando
either." Hando is a completely separate codebase this session has never
touched -- that single fact ruled out every fix attempted across this
entire saga (wrist-ordering; per-hand Whole-Hand Rotation; Base-Only
Curl; Click-Hold/Click-Pose interference; the idle-loop refreshes) as
the actual cause, since none of that code exists in Hando. It pointed
directly at something the 2 projects structurally SHARE.

Found it in `applyCurlToSkeleton()`: the curl/splay axis conversion uses
`baseQuat` (the whole-hand's PRE-wrist orientation -- alignQuat + Whole-
Hand Rotation only) as its reference frame -- ported VERBATIM from
HANDO's own convention, with the code's own existing comment explicitly
noting this was deliberate, to match "HANDO's own anatomical intent
exactly." That shared convention itself is anatomically wrong: `baseQuat`
never includes the wrist bone's own current bend/splay rotation, yet
every finger's base joint is a descendant of the wrist bone (`rHand`,
via its own "carpal" bone -- see the same day's earlier correction).
Anatomically, curl direction should rotate WITH the wrist/palm -- closing
a fist still closes toward YOUR OWN palm no matter how your wrist is
bent. Using a wrist-independent axis means the same curl % increasingly
"misses" the real (rotated) palm the further the wrist moves away from
wherever a pose's curl values happened to be tuned by eye -- exactly
matching "if I turn off responsive wrist splay, it's fine" (extraSplay
is always 0 there, so the mismatch never appears) and "every finger [is]
less curled" (every finger shares this same axis convention -- fixing
only the thumb, as the previous round did, could only ever address a
fraction of the problem, which is exactly why it looked "worse": the
user was now comparing a correctly-tracking thumb against 4 fingers that
still weren't).

Fix: `applyCurlToSkeleton()` now reads the WRIST BONE's own current
world rotation (`wrapperQuat` excluded, the exact same technique
`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` already used) as the curl/splay axis reference,
instead of the static `baseQuat`. This already incorporates `baseQuat`
as its own prefix (the wrist bone is a descendant of the mesh clone that
`baseQuat` orients), so Whole-Hand Rotation's existing behavior is
completely unaffected -- only wrist bend/splay awareness is added on top.

**Verified with a genuinely independent, anatomically-meaningful test --
not another self-consistency check.** Measured the angle between a
curled finger's own segment direction and the wrist bone's own reference
axis, at `wristSplay=0` vs. `wristSplay=-70` (confirmed the wrist itself
rotated a real 70.000 degrees between the two): the finger-to-wrist angle
measured **78.006 degrees at BOTH**, proving curl now genuinely tracks
wrist rotation instead of staying fixed in world space -- the exact
anatomical invariant that should hold. Re-ran the full existing
regression suite (idle-drift + real click-transition round-trip, all 5
fingers, 11 hands spread through the field): still exactly 0.0 degrees,
confirming this change didn't regress either of the same-day per-frame
desync fixes.

**Disclosed, expected side effect:** this changes the rendered appearance
of any existing saved pose that has a nonzero `wristBend`/`wristSplay`
(their curl values were tuned by eye against the OLD, anatomically-
incorrect behavior) -- not a new bug, a direct and correct consequence
of fixing the actual reference frame. Not yet independently confirmed by
the user on a real device/browser at the time of this entry.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=77` -> `?v=78`.

2026-09-14  -  (start not captured -- Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**Cross-checked HANDO's own recent changes for pose-export
compatibility, per direct request ("can you look into hando's new
changes to make sure you two are matching since i will be exporting
poses from hando"), and found 2 real things to fix.**

**(1) The curl-axis fix from the previous entry used a DIFFERENT formula
than HANDO's own concurrent fix for the identical bug.** HANDO was
independently fixed the same day (its own commit `3262675`) for the
exact same root cause found here -- but via a different technique:
HANDO computes the wrist's LOCAL delta-from-its-own-rest rotation
(`wristRestQuat^-1 * wristBone.quaternion`) and composes it with
`modelRoot.quaternion` (delta applied first), instead of reading the
wrist bone's full WORLD quaternion the way this project's own fix just
had. Directly measured whether these 2 independently-derived
"wrist-relative" fixes actually agree: **they don't** -- `rHand`'s own
rest quaternion is far from identity
(`[-0.078, 0.688, -0.086, 0.716]`), so the 2 formulas diverge by a
measured 88.5 degrees for the same wrist state. Since the actual goal
here is pose-export PARITY with HANDO, not an independently-"correct"
convention, switched to HANDO's exact formula. Verified against 2
independent reconstructions of HANDO's own math (not by calling this
project's own code as its own reference): a direct formula comparison
and a full end-to-end bone-rotation reconstruction both measured exactly
0.0 degrees of difference from the real production output. Re-ran the
existing idle-drift + click-round-trip regression suite: still 0.0
degrees.

**(2) HANDO has a real feature this project had no concept of at all:
"Tip-Only Curl"** (4 sliders -- Index/Middle/Ring/Pinky, no thumb), the
mirror of Base-Only Curl (additive rotation on the LAST joint instead of
the first). Same failure mode as the earlier-this-saga Base-Only Curl
gap: a pose exported from HANDO using this slider would have had that
data silently dropped on import here. Ported: `FINGER_TIP_ONLY_CURL_KEY`
map, 4 new sliders (placed between Base-Only Curl and Tip Twist in each
finger's own subgroup, matching HANDO's own ordering), the additive
`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` call on the last joint, and the 4 new keys
added to `POSE_PRESET_KEYS` and each finger's subgroup. Verified in
isolation: setting only `tipOnlyCurlIndex` to 60% rotated ONLY the last
joint (`rIndex3`) by exactly 42.0 degrees (70-degree max * 0.6, matching
`FINGER_MAX_DEG.index[2]`) while joints 0 and 1 measured exactly 0.0
degrees of change. Regression suite re-run again: still 0.0 degrees.

**Also checked and confirmed NO mismatch (informational, no code
change needed):** HANDO's own wrist-before-fingers ordering matches this
project's own convention exactly (both apply wrist pose before finger
curl in their live pose-application pass); HANDO has no equivalent of
Responsive Wrist Splay (its own wrist splay is purely static/slider-set
-- its own bug report that triggered its fix was a static
`wristSplay=-50` + a tween, not anything "reactive," consistent with
this project's own finding that ANY nonzero wrist rotation triggers the
mismatch, not specifically a LIVE one); HANDO's own saved-pose format
additionally carries `hideWrist` and 6 arm/shoulder/elbow keys this
project has no rigging for at all -- these are harmless extra fields on
an imported pose (silently ignored, not applied), not a compatibility
problem.

Cache-buster bumps: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=78` -> `?v=81` across
this round (one intermediate debug-exposure bump).

2026-09-15  -  (start not captured -- Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**Confirmed live: git-tracked Save Settings (previous entry) is genuinely
working in production.** Pushing this round's own commit was rejected
(non-fast-forward) because 3 real auto-commits ("Update dev-panel-
settings.json via Save Settings") had already landed on the remote from
actual production traffic -- exactly HANDO's own documented pattern.
Merged cleanly (the auto-commits only touch the settings JSON, this
round's own commit didn't) and pushed. No longer an open question.

**Fixed a critical correctness bug in the curl-axis math shipped earlier
today, via a direct handoff from a concurrent session working on HANDO.**
That earlier fix computed `axisRefQuat = baseQuat * (wristRest^-1 *
wristBone.quaternion)` -- treating a LOCAL (body-frame) rotation delta as
if it were already a WORLD-frame operator. Those only agree when the
wrist's own rest pose is the identity quaternion, which it isn't here
(confirmed earlier: `rHand`'s rest quaternion is `[-0.078, 0.688, -0.086,
0.716]`). HANDO's own team found and fixed the identical bug in HANDO
itself the same day and handed off the corrected math: a genuine
conjugation, `worldDelta = W1 * delta * W1^-1`, where W1 is the wrist
bone's own CURRENT WORLD quaternion. Adapted (not copied verbatim) for
this project's own extra `wrapperQuat` layer (HANDO has none) by
factoring wrapperQuat back out of W1 before conjugating -- the same
technique `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` already uses.

While implementing this, live testing caught a 2ND, self-introduced bug:
since W1 already includes `baseQuat` as its own ancestor, premultiplying
`baseQuat` onto `worldDelta` again double-applied it -- confirmed both
algebraically and empirically (a real 1-6 degree relative-to-wrist error
across joints with the double-apply; exactly 0.0 degrees once removed).

**Verified with the exact methodology the handoff itself described:**
compared every finger joint's orientation RELATIVE TO THE WRIST at
wristSplay=0 vs. wristSplay=-50 (identical finger values held constant
across the comparison, multiple fingers, every curl/splay/bias/base-
only/mid-only/tip-only/tip-twist slider simultaneously nonzero) -- 0.0000
degrees of difference on every joint tested (thumb, index, pinky, all 3
segments each). Re-ran the existing idle-drift + click-round-trip
regression suite: still 0.0 degrees, confirming this didn't regress
anything from earlier today.

**Re-checked HANDO's own Pose group for new sliders** (direct request)
and found HANDO completed its Base-Only/Mid-Only/Tip-Only joint-isolation
set for all 5 fingers including the thumb (this project only had Base-
Only for all 5, and Tip-Only excluding the thumb -- ported earlier today
matching HANDO's THEN-current scope). Ported the 2 gaps: `tipOnlyCurlThumb`
and `midOnlyCurl{Thumb,Index,Middle,Ring,Pinky}` (5 new sliders, joint-1
targeting, identical mechanism to Base-Only/Tip-Only). Added to
`POSE_PRESET_KEYS` and each finger's own Pose subgroup.

**Added Camera presets (Save/Use/Overwrite/Rename/Delete/Default) + Lock
Camera Pan + Lock Camera Zoom + Set Default Camera As Max Extents**,
direct request. Saved Cameras list-picker mirrors Saved Poses' exact UI
shape (`captureCameraPreset()`/`applyCameraPreset()`), applied directly
to the live camera+pan-target rather than a preview model. Lock Pan/Zoom
compose correctly with the pre-existing Click-Hold-Pose pan-disable (a
hold still always disables pan; releasing it now respects a genuine
permanent lock instead of unconditionally restoring pan).

Max Extents went through several rounds of direct correction before
landing on its final model: zoom-out is capped via OrbitControls' own
native `maxDistance` (zooming in stays completely free -- no snap, no
reset, just a standard max-distance clamp); pan is capped via a custom
per-frame clamp using that SAME radius, since OrbitControls has no
native pan-bounding option (free movement back toward the default
target always stays free; only moving further OUTWARD than the radius
hits a hard wall -- clamped in place, not reset/snapped, per direct
correction: "I dont get snapped back. that only applies to zoom" was
itself further corrected to "the max i can zoom out is the extents", a
plain cap, not a teleport).

Verified live: capture/apply preset round-trip lands exactly back at the
captured camera position AND pan target; Lock Pan/Zoom checkboxes clicked
through the real UI correctly toggle `controls.enablePan`/`enableZoom`;
Max Extents zoom clamps exactly to the default's own distance (260.171)
while zooming in to 0.3x that distance stays completely unclamped; Max
Extents pan clamps a target set to 3x the allowed radius down to exactly
the boundary, while a target already within bounds (0.5x radius) is left
completely untouched.

2026-09-15  -  01:54:19 - 02:11:58 EDT (17m39s, measured; spans investigation,
root-cause discovery, fix, and verification)

**Found and fixed the ACTUAL wrist-splay/curl bug this whole saga was
chasing -- previous "corrected" conjugation formula collapsed to identity
at wristBend=wristSplay=0 exactly, silently discarding `baseQuat`
(alignQuat) from every finger's curl axis.** User reports of "still have
wrist splay issues" persisted even after the prior round's conjugation
fix (`W1 * delta * W1^-1`) passed every relative-to-wrist invariance test
run against it, including live production tests with real saved poses.
Direct user reports narrowed it down over several exchanges: "even fist
doesnt look right... with no responsive wrist splay turned on" and "the
ones with 0 [wristSplay] as the input seem to look fine" turned out to
have it backwards in direction but right in spirit -- poses with
wristSplay=0 (e.g. "Fist") were the ones rendering broken (flat, wing-
like wedge shapes instead of a closed fist), while nonzero-wristSplay
poses ("Neutral - Bent Back", "Fist - Bent Back") rendered correctly.

Root cause: at delta = identity (wristBend = wristSplay = 0 exactly), the
conjugation `W1 * delta * W1^-1` collapses to IDENTITY for ANY W1 at all
-- confirmed live via direct computation (axisRefQuat read back as
[0,0,0,1] instead of baseQuat's actual [-0.712,0.021,0,0.702]). The
relative-to-wrist invariance test used throughout this whole saga only
checks that a finger's orientation relative to the wrist stays constant
as wristSplay CHANGES -- a systematically biased but internally self-
consistent formula can satisfy that test while still being wrong in
absolute terms, which is exactly what happened: every test case run this
entire session (Fist - Bent Back, Neutral - Bent Back, the Click-Hold-
Pose round trip) happened to use a nonzero wristSplay, so the delta=I
degenerate case was never exercised until a real saved pose ("Fist")
finally hit it.

Fix (`applyCurlToSkeleton()`, `src/main.js`): conjugate `delta` by
`wristRestForAxis` (R) alone instead of by the wrist bone's full world
quat (W1) -- `baseQuat * R * delta * R^-1` -- which correctly reduces to
exactly `baseQuat` when delta = I (matching the pre-existing no-wrist-
bone fallback branch, which has always used `baseQuat` directly for that
case) while still rotating the curl axis to track the wrist's actual
bend/splay when delta != I. No longer needs the wrist bone's world
quaternion or `wrapperQuat` for this computation at all.

Verified 3 ways: (1) direct computation -- axisRefQuat now matches
baseQuat exactly at delta=I, <1e-6 per component, both live-production-
read and the new formula computed side by side; (2) visual -- "Fist"
applied via direct cfg values on a fresh local dev-server load now
renders as an actual compact closed fist across the whole field, versus
flat wedge/wing shapes before the fix (screenshot comparison); (3) the
PRE-EXISTING relative-to-wrist invariance check (comparing a finger's
orientation relative to the wrist between wristSplay=0 and wristSplay=-50
with every other slider held identical) still holds at 0.0000 degrees
under the new formula -- the fix does not regress the previously-verified
nonzero-delta case.

Also confirmed, this round: the `wristSplayResponsiveEnabled` /
`wristSplayReactiveEnabled` true-vs-false discrepancy noted at the top of
this investigation was a timing artifact (read moments before vs. after a
real user Save), not a real bug -- resolved once the user's own save
landed and a fresh page load matched. Ruled out as contributing factors,
per direct user correction: the "shoulder/elbow/forearm" and "Hide Wrist"
slider families HANDO has that this project doesn't port 1:1 (`hideWrist`
here is a structurally different, live-reactive-by-cursor-distance Arm
Length feature, not a static per-pose value like HANDO's) -- "shoulder
elbow and forearm isnt relevatn... mostly its the wrist and finger
settings that matter."

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=85` -> `?v=86`.

2026-09-15  -  02:45:28 - 02:58:49 EDT (13m21s, measured)

**Found and fixed a 2nd, unrelated bug that had been masquerading as the
wrist-splay issue this whole time: `FINGER_SIGN.middle`/`.ring` were
still `-1`, never synced with HANDO's own 2026-09-14 sign flip.** User
report after implementing the delta=identity fix (previous entry): "Poses
still look good in Hando after implementation, while mine still look bad.
anything with a wrist splay still looks fucked" -- then, once "Neutral"
was set as Default and "Fist" tested via Click-Pose specifically: "even
fist doesnt look right... with no responsive wrist splay turned on" --
then, decisively, "Point and MiddleFinger also look wrong" (both have
wristSplay=0, ruling out anything wrist-splay-specific) and finally
"anything with a wrist splay still looks fucked" was narrowed further:
"Fist (0 splay) and Fist-Bent Back (-50) look messed up" -- both, not
just the nonzero one.

Direct A/B test settled it: checked out a scratch git worktree at the
commit BEFORE the delta=identity conjugation fix, applied the exact same
"Point" pose data there, and got the SAME fragmented, self-intersecting
geometry as on current production -- proving the conjugation fix (this
file's previous entry) was NOT the cause of this specific breakage, and
something else, present in BOTH the old and new formula, was responsible.

Root cause, found by diffing HANDY DANDIES's `applyCurlToSkeleton()`
constants against HANDO's own: HANDO flipped `FINGER_SIGN.middle`/`.ring`
from `-1` to `1` on 2026-09-14 (direct HANDO user request: "for middle
finger and ring finger curl, inverse the number. right now, curling it is
negative numbers") and migrated its own existing saved poses' curlMiddle/
curlRing values to preserve their visual meaning under the new sign. This
project's own `FINGER_SIGN` was never updated to match -- but every pose
in this project's own saved-pose list was captured/imported from HANDO
AFTER that migration, so all of them (Point, MiddleFinger, Fist, Fist -
Bent Back, ThumbsUp, Wave 1-6...) already use positive curlMiddle/
curlRing for an actual curl, consistent with HANDO's NEW convention --
meaning middle/ring curled BACKWARD on every one of them here, on top of
whatever curl the other correctly-signed fingers were doing, producing
exactly the chaotic, fragmented look reported. "Neutral"/"Neutral - Bent
Back" (all curl values 0, sign irrelevant at 0) were the only 2 poses
unaffected -- coincidentally the same 2 poses this whole wrist-splay
investigation had been testing against, which is why this went unnoticed
until poses with real curl values (Point, Fist) were finally checked.

Fixed with a pure sign flip (`FINGER_SIGN.middle`/`.ring`: `-1` -> `1`),
no data migration needed here (per the above -- this project's own saved
poses already assume the new convention). Verified: "Point" applied via
the real saved-pose data now renders as a correctly-formed pointing hand
(extended index, curled fist body, visible thumb) both in a scratch
worktree test and on live production; "Fist - Bent Back" (wristSplay=-50)
renders as a proper closed fist with fingertips visibly curling toward
the palm; the pre-existing relative-to-wrist invariance check (from the
delta=identity fix) still holds at 0.0000 degrees -- this fix doesn't
touch the wrist-axis math at all, only the finger-curl sign table.
