2026-09-15  -  03:09:52 - 03:21:56 EDT (12m04s, measured)

**Fixed Click-Pose's start-of-transition stutter and a mouse-log click/
drag mislabeling bug, both from a direct user report with a screen
recording and mouse log attached.**

**Click-Pose stutter.** User: "Default pose is Neutral-BentBack. Target
pose is Fist. The fist looks good, But i noticed that right after te
click, the closest hand seems to start some sort of animation
transition, but stops after a split second, then the click-pose function
runs smoothly." Root cause: `startClickHoldPose()` fires unconditionally
on EVERY left `pointerdown` (needed so a genuine hold-and-drag disables
camera pan immediately) -- with no way to tell a plain click apart from
the start of a real hold at press time. The closest hand's own distance-
based start delay can be exactly 0ms, so it began visibly transitioning
toward Click Hold-Pose's own target pose ("Fist - Bent Back" -- a
DIFFERENT pose than Click Pose's own "Fist") on the very next frame, then
reversed the instant the real, brief click released (`endClickHoldPose()`
on `pointerup`) -- all before Click Pose's own (correctly debounced,
per the 2026-09-14 entry) transition even began. Fixed by adding a new
"Hold Confirm Delay (Ms)" slider per trigger (chp/rchp, default 150ms):
`updateClickHoldPoseForHand()` now refuses to leave 'idle' until the hold
has genuinely been sustained past it, and `endClickHoldPose()` skips
starting a retransition for a hand that never left 'idle' (avoiding a
pointless lerp-to-itself using stale/undefined `lastAppliedValues`). The
camera-pan lock -- `startClickHoldPose()`'s OTHER job -- still engages
immediately on pointerdown, unaffected, per the original direct request
that moving the cursor during a hold must never pan even before the hold
is confirmed. Verified live: a real fast click via the actual DOM
pointerdown/pointerup path leaves the closest hand's CHP phase at 'idle'
throughout (confirmed by direct inspection, not just visual judgment).

**Mouse log click/drag mislabeling**, same report: "how come clicks are
logging as Pans even though its just a click, not even a click hold
drag." Root cause: `describeMouseLogTrigger()` was called once per
`pointerup`, BEFORE click-vs-drag was even classified, and always
described button 0/1/2 as "Camera Pan"/"Camera Zoom-Dolly (OrbitControls,
X-drag)" regardless of whether anything was actually dragged -- every
call site, including the genuine "Click at"/"Double-click at" lines,
reused that same drag-worded string. Fixed by passing a `wasDrag` flag
(the caller already computes heldMs/moved) so a plain click is described
by button alone ("Canvas (Left Click)") and the Camera Pan/Zoom-Dolly
wording is reserved for genuine drags. Side effect, also correct: a quick
right-click still gets its own immediate "Right-click at" line, but a
GENUINE right-drag (previously always mislabeled as a plain right-click,
regardless of heldMs) now correctly falls through to "Drag-release ...
Camera Pan (OrbitControls, right-drag)". Verified live: a real click logs
"Click at (x, y) -> Canvas (Left Click)"; a real drag still logs
"Drag-release at (x, y) -> Camera Pan (OrbitControls, left-drag)
(heldMs:2014)".

**New feature: Tween + Double Click Hold Tween.** Direct user request,
modeled on HANDO's own "Tween / Export" dev-panel group but deliberately
narrower ("We dont need that. we just need poses") and with a different
trigger mechanic than HANDO's manual preview slider.

**Tween group** (new DEV_GROUPS entry): `tweenPoses`, a 'multi-select'
control (same generic devpanel/devPanel.js type HANDO's own tween group
uses -- a growable list of dropdowns backed by one ordered array of saved-
pose names) extended here with drag-to-reorder, direct request ("allow me
to click and drag to reorder these"). devpanel/devPanel.js's own
`renderMultiSelectRows()`/`buildMultiSelectRow()` gained a dedicated
`.dp-ms-row-handle` drag icon per row (same §12e "handle icon only, never
the row itself" convention as list-picker's own `.dp-lp-row-handle`) and
now calls the SAME generic `setupReorder()` list-picker rows/groups
already use, resyncing `entry.values` from the post-drop DOM order rather

lighting capture to bundle in the first place).

**Double Click Hold group** (new DEV_GROUPS entry): Master On/Off, a
"Tween Selector" dropdown (options sourced from `savedTweenSequences`),
and a single "Tween Speed (Ms)" slider. 2 rounds of direct clarification
resolved the exact behavior: (1) the sequence always tweens FROM THE
DEFAULT POSE through the selected sequence's own poses in order --
`startDoubleClickHoldTween()` prepends `poseDefaultValues` to the
resolved pose list, so even a 1-pose sequence tweens default->pose1; (2)
on release, every hand retransitions back to default from WHATEVER pose
it's currently in, at the SAME speed as the forward tween -- no separate
retransition-speed control, unlike Click-Hold-Pose's own 2 groups.
Applies IDENTICALLY to every hand at once (no per-hand distance stagger,
unlike Click-Hold-Pose/Click-Pose) -- `updateDoubleClickHoldTween()` is
computed ONCE per frame in `animate()`, not once per hand, and the result
applied to every hand via the existing `applyPoseValuesToHand()`
pipeline (same one Click-Hold-Pose/Click-Pose already use -- no new
skeleton-posing math needed).

**Gesture detection**: "double click hold" (click, release, click-and-
HOLD -- distinct from Click-Pose's own fire-and-forget 'dblclick', which
recognizes at the 2nd RELEASE, and from Click-Hold-Pose's single press-
and-hold). Implemented as a self-contained window pointerdown/up pair:
`dcHoldLastCleanUpTime` records the last left pointerup that was a clean,
quick click (reusing the Mouse Tracking Log's own MOUSE_LOG_MULTICLICK_MS/
MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS/MOUSE_LOG_MOVE_THRESHOLD_PX thresholds for
consistency, not fresh constants); a left pointerdown arriving within
that window starts the tween immediately -- deliberately independent of
chp/click/dblclick's own state (a disclosed simplification, same
"whichever runs last this frame wins" pattern already used for chp vs.
rchp).

Verified live on a local dev server via real dispatched PointerEvents
(down, up, down-and-hold, up) rather than synthetic function calls: drag-
reorder on `tweenPoses` correctly resynced `cfg.tweenPoses` from DOM
order (confirmed via `commit()`'s own downstream state); saving a Tween
Sequence correctly captured only `tweenPoses`; the Double Click Hold
Tween Selector dropdown correctly picked up the newly-saved sequence
name; performing the actual gesture visibly posed every hand in the
field IDENTICALLY (confirmed via screenshot -- a uniform pose across the
whole field, unlike Click-Hold-Pose's own per-hand staggered look) and
releasing correctly retransitioned every hand back to its resting pose,
with no console errors beyond the pre-existing, unrelated
`api/save-settings` 404s (expected on a plain static server, no Vercel
endpoint behind it).

2026-09-15  -  04:01:12 - 04:09:38 EDT (8m26s, measured)

**Double Click Hold: added a Loop checkbox.** Direct follow-up request:
"provide a checkbox to set if it continues to loop or not. As in if i
keep the double click hold, it will continue looping through the tween,
not including the initial default pose. If unchecked, it runs through
the tween once." Unchecked (the pre-existing behavior, still the
default): the hand holds at the final pose once reached. Checked: once
the initial default->pose1->...->poseN pass completes,
`updateDoubleClickHoldTween()` hands off to a new 'looping' phase that
cycles through JUST the named poses (never re-including default) --
poseN->pose1->pose2->...->poseN, wrapping, via a new `lerpLoopSequence()`
helper -- for as long as the hold continues, at the SAME per-segment pace
as the initial pass (`loopSegmentMs = speedMs / namedPoses.length`, so
looping doesn't visibly speed up/slow down relative to lap 1).

Caught and fixed one real bug before ever running it: the loop's own
cyclic clock originally started at `tCyclic = 0`, which maps to
loopPoses[0]->loopPoses[1] -- since the forward pass had JUST ended
exactly at the last pose, this would have produced a visible backward
jump/pop the instant looping began, instead of continuing smoothly.
Fixed by offsetting the loop's start position to `segments - 1` (the WRAP
segment, last pose -> first pose), so motion continues seamlessly with no
pop, confirmed via manual trace of the segment-index math at the
phase-transition boundary.

**Live-verification limitation, disclosed:** confirmed the combined app
loads and runs with 0 console errors through an extended hold (well past
the loop threshold) and a release, and confirmed the Loop checkbox
renders and is wired to `cfg.dcHoldLoop` correctly. Could NOT confirm
frame-by-frame visual animation during the loop via this session's
browser-automation tool -- repeated `canvas.toDataURL()` samples a full
second apart, taken via `javascript_tool`, came back byte-identical even
mid-hold, consistent with this project's own already-documented gotcha
("this session's own browser-automation tool reports
`document.hidden`/`innerWidth === 0`... a characteristic of that tool,
not a page bug" -- and an earlier session's own note that the render
loop can go genuinely paused during JS-exec-based polling specifically).
Correctness here rests on the manually-traced segment math above and the
error-free extended hold, not a confirmed live frame capture -- worth a
real-device check.

**Also present in this commit: a "Right Click" trigger group and an
idle-repose performance fix, from a CONCURRENT session working in this
same local working tree while this task was in progress** (detected via
an on-disk file-change notice mid-edit; confirmed via `git diff` that
both sets of changes are complete, non-overlapping, and coexist without
conflict). Right Click (`rcEnabled`/`rcMode`/`rcTargetPose`/
`rcTweenSelector`/timing controls) is a 3rd fire-and-forget Click-Pose-
family instance on the right button, with a Mode dropdown choosing a
single Target Pose or a full Tween Sequence as its target -- reuses
`resolveTweenSequencePoses()`/`lerpTweenSequence()` from the Tween
feature above. The performance fix replaces `updateRenderOrder()`'s own
unconditional-every-frame idle repose with a `needsIdleRepose` gate
(`cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled || hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame`),
noting a measured ~38ms/frame cost at 255 hands. Neither was authored or
independently verified by this entry's own author -- documented here
only because it landed in the same uncommitted working tree and commit;
see whichever session did that work for its own fuller account if one
gets written separately.

2026-09-15  -  (start not captured -- session resumed from a compacted
summary; Actual: NOT MEASURED)

**Fuller account of the Right Click + idle-repose performance fix**
(originally landed via a concurrent session's commit, disclosed above as
unauthored/unverified by that entry's own author) -- this entry is that
work's own author, verifying it live after the fact.

**Performance fix, confirmed with real measurements (`performance.now()`
around direct `updateRenderOrder()` calls, 255 hands, 50ms gaps between
calls -- same isolated-timing method this project's own earlier lag
investigation established as reliable):** with Responsive Wrist Splay ON,
~30-44ms/call (matches the ~38ms/frame figure already on record); with it
OFF, ~0.2-0.7ms/call -- the idle repose gate (`needsIdleRepose =
cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled || hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame`)
eliminates essentially the entire cost when the feature is off, confirmed
directly rather than assumed from the code alone. `hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame`
forces exactly one repose the frame after a Click-Hold/Click-Pose
transition ends, so a hand returning to idle still re-syncs its
(possibly stale) finger curl before the gate can skip it again.

**Right Click, verified live via `window.__debug`:** `triggerClickPose('rc')`
in Single Pose mode correctly entered 'forward' with a real
`forwardSnapshot` and no `tweenPoses`; manually stepping
`updateRenderOrder()` (this session's browser-automation tool has its own
already-documented rAF-timing unreliability, so real frames were driven
directly rather than waited on) carried the phase through
forward->paused->retransition, with `lastAppliedValues.curlIndex`
reaching the target pose's own value (96, "Fist") then back down through
retransition. Tween mode (a fabricated 2-pose test sequence via
`cfg.savedTweenSequences`) resolved `cp.tweenPoses` to length 3
([forwardSnapshot, pose1, pose2]) as designed, and the same phase
machine played it through to 'idle' with a plausible final
`lastAppliedValues.curlIndex` (89) on the way back to default. The Mode
dropdown's conditional UI (Target Pose visible only in Single Pose mode,
Tween Sequence only in Tween mode) was confirmed toggling live via a
direct `change` event, using an inline `style.display` toggle rather than
the `hidden` attribute -- `.dp-row { display: flex }` in style.css is an
author rule that silently wins the cascade over the UA stylesheet's
`[hidden] { display: none }` at equal specificity regardless of source
order, so `.hidden = true` alone would have done nothing.

**Bug found and fixed post-hoc: 3 empty dropdowns.** `rcMode`,
`rcTargetPose`, and `rcTweenSelector` all rendered with zero `<option>`s
on page load (confirmed via direct DOM inspection, not assumed) -- the
same TDZ-timing issue already worked around for `chpTargetPose`/
`rchpTargetPose`/`clickTargetPose`/`dblclickTargetPose` (a `select`
control's initial `<option>` list is populated during `initDevPanel()`'s
own restore-from-storage step, which runs before `const cfg = ...`
finishes assigning, so any closure reading `cfg` -- or, it turns out,
ANY select built this early, even `rcMode`'s own plain literal array with
no `cfg` dependency at all -- ends up empty and never gets a 2nd chance
outside an explicit one-time re-populate). Fixed by adding the same 3
missing `safeRefreshSelectOptions()` calls to the existing one-time
list right after `cfg` is real. Committed separately (`5daac10`) since
the feature commit itself had already been pushed by the other session
before this fix was found.

Live-tested end to end on the local static dev server (`?dev=1`); 0
console errors beyond the pre-existing, unrelated `api/save-settings`
404s (expected -- no Vercel endpoint behind this local server, matches
this project's own documented single-tier Save Settings design).

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=92` -> `?v=93`.

2026-09-15  -  04:21:00 - 04:30:25 EDT (9m25s, measured)

**Investigated direct user report: "all my click functions stopped
working."** No single confirmed root cause on production, but found and
closed one real, concrete crash path and added a general-purpose
backstop against the whole class of failure this symptom best matches.

**What was verified NOT broken**, directly on live production via the
`window.__debug` hook (already exposed by an earlier commit): called
`triggerClickPose('click')` and a real dispatched click both correctly
advanced `hand._cp.click.phase` through forward -> paused -> retransition
and wrote real, non-default rotation values onto the actual skeleton
bones (confirmed on `rThumb1` directly) -- the entire trigger pipeline
(gesture detection -> `triggerClickPose` -> `updateClickPoseForHand` ->
`applyPoseValuesToHand` -> skeleton) is provably correct and unbroken.
Zero console errors on a fresh production load.

**What live-frame verification could NOT settle:** `renderer.info.render.frame`
appeared stuck across repeated samples, and even an independent
`requestAnimationFrame` probe (nothing to do with this app's own code)
showed zero increments over 2 full seconds under this session's own
browser-automation tool -- matching (and further confirming, via the
generic probe) this project's own already-documented gotcha that this
tool's rAF-based live capture goes unreliable under its own JS-exec
polling. Manually stepping `updateRenderOrder()` directly (bypassing rAF
entirely, via `window.__debug`) confirmed the update pipeline itself
works regardless -- but this means the tool available in this session
could not confirm or rule out a genuinely stalled render loop in the
user's own real browser.

**One real, concrete crash path found and fixed via code review:**
`updateDoubleClickHoldTween()`'s own `speedMs`/`loopSegmentMs`
(Double Click Hold's Loop mode, added 2 entries ago) divide directly by
`cfg.dcHoldTweenSpeedMs` with no guard against it being `NaN`/undefined
(e.g. a stale saved-settings value predating that control). A NaN here
cascades: NaN `tCyclic` -> `poses[NaN]` -> `undefined` -> `lerpPoseValues()`
throwing on `undefined[key]`. Because this throw happens INSIDE
`animate()`'s per-frame body, and recurs on EVERY frame for as long as a
hand keeps looping, `composer.render()` never gets reached again --
the screen freezes at whatever last rendered while every click still
correctly (but invisibly) updates state underneath, exactly matching
"stopped working." Fixed with `Number.isFinite()` guards (fallback to
the control's own 800ms default) at both division sites.

**General-purpose backstop, not tied to the specific bug above:**
wrapped `animate()`'s entire per-frame body in try/catch. Previously, ANY
future per-frame exception (from this bug, a different one, or one not
written yet) would have the same silent-freeze signature -- `console.error`
never shown to a non-technical user, screen just stops updating, "click
functions stopped working" with no visible cause. Now: whatever throws
gets logged loudly and the loop keeps running -- the current frame's
render is skipped, but the NEXT frame gets a clean chance rather than
repeating the same throw forever (rAF was already re-scheduled at the
very top of `animate()`, so this doesn't change WHETHER the loop
survives a one-off throw, only whether a RECURRING one does, and whether
either kind is now visible in the console instead of silent).

**Disclosed limitation:** could not directly reproduce the user's exact
reported freeze in this session's own environment (fresh production
loads never exhibited a recurring per-frame exception). This fix closes
a real, concrete gap consistent with the reported symptom rather than a
confirmed-reproduced root cause -- ask the user to confirm whether this
resolves it, and if it recurs, `console.error`'s own new "animate() frame
threw" message plus its stack trace should now pinpoint the exact cause
directly, rather than requiring another investigation like this one.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=93` -> `?v=94`.

2026-09-15  -  06:09:41 - 07:04:17 EDT (54m36s, measured -- ran well over
its own ~15-25min opening ETA; see this entry's own closing note for why)

**Root-caused and fixed the ACTUAL "all my click functions stopped
working" bug**, following a much more specific direct user report this
time: "1. Startup (hard refresh) 2. I see the hands but with a weird
surface texture as if there is overlapping 3d models... rotation etc
looks off. 3. I click, then suddenly it looks correct... Palm following
cursor works. 4. I click or click hold to test click function. Nothing
happens."

**Reproduced directly on a fresh production load, visually** (a real
screenshot, not a JS-exec query -- see this entry's own "tool
limitation" note below for why that distinction mattered): every hand
rendered as long, straight, spike-like shapes radiating outward -- the
raw, un-posed GLB bind pose (straight fingers/forearm), not this
project's actual default pose. Confirmed via `window.__debug`:
`hands[0].currentBaseQuat` read exactly `[0,0,0,1]` (IDENTITY) on this
fresh load, while `cloneBaseQuat` (the correct, already-computed shared
value) read `[-0.712, 0.021, 0, 0.702]` -- and `cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled`
was `false` for this real user's actual saved settings.

**Root cause:** `hand.currentBaseQuat` is seeded from `cloneBaseQuat` at
hand-creation time (`rebuildField()`) -- if `cloneBaseQuat` is STILL its
own module-load-time identity default at that exact moment (cfg hasn't
finished restoring from the git-tracked settings log yet when the field
first builds -- a real, plausible race, not a hypothetical), every
hand's own snapshot freezes on that stale identity FOREVER, unless
something re-syncs it. Before the previous entry's idle-repose
performance gate existed, that re-sync ran UNCONDITIONALLY every single
frame, so a stale snapshot self-corrected within 1 frame, invisibly --
nobody had ever seen this bug because it never survived long enough to
be visible. That gate's `needsIdleRepose = cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled
|| hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame` broke this guarantee for anyone with
Responsive Wrist Splay off: neither condition is true on a hand's very
first real frame, so the resync never runs at all until SOMETHING ELSE
incidentally triggers it -- exactly what a click does (either
`_wasOverriddenLastFrame` forcing 1 catch-up frame after the click ends,
or the click's own `applyPoseValuesToHand` independently recomputing a
correct `currentBaseQuat` from its own target pose). Neither is a real
fix; both are lucky side effects that happen to look like "the first
click fixes it."

**Fix:** added `hand.everReposed` (false at creation, see
`rebuildField()`'s own comment on the `hand` object literal) and folded
it into the gate: `needsIdleRepose = cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled ||
hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame || !hand.everReposed`. Forces exactly ONE
guaranteed full repose per hand regardless of the other 2 conditions,
then never forces it again -- restores the original per-hand correctness
guarantee without reintroducing the ~38ms/frame cost the performance fix
was written to eliminate.

**Verified directly** (bypassing the render loop entirely, per this
project's own established "manually step the update function instead of
trusting live rAF" methodology): simulated the exact broken state on a
real hand (`currentBaseQuat` forced to identity, `everReposed`/
`_wasOverriddenLastFrame` both false, `wristSplayResponsiveEnabled`
false) and called `updateRenderOrder()` ONCE, with no click involved --
`currentBaseQuat` correctly self-healed to match `cloneBaseQuat`, and
`everReposed` flipped true.

**Tool limitation, disclosed (why this took as long as it did):** this
session's own browser-automation tool repeatedly reported
`document.hidden: true` / `window.innerWidth: 0` on tabs that were
genuinely fronted and interactive -- the SAME already-documented gotcha
from earlier this project, re-confirmed multiple times before a plain
`computer{screenshot}` call (which this project's own CLAUDE.md already
notes works correctly even when the JS-exec path doesn't) finally showed
the real, reproducible visual bug directly. A `GL_INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION:
... Attachment has zero size` WebGL warning also appeared on that same
fresh load -- real (browser/GPU-level, not JS-exec-spoofable) but NOT
investigated further this round, since the screenshot + `currentBaseQuat`
evidence already fully explained the reported symptom on its own; worth
a separate look if a startup sizing issue is ever reported on its own.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=94` -> `?v=95`.

2026-09-15  -  07:08:20 - 07:12:46 EDT (4m26s, measured)

**Found and fixed the REAL "click functinos still not working" bug --
distinct from both prior rounds' fixes.** Direct user report with a fresh
mouse log showing repeated genuine clicks/holds producing no visible
effect. The previous entry's `currentBaseQuat`/`everReposed` fix was real
and correct (confirmed fixing the startup appearance) but didn't touch
this: the idle-repose performance gate (landed via a concurrent session's
commit, `4d25b9a`) replaced the original `if (!overridden) { ...idle
repose... }` with `if (needsIdleRepose)`, where `needsIdleRepose` never
actually included `!overridden` as a condition.

Concretely: `hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame` is true starting the 2nd frame
of ANY active trigger sequence (chp/rchp/click/dblclick/rc/dcHold) and
stays true for every frame the sequence keeps running. Since
`needsIdleRepose = cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled || hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame
|| !hand.everReposed` doesn't check `overridden` for the CURRENT frame,
it was ALSO true on every one of those frames -- so the idle-repose
branch ran immediately after `applyPoseValuesToHand()` had just written
that frame's own in-progress transition pose, and overwrote it right
back to `cfg`'s plain default values. Net effect: every trigger's pose
only ever stuck for a single frame before being stomped back to default,
frame after frame, at normal framerate indistinguishable from "the click
did nothing at all."

Fixed with one added condition: `const needsIdleRepose = !overridden &&
(cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled || hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame ||
!hand.everReposed)` -- restores the original mutual exclusion (a hand is
EITHER actively driven by a trigger this frame OR eligible for idle
repose, never both) without touching any of the earlier fix's 3
legitimate conditions.

**Also fixed, found in passing while verifying the above:** a concurrent
session's own rename (`updateRightClickModeVisibility()` ->
`updateClickTriggerModeVisibility(p, extraSinglePoseKeys)`, generalizing
Right Click's Mode dropdown to every Click-family trigger group) missed
1 call site -- the ORIGINAL `rcMode` control's own `onChange`, still
calling the old, now-undefined function name. Threw a live
`ReferenceError` (caught by `safeRefreshSelectOptions()`'s own try/catch,
so contained -- but Right Click's own mode-specific rows silently stopped
toggling). Fixed by updating that one call site to the new signature.

**Verified directly** (bypassing the render loop, same "manually step
the update function" methodology as the previous 2 entries): triggered a
real Click Pose transition toward "ThumbsUp" and stepped `updateRenderOrder()`
with REAL elapsed time between calls (not a tight synchronous loop, which
would never show meaningful `progress` advancement) -- `thumbCurl`
progressed smoothly and monotonically (78.95 -> 54.69 -> 34.27 -> 11.37
-> -9.19 -> -26.86 -> -37, reaching the target and pausing), with no
snap-back to the idle default value at any intermediate frame.

Note: a concurrent session was actively committing to this same file
throughout this investigation (generalizing Mode/Tween-sequence support
from Right Click to every other Click-family trigger group) -- confirmed
via repeated `git diff` checks that its changes and this fix's own
changes never overlapped or conflicted.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=95` -> `?v=97` (an
intermediate `?v=96` was superseded mid-fix by this entry's own 2nd
change, same as the earlier `?v=91`/`?v=92` concurrent-bump note).

to a fresh forward start -- restarting the tween from scratch every

from that condition.

**Fixed: looping was disregarding the default pose. Added: a Loop Mode

`namedPoses` ONLY, excluding the default/anchor pose -- correct per an

initial default pose"), but exactly backwards from what's now being
asked. Fixed by pointing `loopPoses` at the SAME full sequence
(`dcHoldTween.poses` / `trig.tweenPoses`, anchor included) the forward
pass already plays, instead of a named-poses-only subset -- `lerpLoopSequence()`
itself needed no change at all, since it was always generic over

UNCHANGED on purpose: still divides by `namedPoses.length`, not the new

pong/triangle wave through the same pose sequence Loop wraps through --
bounces at each end instead of wrapping, delegating the actual pose lerp
back to `lerpTweenSequence()` (position/segments is always a valid `t`
in [0,1]) rather than duplicating its segment-index math. Its own
`+ segments` phase shift is baked in, so the caller's raw elapsed-
segments count (no `segments - 1` offset, unlike Loop) already starts
exactly where the forward pass ended and immediately reverses.
`updateClickHoldPoseForHand()`'s existing 'looping' phase now branches
on `cfg[${p}LoopMode]` to pick which lerp function to call, rather than
adding a whole separate phase.

just the one Mode dropdown the existing function already handles.

**Real bug caught and fixed before considering this done:** on a frame
where `elapsedSegments` overshoots past the true lap boundary before the
"lap complete" check runs (inherent to frame-rate-dependent stepping --
confirmed live, not hypothetical), Loop mode's hold froze at whatever
slightly-past-boundary INTERPOLATED value happened to be computed that
frame (observed: -64 instead of the intended exact boundary pose's -1),
not the clean boundary pose. Fixed by clamping the elapsed-segments count
used for the applied VALUE to exactly one lap (`Math.min(elapsedSegments,
segments)`), while still using the unclamped count to detect "lap
complete" -- so an overshoot is still detected correctly, it just no
longer bleeds into which pose gets frozen.

**Verified live via `window.__debug`:** confirmed the revert (a 1-named-
pose sequence no longer loops at all); confirmed Loop's hold now freezes

verifying it disappeared after the fix; Oscillate's own hold traced out
exactly "hold at end A -> flip -> play to end B -> hold at end B -> flip
-> play back to end A -> hold," direction flipping correctly every lap.

**Field Layout + Camera made per-device (Desktop/Mobile/Landscape); the
real "double click acting weird" bug root-caused and fixed.** Two direct
requests in one message.

**1. "Allow for separate field layout settings and camera settings for
Desktop and Mobile."** Investigated devPanel.js's own `perDevice`
mechanism first rather than assuming: `commit()`/`syncValue()` already
gate live `onChange` firing behind `editingDevice === realDeviceClass()`
generically (confirmed by reading their own source, not just their
comments) -- switching the PANEL's own tab to Mobile while genuinely on
a desktop browser previews/stores what Mobile will see WITHOUT touching
the current live scene, by design (matches how the built-in Dev Panel
chrome group's own perDevice fields already behave). This meant the fix
was purely additive: `perDevice: true` on every Field Layout control
(`fieldRows`/`fieldCols`/`rowSpacing`/`columnSpacing`/`handScale`/
`alternateRowOffset`/`progressiveRowOffset`/`useProgressiveOffset`) and
every live Camera control (`cameraX`/`Y`/`Z`/`cameraFov`/`cameraZoom`/
`lockCameraPan`/`lockCameraZoom`/`cameraMaxExtentsEnabled`) -- no
devPanel.js changes needed at all. Deliberately did NOT add
`defMobile`/`defLandscape` overrides (all 3 devices start identical,
cloned from `def`) -- there's no design brief for what Mobile's own
layout/camera SHOULD look like, so inventing numbers here would be
guessing; that's a live tuning decision for whoever opens the panel's
own Mobile tab from here on. `savedCameras` (the named-preset list)
deliberately stays SHARED, not per-device -- a preset is a reusable
recipe, not itself a spatial setting; making the list per-device would
just fragment one preset library into 3 for no benefit. **A real, pre-
existing gap noticed along the way, NOT fixed (out of scope for this
request):** devPanel.js's own generic per-group seeding
(`initDevPanel()`'s own `groups.forEach(...)`) only ever reads a
control's plain `def`, never `defMobile`/`defLandscape` -- those
overrides only actually work for the built-in Dev Panel chrome group's
own SEPARATE seeding code. Not a problem for this task (no divergent
defaults were needed), but worth knowing before any future project
tries to seed a project-supplied perDevice control with a genuinely
different Mobile default via `defMobile` -- it would be silently
ignored.

Verified live: switching to the Mobile tab and editing Rows (Count) to
7 left the live scene (255 hands, matching Desktop's own Rows=15)
completely unchanged; switching back to Desktop showed its own Rows
still at 15; switching back to Mobile showed 7 still there -- confirming
genuinely independent, persisted per-device state, not a shared value
with a cosmetic tab indicator.

**2. "Double click is still acting weird. I think its registering a
single click first, then when it realizes its double, it causes an
issue."** Investigated by reasoning through the click/dblclick debounce
logic first (concluded it should already be correct in isolation), then
confirmed that conclusion live by simulating a realistic double-click
via real dispatched `PointerEvent`s (not internal function calls, since
the bug could plausibly live in the event-handling layer itself) with
Click-Hold-Pose DISABLED -- confirmed clean, only `dblclick` fired, no
`click`. Re-enabling Click Hold-Pose and repeating with a NORMAL-speed
double-click (60ms presses) also stayed clean. **The actual root cause
only appeared with a SLOWER, still entirely ordinary press duration:**
a 200ms press (not an edge case -- a very plausible speed for a real
double-click's own first tap, especially via trackpad) drove
`chp`'s own phase into `'forward'` mid-press, confirmed via direct
per-hand phase sampling. Traced to 2 DIFFERENT constants serving what
should have been the SAME purpose: Click Hold-Pose's own Hold Confirm
Delay (150ms -- how long before its target pose becomes VISIBLE) vs.
`MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS` (500ms -- the separate threshold deciding
whether a release counts as a genuine hold, suppressing Click Pose/
Double-Click Pose's own trigger). Any press landing in the 150-500ms
gap crossed the "become visible" line without crossing the "count as a
hold" line -- chp's own target pose flashed on and reversed, entirely
independent of (and visually colliding with) whatever Click Pose/
Double-Click Pose went on to do once the debounce resolved. Exactly the
symptom described: something appears to fire and immediately get
corrected, right as the real double-click also resolves.

Fixed by raising Hold Confirm Delay's own default from 150 to 500ms
(matching `MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS` exactly, closing the gap rather than
just narrowing it) and its slider's own max from 500 to 1000 for tuning
headroom above that (both chp and rchp, via the shared
`makeClickHoldPoseGroup()` factory's own fallback default). **Also
corrected the same value in the live git-tracked settings**
(`data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json`, all 3 device profiles, both
chp and rchp) -- the user's own real saved value was `0`ms, an even more
exposed case than the old 150ms code default (chp could become visible
on literally the very next processed frame after any pointerdown at
all). A code-level default change alone would NOT have fixed this for
the user's own already-deployed settings, since an explicitly-saved
value always overrides a code default.

Verified live, twice: the exact same 200ms-press double-click that
originally reproduced the flash no longer moves `chp`'s own phase out
of `'idle'` at all with the fix in place -- confirmed via a live `cfg`
override first, then re-confirmed on a genuinely fresh page load
(`localStorage.clear()`, hard navigation) picking up the new 500ms code
default with no manual override needed. 0 console errors beyond the
pre-existing, unrelated `api/save-settings` 404s.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=103` -> `?v=104`.

2026-09-15  -  18:23:50 - 18:29:29 EDT (5m39s, measured; excludes doc/ETA
follow-up)

**Shift-click multi-select in devPanel.js's list-pickers, feeding an
auto-group "+Group."** Direct request: "within my Tween and Pose
selector, allow me to shift click to select multiple selections. When
one or multiple selections are made, and if i click the +group button,
It will automatically place the selected items in the new group."

Implemented as a genuine `devPanel.js` engine capability, not a
main.js-only patch -- `buildListPickerRow()`/`renderListPickerItemRow()`
are shared verbatim by EVERY list-picker in this file (Saved Poses,
Saved Tween Sequences, Saved Cameras, and any future one), so this
applies uniformly without per-control wiring, matching CLAUDE.md's own
file-map guidance on when a devPanel.js-level change (vs. main.js-only)
is the right call.

`entry.selectedItem` keeps its existing, unchanged single-item meaning
-- Use/Rename/Overwrite/Delete still only ever act on ONE item, since
nothing asked for those 4 to become batch operations (a destructive
action like Delete silently going multi-target was a real risk worth
avoiding without being asked). A new, separate `entry.multiSelected`
(a `Set`) tracks the shift-click range; only `+Group`'s own handler
reads it. Shift-click reads the CURRENT RENDERED `.dp-list-picker-row`
DOM order (not `entry.items` directly), so the range correctly spans
group boundaries the same way a user visually sees the flattened list --
no separate tree-flattening logic needed. `entry.selectAnchor` records
the last PLAIN (non-shift) click; a further shift-click always extends/
shrinks from that SAME anchor, not the most recent shift-click endpoint
-- standard file-manager convention. A plain click resets to a single
selection and moves the anchor. Visual state: the single active item
keeps its existing solid-fill highlight; the rest of a multi-selected
range gets a NEW, visually distinct accent-colored outline (added to
style.css) -- deliberately not identical styling, so it stays clear
which one row Delete/Rename/Use/Overwrite would act on vs. which rows
+Group would sweep up.

`+Group`'s own handler now checks `entry.multiSelected.size > 0`: with
a selection, the new group is created ALREADY populated with those
items (`.group` reassigned on each, re-parenting anything that was
previously in a different group -- "place" reads as move, not copy);
with nothing selected, falls back to the ORIGINAL behavior unchanged
(an empty group to drag items into later). Multi-select state is
invalidated the same way `selectedItem` already was: on an external
value refresh (Reset/tab-switch/remote restore, in `displayValue()`)
and on Delete (a stale reference to the just-deleted item is harmless
on its own, but OTHER items left over in a leftover `multiSelected`
could otherwise get silently auto-grouped by a LATER `+Group` click
with nothing visibly re-selected in between).

Verified live via dispatched `MouseEvent('click', {shiftKey})` on the
real row elements (a faithful simulation -- the listener reads
`e.shiftKey` off whatever Event it receives, so this exercises the
identical code path a real shift-click would): clicking Saved Poses row
0 then shift-clicking row 3 ("Point" -> "Fist") selected exactly the 4
rows in between; the single active item stayed "Fist" only; clicking
`+Group` moved exactly those 4 into a new group, leaving the other 7
poses completely untouched; a 2nd `+Group` click with nothing selected
still created a genuinely empty group ("New Group (2)", correctly
avoiding the name collision), confirming the original no-selection path
is unchanged; a subsequent plain click correctly collapsed back to
single-item selection with no lingering multi-highlight. Did NOT
separately re-verify Saved Tween Sequences -- it's the exact same
shared code path (not a per-control reimplementation), so a 2nd live
pass would have been redundant confirmation, not new information, per
this project's own usage-efficiency convention. 0 console errors beyond
the pre-existing, unrelated `api/save-settings` 404s.

**2. Separate direct request, arriving mid-task: "When I add a new

exposed a real gap: the auto-assigned group still rendered at the
BOTTOM, since `entry.pendingGroups` is unconditionally processed before/
after `entry.items` regardless of which group was actually newest --
array position within one queue says nothing about recency relative to
the OTHER queue. Fixed properly with a new `entry.groupOrder` (an
explicit creation-order priority list, prepended on EVERY group
creation regardless of which underlying array it touches, consulted as
a final re-sort pass in `renderListPickerRows()` over both queues'
combined output) -- kept in sync on rename/remove (same pattern
`pendingGroups` already uses) and cleared ENTIRELY the instant the user
manually drags any group (`syncListPickerFromDom()`), since past that
point the user's own new arrangement is the real source of truth and
re-imposing a stale creation-order priority on top of it would silently
undo their drag on the next unrelated re-render.

Verified live: re-ran the exact sequence that exposed the bug (2 empty
groups, then an auto-assigned 3rd) -- all 3 now render newest-first
regardless of kind; a 4th, genuinely empty group (nothing selected)
confirmed still lands at the very top too, with the originally-reported
symptom (new groups landing at the bottom) fully resolved for every
creation path. 0 console errors beyond the pre-existing, unrelated
`api/save-settings` 404s.

is still intact and correctly excludes retransition frames; wrist/whole-

samples, t=0 to t=1, using the user's own real Right-Click Tween

interpolates smoothly and monotonically, no reversal, no discontinuity,
confirmed analytically too (`curlBiasWeight()`'s quadratic interaction
with a linearly-changing curlBias does not introduce a sign reversal for
this specific pose pair). Leading hypothesis, NOT verified: an inherent
property of this project's linear-per-slider interpolation approach --
composing multiple SEQUENTIAL, non-commuting axis rotations (splay,
splay2, curl, base/mid/tip-only curl, tip-twist) can produce a visually
"off" intermediate 3D orientation even when every individual contributing
value moves smoothly, most likely to be visible on ring specifically
since `splayRing` swings the most dramatically of any single value in
the traced case (-34 -> 37, a full sign crossing). Live visual
confirmation was NOT obtained this round -- this session's own browser
tool's screenshot-after-JS-mutation path showed a stale/unchanged frame
despite a real `composer.render()` call, a NEW variant of this project's
own already-documented rendering-pipeline unreliability gotcha, not
chased further given time already spent. Left open -- see
PROJECT_PROGRESS.md for the standing status.

**2. Added a "Clear" button to the Mouse Tracking Log widget itself**
(`buildMouseTrackingLogWidget()`), next to its existing Copy/Save
buttons -- direct request. A separate `clearMouseLogBtn` DEV_GROUPS
control already existed elsewhere in the panel calling the same
`clearMouseTrackingLog()`, but not here, where it's actually convenient;

**3. "Why does the dev panel use the Landscape tab for desktop, instead
of the desktop tab?" -- ANSWERED, no fix applied (a shared cross-project
engine file, needs approval first).** `devPanel.js`'s own `realDeviceClass()`
classifies purely by viewport size: Desktop only when BOTH
`window.innerWidth` and `innerHeight` are >=768px; below that, Landscape
if wider than tall, else Mobile -- no touch-capability check at all. The
user's own most recent Mouse Tracking Log showed "Viewport (start):
1536x730" -- 730 < 768, so a real desktop browser window that simply
isn't tall enough (an unmaximized window, limited screen real estate,
browser chrome eating into the viewport) gets misclassified as a
landscape-orientation touchscreen device. A real fix (checking
`navigator.maxTouchPoints`/`'ontouchstart' in window` to tell a genuine
touchscreen apart from a short non-touch desktop window) would change
`devPanel.js`, the generic engine reused near-verbatim across projects
per this project's own CLAUDE.md file-map note ("do not fork it") --
surfaced to the user rather than silently changed, since it affects
every project sharing this engine, not just this one.

state machine (Click Pose, Click-Hold-Pose, Right Click, Double Click,
Double Click Hold, the new Pose Preview "Run") exactly where it is,
resuming from there rather than jumping forward by the paused duration.
Mechanism: a `nowVirtual()` "virtual clock" (`performance.now() -
pauseOffsetMs`) that every ANIMATION-timing call site in this file now
reads instead of raw `performance.now()` (trigger start-times AND the
per-frame elapsed-time comparisons against them) -- `animate()` itself
skips `updateCursorTarget()`/the cursor-tracking rotation step/
`updateRenderOrder()` entirely while paused (freezing the visual result),
and `pauseOffsetMs` absorbs the real paused duration on resume so the
very next elapsed-time calculation is unaffected by the gap. Deliberately
left UNTOUCHED (still raw `performance.now()`): the Mouse Tracking Log's
own timestamps and the click-vs-hold/double-click gesture-detection
debounce windows -- gesture detection isn't animation state and should
keep reflecting real wall-clock time regardless of pause. Camera
navigation (OrbitControls damping/pan-extent enforcement) deliberately
keeps running while paused, so the user can still look around.
Verified live via `window.__debug.updateRenderOrder()` called directly
(bypassing `requestAnimationFrame`, which this session's own browser
tool's JS-exec path does not reliably fire -- a known, already-documented
gotcha in this project's own CLAUDE.md): triggered a Click-Hold-Pose with
a 5000ms transition speed, sampled `curlIndex` mid-transition (96),
clicked PAUSE, waited a REAL 1500ms, clicked RESUME, sampled again
immediately -- `curlIndex` was still exactly 96 (delta 0), confirming the
1500ms real gap was correctly absorbed rather than counted toward the
transition's own elapsed time. A separate control run (no pause) over an
equivalent real interval showed the value actually progressing (96 -> 0
on a different field, confirming the state machine isn't simply stuck).

`.dp-pose-preview-canvas`'s own CSS (`style.css`) also updated -- dropped
its old fixed `aspect-ratio: 1/1` (a leftover from the always-square
embedded-canvas era), since the floating panel is now independently
resizable in both dimensions.

**1. Interrupting a mid-transition hand with a NEW trigger no longer
snaps it to a stale default/trigger-time snapshot.** Direct request:
"I want them to continue their existing transition until it gets
interrupted by the new one... the ones that arent triggered yet will
continue their first tween. When they do get interrupted... they will
smoothly begin transitioning from their current position instead of the
default position." Root cause (both Click-Hold-Pose and Click-Pose
families): a hand's own distance-based delay only ever delayed when its

whatever phase/values it was already running) happened immediately,
synchronously, for every hand at once, using one SHARED trigger-time
snapshot (effectively "the default pose"). Rebuilt into a genuine

only ARM a per-hand pending claim (computed once, per-hand, from the

over once THAT hand's own delay has genuinely elapsed, reading its FROM
value fresh, right then, from `hand._lastPoseValues` (a new field,
stashed by `applyPoseValuesToHand()` -- the ONE place every trigger
family funnels through -- whenever `hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame`, an
already-existing field, confirms the hand was genuinely active as of
last frame). Until commit, `chp.phase`/`cp.phase` is untouched, so
whatever WAS running keeps running, completely unaffected -- literally
"continue their first tween." dcHold's own twice-confirmed "always
starts from default" exception is preserved, but now only for a
genuinely-idle hand -- an interrupted dcHold also continues smoothly
like every other trigger, since that's what this new request asks for.
Live-verified end to end (self-contained polling script, avoiding the
cross-call tool-latency confound a multi-call test hit first): chp
triggered toward "Fist" (curlIndex 0->96) with a forced idle-hand
FROM=0; mid-flight (curlIndex=17) interrupted with rchp -- chp's own
phase stayed 'forward' and its curlIndex kept climbing (34->39->58->75->
92->96) entirely on its own while rchp's own claim was still pending;
once rchp's own delay elapsed, its anchor was exactly 96 (chp's own
fully-settled value), not a default/zero jump. Click-Pose family
(click/dblclick) confirmed structurally identical (deferred pendingClaimAt,
phase preserved through an interrupt), reusing the exact same proven
mechanism.

**ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED a MAJOR pre-existing devPanel.js bug: real
visitors of the live production URL (no `?dev=1`) have NEVER actually
seen anything saved via the dev panel's own Save/Sync button --
ever.** Direct user report: "How come when I open .../?dev=1 on mobile
it looks fine. But on .../ it's still old settings. I used incognito so
it's not cache." Confirmed by direct code read: `initDevPanel()` had a
hard `if (!DEV_MODE) return cfg` immediately after seeding `cfg` with
pure per-control code `def:` values -- the ONLY code path that ever
restores REAL saved/remote values (`resetSettings()`) lives much later,
entirely inside the DEV_MODE-only panel-building branch this early
return skips past. `DEV_MODE` itself requires `?dev=1`, `localhost`,
`127.0.0.1`, or `file:` -- a normal visitor on the bare production URL
satisfies none of these, so `resetSettings()` (and therefore the whole
Save/Sync feature's own real purpose) has NEVER actually run for them,
on any project ever built on this shared engine, not just this one.
Fixed with a new `restoreValuesForEveryVisitor()`, called unconditionally
BEFORE the `DEV_MODE` check -- reuses `applyStoredValues()` directly
(confirmed safe to call with no panel DOM built yet: every DOM-touching
step inside it already no-ops gracefully on a control with no
corresponding row, same tolerance `buildRow()`'s own 'select' branch
already documents), restoring cfg/store and firing the new `onRestore`
hook (see this file's own earlier entry) for every visitor. Deliberately
skips row order and panel geometry (both meaningless without a panel) --
a DEV_MODE visitor still separately gets those, plus a harmless,
accepted-as-a-tradeoff redundant 2nd value-restore fetch, via the
existing `resetSettings()` later in the normal panel-setup path.
Live-verified (localhost is unconditionally DEV_MODE per its own
definition, so the `!DEV_MODE` branch itself can't be exercised locally
without a non-localhost/non-file test domain -- verified the RESTORE
MECHANISM itself instead, from its new earlier call site): mocked a
delayed remote-settings response exactly as the prior entry's own test
did -- `cfg`/`poseDefaultValues` both correctly went from stale code
defaults to the real mocked values once the fetch resolved, and the
panel + field still rendered correctly afterward (screenshot-confirmed:
hands showing the correct curled/posed default, panel fully functional).
The actual "does a non-dev visitor now get real settings" behavior still
needs confirming on the real production URL once this deploys, since
that specific code path can't be reached on `localhost`.

2026-09-16  -  02:42:59 - 02:56:55 EDT (13m56s, measured)

**Added Triple-Click / Triple-Click Hold / Quadruple-Click / Quadruple-
Click Hold -- 4 new trigger groups, direct request.** Extends both of
this project's already-generalized trigger families (the fire-and-
forget Click Pose family and the hold-based Click-Hold-Pose family,
both driven by a shared, per-key-generic `makeClickPoseGroup()`/
`makeClickHoldPoseGroup()` + `CLICK_POSE_KEYS`/`CLICK_HOLD_KEYS` array
architecture) from 2-deep (click/dblclick, dcHold) to 4-deep. Most of
the actual state-machine work (deferred-claim interruption continuity,
Tween mode, Retransition settings, everything from this file's earlier
2026-09-16 entries) came for free from that existing genericity -- the
real work was in the 2 gesture-DETECTION layers:

**1. `clickHoldPoseTriggers`/`clickPoseTriggers`** (previously 3- and
3-entry hand-written object literals) **converted to `Object.fromEntries()`
generated from their own KEYS array** -- both now automatically cover
any future addition, not just this one.

**2. Click-count debounce dispatcher** generalized from a hardcoded
"1 -> click, 2+ -> dblclick" `if`/`else if` into a `CLICK_COUNT_CHAIN_KEYS`
lookup table (`['click','dblclick','tripleClick','quadClick']`), the
last entry absorbing any count beyond its own (a 5th+ click still just
re-fires quadClick).

**3. dcHold's own gesture-detection (a binary "was the last release
clean" flag) generalized into a running chain count** (`clickHoldChainCount`)
via a parallel `CLICK_HOLD_CHAIN_KEYS` lookup, so a 3rd or 4th press --
each preceded by its own unbroken run of quick clean releases -- can
also become a hold (tripleClickHold/quadClickHold respectively). **A
real bug was caught here BEFORE shipping, via live testing (not a user
report):** dcHold's own design starts a hold IMMEDIATELY on the
qualifying press (an already-established, deliberate behavior, not
something this round changed), so the 2nd press of a still-building
3-or-4-click chain ALSO briefly pulses dcHold the instant it's pressed.
The first version of the release handler treated "a chain hold was
active" as proof the chain was OVER, resetting the chain count on every
intermediate click -- confirmed live: a click-click-HOLD sequence meant
to become tripleClickHold never committed even after 40 polling ticks
(~2.4s), because the 2nd click's own brief dcHold pulse reset the count
to 0 before the 3rd press could ever see chainCount===2. Fixed by
deciding "does the chain continue" purely from that release's own
heldMs/moved classification (same as a normal click), decoupled from
whether a hold happened to be pulsing on that exact press.

Also generalized 2 other dcHold-specific hardcodes that would otherwise
have silently missed the 2 new triggers: `endClickHoldPose()`'s own
camera-pan-lock restore condition (used to check only chp/rchp/dcHold's
own `.active`, now iterates all of `CLICK_HOLD_KEYS`) and the `blur`
handler's own "release everything, never get stuck" safety net (same
generalization, plus now also resets the click-hold chain's own
tracking state). `tripleClickHold`/`quadClickHold` inherit dcHold's own
twice-confirmed "Tween mode always starts from default" exception
(extended from a single `p === 'dcHold'` check to all 3 multi-click-
hold-family keys) rather than chp/rchp's plain-single-press behavior,
since they're the same gesture family, just a longer chain.

New groups ship with `enabled: false` by default (unlike click/dblclick,
which ship enabled) -- a not-yet-tuned trigger shouldn't start firing on
every visitor's 3rd/4th click the instant this ships; same convention
chp/rchp/dcHold already established.

Verified live via dispatched PointerEvents (confirmed reaching real
window-level listeners correctly, via a working chp sanity check, after
an initial false negative traced to under-waiting for a hold's own
distance-based commit delay, not a real bug): 1/2/3/4 quick clicks each
fired exactly their own trigger (click/dblclick/tripleClick/quadClick),
no cross-contamination; dcHold (regression) still commits correctly
after the chain-detection rewrite; tripleClickHold/quadClickHold both
commit correctly on their own qualifying held press, with the correct
target-pose anchor; a plain (no-hold) 3-click sequence correctly fires
only `tripleClick`, with the hold family's own brief pulses on clicks 2
and 3 settling cleanly back to idle rather than getting stuck. 0 console
errors throughout.

Cache-buster bumps: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=114` -> `?v=115` (an
intermediate `?v=114` was cut mid-testing, after the chain-count bug
above was caught and fixed before ever being shipped).

2026-09-16  -  03:53:31 - 04:04:27 EDT (10m56s, measured)

**ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED a severe startup-lag regression, direct user
report: "way way way worse than ever before... its mainly laggy on
startup... the hands are getting into position and setting their
crops... then once it reaches some equilibrium state then the app is
fine" (and interacting DURING that settle makes it worse still).**
Self-inflicted by this SAME session's own earlier fix (2 entries above):
`restoreValuesForEveryVisitor()` now correctly fetches real saved
settings for EVERY visitor, not just `?dev=1` ones -- but that fetch is
a real network round-trip, while the GLTFLoader callback (`rebuildField()`,
building every hand from whatever `cfg` currently holds) only needs a
local static asset and almost always finishes first. Net effect: the
field built and revealed ONCE with CODE DEFAULTS, then a moment later
the real settings landed and every affected control's own onChange
fired (`rebuildField()` again for fieldRows/fieldCols, plus every other
Pose/Camera/Lighting control's own reflow) -- visibly rebuilding/
repositioning/recropping the entire field in front of the user, and
genuinely doing the expensive field-build work twice.

Fixed with a startup gate (`tryStartField()`): the loading screen now
stays up until BOTH the model has loaded (`modelMeasurementsReady`) AND
settings restore has actually landed (`startupSettingsReady`, set from
`onRestore`), so the field builds exactly once, using the real settings.
A bounded 6000ms fallback timeout still starts the field on whatever
`cfg` currently holds if restore is taking unreasonably long, so a
visitor on a genuinely broken connection is never left staring at
"Loading hands..." forever. 6000ms is a judgment call, but sized against
a real measurement: a live production fetch to `/api/save-settings`
(warm, ~297KB response) measured 332.6ms total (291.4ms fetch + 41.2ms
JSON parse) -- roughly 18x headroom over that.

**Also fixed a related gap in devPanel.js's own `onRestore` hook**
(added earlier this session): the localStorage-restore branch only
called `onRestore` when something was actually FOUND to restore --
meaning a brand-new visitor with empty localStorage (no remoteSave
configured) would never fire it at all, and a host using it to gate
startup (like the fix above) would hang until its own fallback timeout,
every single time, for every first-time visitor. Now fires regardless,
since "the restore attempt concluded" (including "there was genuinely
nothing to restore") is the correct signal, not "real data was found."

**A real testing pitfall hit and resolved while verifying this:**
navigating and then checking state via 2 SEPARATE tool calls
(`navigate` then `javascript_exec`) can have enough real wall-clock gap
between them to itself exceed the 6000ms fallback timeout, making a
correctly-gated startup look broken (revealing with defaults immediately,
then visibly rebuilding once a slow test mock finally resolved) --
purely a test-harness artifact, not a real bug, confirmed by re-running
the same test via `browser_batch` (bundling navigate + the check script
into one round-trip, eliminating the gap): the loading screen correctly
stayed up through 6 consecutive 100ms polls while `modelMeasurementsReady`
was already true but `startupSettingsReady` wasn't yet, then revealed
in one shot with the correct hand count (75, matching the mocked
settings) the instant both flags flipped, and never changed again over
a further 1.5s observation window. The timeout-fallback path was also
verified independently (no mock at all -- real local 404s): the field
correctly revealed with code-default values once the 6s timeout elapsed,
rather than hanging forever.

2026-09-16  -  04:08:49 - 04:14:09 EDT (5m20s, measured)

**The settings-restore-race fix (2 entries above) was NOT the real
startup-lag cause -- direct user report after that deploy: "nope same
issue. i still see the load screen and its still slow," then the actual
disambiguating clue: "on mobile it doesnt lag, but the startup animation
thing is still happening... its smooth though."** That means the
visible "hands getting into position" IS the expected, correct cursor-
tracking damping animation (`hand.wrapper.quaternion.slerp` toward the
cursor, `cfg.trackingDamping`) -- it happens on BOTH platforms, and
isn't itself a bug. The real bug: desktop drops frames specifically
WHILE that normal animation is playing; mobile doesn't.

**Root cause: lazy WebGL shader compilation, spread across the first
few visible/animating frames instead of happening once, upfront.**
Every field hand gets its own CLONED material (`createToonMaterial()`'s
own comment: required for per-hand wrist-clip planes; a shared
`customProgramCacheKey` was tried and reverted for a real, unrelated
correctness bug on 2026-09-14) -- and WebGL only compiles a material's
shader program the first time it's actually DRAWN, not when it's
created. With up to ~240+ separate per-hand materials, that means up to
~240+ separate GPU program compiles were silently happening across
whichever frames first drew each hand -- exactly the visible "settling"
window, since that's also when cursor-tracking is still converging.
Raw shader-compile speed is genuinely GPU/driver-dependent and known to
vary far more between a desktop's own GPU/driver and a modern phone's
than the actual CPU/JS work does, plausibly explaining the platform
split on its own.

**Fixed with `renderer.compile(scene, camera)`** (three.js's own
standard fix for exactly this class of stutter), called inside
`tryStartField()` right after `rebuildField()`, forcing every material
to compile HERE, synchronously, behind the loading screen -- moving the
entire compile cost to one controlled point instead of smearing it
across the first several visible, actively-animating frames. Not
claimed as a guaranteed full fix for the platform gap itself (raw GPU
compile speed isn't something this app can change), but it directly
addresses the actual mechanism (compiling during the visible animation)
regardless of how slow compiling turns out to be on any given machine.

A temporary timing log (`console.log('[startup] renderer.compile()...')`)
was added and DELIBERATELY LEFT IN this deploy -- measured 34.3ms for
255 hands in this session's own test environment (not representative of
the user's own reported hardware, which is the whole point of shipping
it: the user checking their own browser console gives a REAL number for
their actual GPU/driver, the only way to confirm or refute the
hypothesis for their specific case). Plan to remove it in a follow-up
once confirmed either way.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=118` -> `?v=119`.

to be the cause, though not proof positive on its own.

**Rather than propose a 3rd guess, added a real frame-cost profiler**
(`__frameProfiler`, inside `animate()`) -- breaks down where per-frame
time is ACTUALLY going: `updateRenderOrder()` (per-hand trigger/idle-
repose logic) vs. `composer.render()` (GPU draw submission) vs.
everything else, logged once per second as a rolling-window average +
instantaneous FPS to the console (`[frame-profile] fps=... avgUpdateRenderOrder=...ms
avgComposerRender=...ms hands=N over F frames`). Verified live (local):
correctly logs sane numbers (~100+fps, sub-ms costs, matching the local
test's empty/default settings). Meant to be temporary -- removed once
the real bottleneck is identified from the user's own reported numbers,
which will directly show whether the cost is in this app's own JS
(updateRenderOrder), the GPU submission call itself (composer.render,
in which case it's likely hardware/driver-bound, not something this
codebase can fix), or neither (pointing further afield, e.g. a genuine
dropped-frame/GC-pause pattern this profiler wouldn't directly capture).

very first real draw call -- consistent with SOME residual first-frame
GPU cost even after `renderer.compile()`, though small next to what
follows) then a SUSTAINED, CONTINUOUS steady state across 15+ one-
second windows: `fps≈4.5-4.9`, `avgUpdateRenderOrder≈25-42ms`,
`avgComposerRender≈8-16ms` -- i.e. this app's own per-hand JavaScript
logic (NOT the GPU draw call) is the dominant, ONGOING cost, roughly
2-4x larger than rendering itself, every single frame, not just at
startup.

Traced to "Responsive Wrist Splay" (`cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled`,
confirmed `true` on both desktop AND mobile in the live settings data):
`updateRenderOrder()`'s own `needsIdleRepose` gate
(`!overridden && (cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled || ...)`) means the
FULL per-hand repose (wrist bone + all 5 fingers, world-axis-corrected
bone math via `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`) runs EVERY FRAME, for EVERY
hand, whenever this master toggle is on -- REGARDLESS of whether
"Reactive" mode is actually active. This is NOT a new regression from
this session's own work: it's the SAME cost already measured and
documented on 2026-09-15 ("~38ms/frame at 255 hands, the actual lag
bottleneck, not rendering itself (~11ms)") -- the user's own new
numbers (25-42ms at 240 hands) land squarely in that same range,
confirming it's the identical, pre-existing, already-known-expensive
code path, not something freshly broken.

Investigated whether the per-hand math itself has an easy win: the hot
functions (`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`, `applyCurlToSkeleton()`,
`applyWristPoseToSkeleton()`) already reuse module-level scratch
Vector3/Quaternion objects throughout (no obvious per-call allocation
churn) and `computeResponsiveWristSplayDeg()`'s own curve evaluation
reads an already-parsed, cached curve (no JSON.parse in the hot path) --
this is already a reasonably allocation-conscious implementation, not
an obvious quick fix. The likely remaining cost is the sheer volume of
world-space quaternion math itself (`getWorldQuaternion()`/
`getWorldPosition()` walk each bone's own ancestor chain) x 5 fingers x
several joints x every hand, every frame -- genuinely CPU-bound work
that scales with hand count, not a wasteful pattern to trivially strip
out.

**Presented to the user as a real tradeoff decision (per this project's
own §0a -- not unilaterally disabled):** turning off "Responsive Wrist
Splay" should immediately and dramatically cut `avgUpdateRenderOrder`
back toward ~0 (idle-repose would then only run on the rare "just
finished a transition" frame, not continuously), at the cost of losing
the live cursor-distance-reactive wrist splay effect. Alternative: keep
the feature and pursue a deeper optimization pass on the per-hand repose
math itself (uncertain how much headroom actually exists, given the
already-conservative allocation pattern found above). Awaiting the
user's choice.

2026-09-16  -  04:37:07 - 04:52:22 EDT (15m15s, measured)

**Responsive Wrist Splay: "something in between" fix -- staggers the
confirmed per-frame repose cost across N frames instead of disabling
the feature or leaving it at full per-frame cost.** Direct follow-up to
the prior entry's DIAGNOSIS COMPLETE finding; the user was asked to
choose between disabling the feature, optimizing the math, or "something
in between -- you tell me." Implemented: a new dev-panel slider,
`wristSplayReposeStagger` ("Reactive Splay Update Stagger (Frames,
1=Off)", Responsive Wrist Splay group, min 1, max 8, def 4). Every hand
still reacts to the cursor; each hand's own reactive-splay repose now
only runs on 1 of every N frames (round-robin by field index) instead of
every hand every frame -- spreading the SAME total work across more
frames rather than doing all of it every frame, cutting the per-frame
cost by roughly the stagger factor. A new module-level frame counter
(`idleReposeFrameCounter`, incremented once per `updateRenderOrder()`
call) plus each hand's own array index (`i`) determine whose "turn" it
is this frame: `(idleReposeFrameCounter % staggerCount) === (i %
staggerCount)`.

Deliberately preserves the 2 OTHER reasons `needsIdleRepose` can fire --
`hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame` (post-transition resync) and
`!hand.everReposed` (first-ever repose) -- as unconditional/immediate,
NOT staggered. Both are exactly the 2 conditions the prior 2026-09-15
"CORRECTED"/"CORRECTED AGAIN" entries fixed real production bugs around
(stale bind-pose on fresh load; click poses stomped back to default) --
staggering either of them would risk reintroducing one of those same
bugs. New gate: `needsIdleRepose = !overridden &&
((cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled && isThisHandsStaggerTurn) ||
hand._wasOverriddenLastFrame || !hand.everReposed)`.

**Bug found and fixed during this same pass (via live testing):** the
new `idleReposeFrameCounter` was originally declared with `let` directly
above `updateRenderOrder()`'s own definition, far down the file. But
`animate()` -- defined and called earlier in the file, at its own `animate()`
kickoff line -- calls `updateRenderOrder()` synchronously on that very
first call, before the file's own top-to-bottom evaluation ever reaches
the `let` declaration further down -- a textbook temporal-dead-zone
crash (`Cannot access 'idleReposeFrameCounter' before initialization`),
confirmed live via a real browser console error on first test load.
Fixed by moving the declaration up to the top of the file, alongside the
other early module-level state (`let modelRoot = null` / `modelLoaded`,
~line 194) -- well before `animate()`'s own first synchronous call, so
the binding is long past its TDZ by the time `updateRenderOrder()` is
ever reached.

**Verification: PARTIAL, environmentally limited -- disclosed
honestly, not glossed over.** One full live load (dev panel + field)
completed successfully BEFORE the TDZ bug was introduced/fixed,
confirming `wristSplayReposeStagger` seeds correctly to its def (4) via
`cfg` and the field still builds normally (hands=255). After the TDZ
fix, `node --check` confirms valid syntax, and a direct fetch of
`src/main.js` standalone (bypassing the full app) loads completely and
correctly -- ruling out any transfer/syntax corruption in the fixed
file. However, every subsequent FULL-APP load attempt (3 different
server processes, 2 different ports, both fresh tabs and reused ones)
hit `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` specifically on `main.js` when loaded as
part of the full page (never when fetched standalone) -- consistent
with this project's own previously-documented local-test-server flakiness
(a single-threaded Python `http.server`, choked by this exact session's
own accumulated settings-fetch retry loops from repeated navigations),
not a symptom of the code change itself. Did not keep escalating this
(per CLAUDE.md §0b -- diminishing-return environmental debugging); the
TDZ fix itself is a minimal, well-understood JS ordering change (move a
`let` declaration earlier in the same module, zero logic change), and
the feature logic it doesn't touch was already confirmed working in the
one clean load obtained. Recommend the user confirm on their own device
after this deploys.

Cache-buster bump: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=121` -> `?v=122`.

2026-09-16  -  04:53:48 - 05:09:55 EDT (16m7s, measured)

**Triple/quad-click "registers as double-click first, no confirm delay"
-- root-caused via direct settings inspection, 2 fixes shipped, both
confirmed by the user's own answers before implementing.** Direct report
("when i do triple or quad click. it registers and trigges double click
first. I dont see a doubel clcik confirm delay").

Found via `data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json`: `tripleClickEnabled`/
`quadClickEnabled` (the fire-and-forget click-pose tiers) were BOTH
`false` on all 3 devices, while `tripleClickHoldEnabled`/
`quadClickHoldEnabled` (the separate HOLD-based tiers) were `true` --
these are 2 independent toggles per tier, not one. `triggerClickPose()`
(`main.js` ~4408) does `if (!cfg[`${p}Enabled`]) return` -- with the
plain tier off, a genuine triple-click there was a silent no-op
regardless of timing. Separately, the shared `MOUSE_LOG_MULTICLICK_MS`
debounce window (350ms) governs how long the fire-and-forget click
counter waits for another click before locking in whatever count it has
so far; a real 3-click gesture has more gaps that can exceed a short
window than a 2-click one does, so a slightly slow gap between click 2
and 3 gets read as "sequence over," visibly firing `dblclick`'s own pose
(enabled) with no distinguishable "confirming..." UI -- then the 3rd
click starts a brand-new, separate sequence on its own. Both mechanisms
plausibly combine to produce exactly the reported symptom.

Confirmed with the user via 2 direct questions before touching anything:
(1) enable `tripleClickEnabled`/`quadClickEnabled` -- yes; (2) widen the
debounce window -- yes. Fixed: flipped `tripleClickEnabled`/
`quadClickEnabled` to `true` across all 3 device blocks directly in
`dev-panel-settings.json` (same precedent as the earlier
`chpHoldConfirmMs`/`rchpHoldConfirmMs` correction), minimal diff
verified via `git diff` (exactly the 6 intended lines, nothing else
touched). `MOUSE_LOG_MULTICLICK_MS` widened 350ms -> 450ms (`main.js`
~173) -- a disclosed judgment call, not a measured value (CLAUDE.md
§0c), since no click-timing telemetry exists to derive an exact number
from; a modest, reversible increase. This constant is shared by 3
systems (the fire-and-forget click-pose debounce, the click-hold chain
continuation window, and the Mouse Tracking Log's own multi-click
classification) by original design, so all 3 get slightly more forgiving
timing from this one change.

**Cursor Tracking settings: made independently adjustable per Desktop/
Mobile/Landscape.** Direct request ("for cursor tracking settings, let
mobile and desktop have different settings"). Added `perDevice: true` to
all 6 Cursor Tracking controls (`trackingEnabled`, `trackingDamping`,
`targetDepthFactor`, `showTargetMarker`, `palmFacesCursor`,
`palmFaceRotationOffset`) -- the same devPanel.js mechanism (§12f)
already used throughout this file (Field Layout's spacing/offset
controls, every Dev Panel chrome control) for exactly this purpose;

already (no divergence to reconcile), so this is a safe, non-disruptive
transition -- each tab keeps showing its current value, now independently

no touch-input equivalent -- corrected that note in place (not deleted)
per this project's own established convention, distinguishing "the
interaction has no touch counterpart" (still true) from "its tuning
should be shared across devices" (no longer what's wanted).

New "Loading Preview" dev-panel group (placed before "Pose"):

none`) so a disabled/not-yet-built preview never shows as an empty box --
`buildLoadingPreview()` only reveals it when actually building.

**Follow-up 1 of 2 for the same "triple/quad-click doesn't work" thread
this session: "quad click functions dont work even when turned on."**
Root cause was NOT the enable toggles this time (those were already
fixed and confirmed `true`) -- it was that `tripleClickTweenSelector`
and `quadClickTargetPose` were both empty strings in the live settings.
`updateClickPoseForHand()`'s own commit step ([main.js:4543] Tween mode,
[main.js:4546-4547] Single Pose mode) deliberately abandons a hand's

were firing and running their whole state machine every time, just with
nothing to transition to, indistinguishable from "not working." This is
a live-settings/configuration gap, not a code bug -- reported the finding
and left the actual pose/sequence choice to the user rather than

**Follow-up 2 of 2, same day: "when i try quad click. it triggers triple
click first."** Investigated whether the click-hold-chain's own
`lastPointerupWasHoldRelease` flag (used to suppress a click firing on
top of a chp/rchp hold-release) could be cross-suppressing a click in
the SEPARATE fire-and-forget count -- ruled out directly: that flag only
ever reads `clickHoldPoseTriggers.chp`/`.rchp`, never dcHold/
tripleClickHold/quadClickHold, and neither pointerdown/pointerup
listener in the click-hold-chain family calls preventDefault/
stopPropagation, so it can't be silently eating an event meant for the
fire-and-forget listener either. That leaves the ALREADY-SUSPECTED
timing explanation from the prior round's own entry, now confirmed by
this new report: the shared multi-click debounce window (widened
350ms -> 450ms that round) still isn't forgiving enough for a genuine
4-click attempt specifically -- a 4-click gesture has 3 separate gaps
that can each exceed the window (vs. a double-click's 1), and a 4th
click is inherently a less-practiced human gesture than a 2nd, more
likely to run long. Since this SAME constant has now needed re-tuning
twice in one day, converted it from a hardcoded value
(`MOUSE_LOG_MULTICLICK_MS`) into an actual dev-panel slider
(`cfg.multiClickWindowMs`, Debug group, "Multi-Click Window (Ms) --
Click/Hold Chains + Mouse Log," min 200/max 1200/def 600) instead of
bumping the literal a 3rd time -- per CLAUDE.md §12n ("feel/response
curves are sliders, not constants"), so any further tuning is a live
slider drag, not another code round-trip. 600ms default is this round's
own judgment call (§0c -- no real click-timing telemetry exists), a
further modest increase over 450ms. All 3 real usage sites (Click Pose's
own debounce, the Click-Hold chain's continuation window, the Mouse
Tracking Log's own multi-click classification) now read the same live
`cfg.multiClickWindowMs` instead of the removed constant -- deliberately
still ONE shared setting, not 3 separately-tuned ones, matching this
value's own original "for consistency" intent. New key, no existing
live-settings value to migrate -- `def: 600` applies automatically on
next restore for anyone who hasn't already saved a value for it.

correct label; `cfg.multiClickWindowMs` seeds to `600` on a fresh load;
`node --check` passes; no new console errors. Did not independently
re-verify the actual multi-click TIMING behavior live this round (the
underlying debounce logic itself is unchanged, only its source value
moved from a constant to a cfg read) -- recommend the user try a genuine
quad-click again after this deploys, and adjust the new slider directly
if 600ms still isn't quite enough.
