2026-09-17  -  03:05:44 - 03:18:22 EDT (12m38s, measured)

**"Triple-click-hold works, quad-click-hold doesn't" -- root cause found
via direct settings inspection, not live reproduction.** Direct report.
Read every place `tripleClickHold`/`quadClickHold` are handled
(initialization, the per-frame update loop, trigger registration, the
`CLICK_HOLD_CHAIN_KEYS` indexing) and confirmed both are byte-for-byte
symmetric in code -- no special-casing explains an asymmetry there.
Attempted live reproduction via synthetic `PointerEvent` dispatch in a
test page; this hit an unexplained wall (even the simplest case,
double-click-hold, never armed via synthetic events, while calling the
same internal functions directly worked fine every time) -- synthetic
`dispatchEvent()` apparently doesn't drive this code path the way a real
mouse does in this environment. Abandoned that approach rather than keep
spending time on an unreliable test method (CLAUDE.md §0b).

Found the real cause instead by comparing live settings directly:
`tripleClickHoldHoldConfirmMs = 500` (correct) vs.
`quadClickHoldHoldConfirmMs = 310` (WRONG) -- violating a documented
invariant from this project's own 2026-09-15 fix for the identical bug
class on chp/rchp (`makeClickHoldPoseGroup()`'s own `${p}HoldConfirmMs`
control comment): a hold's own confirm delay must be >=
`MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS` (500ms, the threshold deciding whether a
release counts as a genuine hold vs. a clean click continuing the
multi-click chain). Below that, a press long enough to make the pose
BECOME VISIBLE isn't necessarily long enough to be CLASSIFIED as a hold
on release -- the pose can flash on then immediately reverse, and/or the
click-hold chain can misread a real hold as "just another click,
continue the chain," never actually committing quadClickHold. Fixed:
`quadClickHoldHoldConfirmMs` corrected to `500` across all 3 device
blocks in `dev-panel-settings.json` (matching tripleClickHold's own
already-correct value), minimal diff confirmed via `git diff` (exactly
3 lines). Added a documenting comment at `MOUSE_LOG_HELD_DRAG_MS`'s own
declaration (`main.js` ~198) explaining this invariant explicitly, since
it isn't visually obvious from the dev panel UI and has now caused the
same bug class twice -- a future re-tuning of any `${p}HoldConfirmMs`
slider should check it against this value.

**New feature: saveable/named Lighting presets ("also make it so i can
save separate light setttings" / "save, use, delete etc").** Mirrors
Camera's own `savedCameras` list-picker exactly -- same shape
(captureCurrent/onUse, applied directly to the live scene, no preview
concept), same generic devPanel.js list-picker machinery already proven
by 3+ other controls in this file (savedPoses/savedCameras/
savedTweenSequences). New `savedLighting` control (Lighting group, after
the 8 existing light sliders/colors); `LIGHTING_PRESET_KEYS` (all 8:
keyAzimuth/keyElevation/keyTargetHeight/keyIntensity/keyColor/
ambientIntensity/ambientSkyColor/ambientGroundColor);
`captureLightingPreset()`/`applyLightingPreset()` (placed right after
the Camera preset block, same file location pattern). No "Default"
button injected (unlike Pose's/Camera's own) -- deliberately, since
nothing else in this project reads a tracked "lighting default" the way
`poseDefaultValues`/`cameraDefaultValues` are read elsewhere; Save/Use/
Rename/Delete (the generic list-picker's own built-ins) are the whole
ask. `applyLightingPreset()` writes through to `cfg` first (so
`updateKeyLightPosition()`, which reads `cfg` live rather than a passed
value, picks up the change), then sets `keyLight`/`hemiLight` directly,

**New feature: lock a dev-panel group so its own settings can't be

("Look into Clicko... how the dev panel in Clicko, I have the ability to
lock a group so that group can no longer be reordered within the

Investigated Clicko's implementation first (`CLICKO/index.html`):
a `lockedGroups` Set (keyed per-tab, since Clicko builds 3 SEPARATE DOM
trees for Desktop/Mobile/Landscape) with a 🔒/🔓 toggle icon absolutely
positioned over each group's title-bar corner (needed there since the
icon has to be a sibling, not a child, of the rename-target title
element, and Clicko's title bar has no natural flex-end slot); the
generic drag-reorder handler refuses to even start a drag on a `.dev-row`
whose own parent group is locked, but a locked group itself can still be
dragged/reordered as a whole among other groups -- only its own
row-order is frozen.

Ported the same SEMANTIC into this project's own shared
`src/devpanel/devPanel.js` engine, adapted to its own architecture
rather than copied verbatim (this engine builds ONE set of `.dp-group`
elements, not 3 per-tab trees, so no tab-scoped key was needed; its
`.dp-group-header` is already a flex row, so `margin-left:auto` on the
icon places it at the far right with no absolute-positioning workaround
needed; lock state lives on the group element itself as a
`dp-group-locked` class, the SAME idiom this file already uses for
`collapsed` state, rather than introducing a separate tracked Set).

`createGroupElement()` now builds and appends a `.dp-group-lock-icon`
(🔒/🔓) as a 4th child of the group header, alongside the existing
drag-handle/arrow/title-text -- covers every group-creation path for
free (buildDevPanel()'s own code-defined groups, addCustomGroup(),
applyOrder()'s recreation of a user-created group) since all 3 already
funnel through this one function. `setupReorder()` gained an optional
6th `isLocked(item)` parameter, checked right after resolving the
dragged item and before arming the drag -- only the row-reorder call
site passes one (`item.closest('.dp-group')`'s own `dp-group-locked`
class); the group-reorder call site, the list-picker's own internal
reorder, and the multi-select reorder are all untouched, matching
Clicko's own scoping exactly. `captureGroup()`/`applyOrder()` persist
`locked` the same way they already persist `collapsed` -- automatically
covered by every existing persistence path (Copy, Save/Sync, Reset, and
the "Saved Dev Settings" full-panel snapshot), since all 4 already
funnel through these same 2 functions; no separate save/restore wiring
needed anywhere.

Verified live end to end: locking the Camera group via its own icon

2026-09-17  -  06:50:32 - 06:57:46 EDT (~7m14s, measured)

**Phase 1, part 2 of the Click Function overhaul: Offset On/Off (X/Y)
and Rotation On/Off (X/Y/Z) added to all 10 triggers, both DEV_GROUPS
controls and real runtime application.** Continues directly from the
previous entry's own "not yet done" list. Added the same 7-control block
(`${p}OffsetEnabled`/`OffsetX`/`OffsetY`, `${p}RotationEnabled`/
`RotationX`/`RotationY`/`RotationZ`) to `makeClickHoldPoseGroup()`,
`makeClickPoseGroup()`, and Right Click's own hand-written block, right
after each group's `${p}Mode` control. New `updateOffsetRotationVisibility(p)`
shows/hides each row from its own Enabled checkbox (independent of
Mode -- Offset/Rotation apply the same in Single Pose and Sequence
mode alike), wired into the same initial-sync `CLICK_POSE_KEYS.forEach`/
`CLICK_HOLD_KEYS.forEach` lines `updateClickTriggerModeVisibility()`
already used -- `rc` needed no separate wiring since it's already a
`CLICK_POSE_KEYS` member.

New `applyOffsetRotationToHand(hand, p, progress)` (module-level scratch
Vector3/Euler/Quaternion, no per-frame allocation) composes both
ADDITIVELY onto `hand.wrapper.position`/`.quaternion` -- `addScaledVector`
for Offset, `quaternion.multiply()` (never assignment) for Rotation, so
neither overwrites whatever cursor-tracking/arm-length already set this
frame or the pose's own wrist rotation, exactly the "wont overwrite each
other nor fight against each other" requirement from the original spec.
Offset moves along the CAMERA's own fixed right/up vectors
(`camera.matrixWorld` columns 0/1), not world/local axes -- this
project's camera only pans/zooms, never rotates (CLAUDE.md), so this
stays a stable "up down left right in browser terms" regardless of
framing. Called immediately after `applyPoseValuesToHand()` in every
phase of `updateClickHoldPoseForHand()`/`updateClickPoseForHand()`:
forward/paused (progress = the same per-hand forward-phase progress the
pose-lerp itself uses, so Offset ramps 0->target exactly like Rotation,
per direct user choice "Ramps like Rotation"), looping (progress = 1,
since looping only starts once forward is fully complete), retransition
(progress = `1 - progress`, ramping back down to 0 as the hand returns
to default -- the inverse of the forward ramp).

**Verified live:** `node --check` passes after both the DEV_GROUPS
insertion and the runtime wiring. Live in the browser via
`window.__debug`: confirmed all 10 "Offset On/Off" rows exist in the
dev panel. This session's browser-automation tool showed its own
previously-documented rAF-not-firing behavior (CLAUDE.md's own Gotchas
entry) -- `triggerClickPose()` armed a pending claim but `animate()`
never advanced it, so the normal click-and-wait test showed no visible
movement. Worked around exactly as that Gotchas entry recommends:
called `updateClickPoseForHand()`
directly (exposed on `window.__debug`) with a manufactured `now`,
bypassing the render loop entirely. With Offset (10, 5) and
RotationZ=45 on the `click` trigger: at progress=1.0 the hand's
position moved by almost exactly (+10, +5) (a small Z component from
the camera's own non-axis-aligned right/up, as expected) and its
quaternion became exactly the 45 degree Z-axis quaternion
(sin(22.5 deg)=0.3827, cos(22.5 deg)=0.9239, confirmed exactly); a
follow-up progress=0.5 test (run without resetting the hand's transform
from the first test) showed the quaternion compose to 67.5 degrees
total (45 from the leftover first test + 22.5 = half of 45 for this
one), directly confirming both the 0->target ramp AND additive
composition on top of a prior transform, not overwrite. No runtime
errors in either test.

**Phase 1, part 3 of the Click Function overhaul: Animation Speed Curve
On/Off, Start Time Curve On/Off, and Retransition On/Off added to all
10 triggers.** Continues directly from the previous 2 entries. All 3
are Single-Pose-mode-only gates, per the original spec's own grouping
("Single-Pose-mode settings: ... Animation Speed Curve on/off [NEW],
Start Time Curve on/off [wraps existing], ... Retransition on/off [NEW
behavioral gate]") -- Sequence/Tween mode's own separate Tween-prefixed
timing trio is deliberately untouched, a disclosed scoping choice.

Animation Speed Curve is a genuinely NEW distance->speed curve/range
pair (reuses `computeStartDelayMs()`'s already-generic curve-eval math
rather than writing a 2nd copy of it), overriding the flat Animation
Speed slider when enabled. Computed once per hand at trigger/arm time
(`pendingFrozenSpeedMs` -> `frozenSpeedMs`), same "frozen at trigger
time" treatment `chp.frozenSplayDeg`/the Start Time delay already get.
Start Time Curve On/Off wraps the EXISTING StartTimeCurve/StartTimeRange
controls (default true, preserving current behavior) -- off means every
hand starts its forward transition immediately, no distance-based
stagger. Retransition On/Off (default true) is the new "behavioral
gate": off means the hand never enters the 'retransition' phase at all
and stays at its end pose forever. For hold-based triggers,
`endClickHoldPose()` simply `return`s before touching `chp.phase` when
this gate is off -- whatever phase the hand was already in
(forward/looping) keeps running untouched, which naturally settles at
the target pose once forward's own progress clamps at 1 (no extra
"frozen" phase needed). For fire-and-forget triggers, the existing
'paused' phase's own pause-duration check is simply skipped when the
gate is off, so it never transitions to 'retransition'.

Refactored `updateClickTriggerModeVisibility()`: StartTimeCurve/
StartTimeRange and the whole Retransition trio moved OUT of its
hardcoded `singlePoseKeys` list -- new `updateSingleTimingGateVisibility(p)`
now owns those rows' visibility entirely (mode check AND each control's
own on/off state combined in one place), so 2 different functions never
race to set the same row's `display` value.

directions by comparing `cp.pendingClaimAt` against `performance.now()`
at trigger time (disabled -> delay exactly 0; enabled with a real
curve/range -> a large nonzero curve-driven delay). Speed Curve's

back to the flat `TransitionSpeedMs` slider) -- the ENABLED/curve-

2026-09-17  -  07:13:50 - 07:16:52 EDT (~3m2s, measured)

**Phase 1, part 4 of the Click Function overhaul: Sequence Mode
(Count/Loop/Oscillate) for the 5 fire-and-forget triggers.** Direct
follow-up request retrofitting the same "Sequence Mode - Count, Loop,
Oscillate" concept originally specced for the not-yet-built Loading
Preview onto click/dblclick/rc/tripleClick/quadClick's own Sequence-mode
playback: "provide me settings similar to above where a single click
function will provide the Sequence Mode - Count, Loop, Oscillate
setting availability. So a single click can trigger a sequence to run 3
times, then stop." New controls, Sequence mode only: Sequence Mode
(top-level Count/Loop/Oscillate), Sequence Count + Sequence Count Mode
(Loop/Oscillate) nested under Count, Loop Transition On/Off (instant
jump to frame 1 vs smooth tween back, shown only for a Loop-style
repeat), Sequence Hold Duration (Ms) between laps. New
`updateSequencePlayModeVisibility(p)` governs their rows.

New 'sequencePlaying' phase in `updateClickPoseForHand()`, entered right
after the initial forward pass (lap 1) whenever a repeating PlayMode
applies. Reuses existing machinery rather than writing new
interpolation math: `lerpTweenSequence()` for Oscillate (direction-
flipped `t`) and for the "instant jump back" Loop-Transition-off case;
`lerpLoopSequence()` (the exact same cyclic wrap math Click Hold-Pose's
own Loop Mode already uses) for the smooth-transition Loop case. Count
mode stops once `sequenceLapIndex` exceeds `SequenceCount`, falling
into the existing Pause/Retransition flow completely unchanged --
literally "run 3 times, then stop." Top-level Loop/Oscillate PlayMode
runs unbounded (a disclosed simplification: a fire-and-forget click has
no release event to bound an infinite loop on, unlike Click Hold-Pose's
own hold-bounded Loop Mode).

**2 disclosed simplifications vs. the original spec text** (both
judgment calls, not literal instructions either way): (1) the spec's
own "a single run is 1 count, an oscillate/loop pass is 2 counts"
weighted-counting arithmetic was NOT implemented literally --
`SequenceCount` counts physical one-way traversals directly, the
simplest reading that still fully satisfies the user's own stated
validation case ("run 3 times, then stop"); (2) top-level Loop/
Oscillate PlayMode run unbounded rather than any implied count.

**Phase 2 of the Click Function overhaul: Sequence-mode On Release Mode
(Stop/Complete Sequence) + Trigger All Hands, for the 5 hold-based
triggers.** Only chp/rchp/dcHold/tripleClickHold/quadClickHold get
this -- they're the only triggers with a genuine "release" event mid-
tween; the fire-and-forget family has nothing to release.

'On Release Mode' (default 'Stop', preserves exact existing behavior --
release immediately begins retransition) vs 'Complete Sequence' (new:
the hand keeps playing -- finishes the current forward pass, or the
current lap if already looping -- instead of stopping immediately on
release). Implemented via a `chp.releasePending` flag:
`endClickHoldPose()` leaves `chp.phase` completely untouched when
Complete Sequence is selected (just sets the flag and returns);
`updateClickHoldPoseForHand()`'s own forward and looping phases check
the flag at their own natural completion point (end of the current
pass or lap) and call a new shared `beginTweenReleaseStop()` helper
from there once it's genuinely time to stop.

'Trigger All Hands' (checkbox, default off) -- direct clarification via
AskUserQuestion this session ("force simultaneous release... ALL hands
immediately begin their stop/retransition together, overriding each
hand's own normal distance-based stagger delay"). When on, bypasses the
existing Tween Retransition Start Time Curve/Range stagger entirely
(delay forced to 0 for every hand) instead of each hand computing its
own distance-based delay -- applies in both Stop and Complete Sequence
modes.

**Deliberately NOT implemented this slice, flagged rather than
guessed:** the original spec text's "Tween Stop Start Time Curve"/
"Tween Stop Delay". On close inspection these appear to substantially
duplicate the ALREADY-EXISTING Tween Retransition Start Time Curve/
Range (which already staggers exactly "when does this hand's
retransition begin" for Tween mode) -- building a 2nd curve/range pair
for the identical purpose would be unnecessary duplication (CLAUDE.md
§0a's own architecture-judgment guidance). Surfaced this to the user
rather than silently building a redundant control or silently dropping
the spec item.

**Ported Delete Group/Setting + Undo from TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html's own

**Delete Group/Setting + Undo** -- a close, verified port:
- `#devDeleteGroupBtn`-equivalent (🗑) added to the header alongside the
  4 existing icon buttons. Plain click arms/disarms; a capturing click
  listener on `panel` then deletes whatever `.dp-group`/`.dp-row` is
  clicked next, at any nesting depth, cross-disarming Add Group's own
  selection-arm mode (and vice versa).
- `findDevDeleteProtectionReason()` walks the full ancestor chain,
  refusing deletion only inside "Dev Panel" -- this project's ONE actual
  mandatory built-in group (unlike the template, this project's "Debug"
  is a plain project-specific group like any other, not standing
  scaffolding, so it's correctly left deletable).
- Undo (`#devUndoBtn`, ↶, + Ctrl+Z): a plain in-memory stack of
  `captureFullPanelState()` snapshots (JSON-deep-cloned -- REQUIRED, not
  defensive, since that function's own `values` field is a shallow copy
  and a later in-place mutation of a nested array/object would
  otherwise silently corrupt an already-pushed snapshot) plus a
  separate `{kind:'delete', node, parent, nextSibling}` entry for
  deletions specifically (a value snapshot alone can't recreate a
  deleted setting's actual control markup). Snapshot-push is gated to
  once per pointerdown-to-pointerup "gesture" via a single capturing
  panel-wide `pointerdown` listener, with 3 independent reset paths
  (pointerup, pointercancel, window focus) plus a 2000ms safety timeout
  -- ported directly from the template's own documented lesson: a
  native `<input type=color>` picker never delivers a pointerup back to
  the page at all, which would otherwise permanently stick the gate
  open. Also ported the template's OWN documented self-reference bug
  fix verbatim: the Undo button is itself inside `panel`, so without an
  explicit exclusion, clicking it would push a snapshot of the ALREADY-
  changed state and then immediately pop that same entry back onto
  itself -- a silent no-op. Save/Sync clears the stack
  (`clearDevPanelUndoStack()`, wired into `saveBtn`'s existing handler).
- Verified live (`?dev=1`, real `pointerdown`+`click`+`pointerup`
  dispatches -- NEVER `.click()`, per the template's own explicit
  warning that `.click()` alone doesn't fire `pointerdown` and would
  silently hide the self-reference bug): a value change + Undo restored
  the exact prior value; deleting "Dev Panel" was refused; deleting a
  real setting (`fieldRows`) then Undo restored it in its exact original
  DOM position with its value intact; Save then Undo was correctly a
  no-op (stack cleared). `node --check` clean.

**The "Independent from Desktop" mobile/landscape checkbox system --
NOT a 1:1 port, a redesign for this project's real architecture (flagged
to the user via AskUserQuestion before building; "build the live
checkbox system" was the explicit answer over "rely on existing
perDevice").** The template's own version creates 3 SEPARATE DOM rows
per control (`sliderFoo`/`sliderMobileFoo`/`sliderLandscapeFoo`) and
live-mirrors between them via cross-element wiring
(`wireDesktopMirrorSource()`/`applyDesktopMirror()`). This project has
exactly ONE row per control and 3 store slots already (`store.desktop`/
`.mobile`/`.landscape`, per §12f) -- so the adapted design folds the
mirroring directly into the existing single write-path, `commit()`,
rather than needing any DOM-to-DOM syncing at all:
- New opt-in flag, `dynamicDevice: true`, on a control's registration
  (`main.js`'s `DEV_GROUPS`) -- covers the same 4 uniform types the
  template itself scopes to (slider/color/select/checkbox), not text/
  list-picker/multi-select/button.
- `commit()` grew a `ctrl.dynamicDevice` branch: editing from Desktop
  writes `store.desktop` AND mirrors into every non-independent Mobile/
  Landscape slot directly; editing a non-independent Mobile/Landscape
  row redirects to `store.desktop` (then re-mirrors to the OTHER
  device); editing an INDEPENDENT row writes only its own slot. Because
  `store.mobile[key]`/`store.landscape[key]` are ALREADY always kept
  current by this mirroring while non-independent, there's no separate
  "retained value" cache needed (unlike the template's own
  `devDeviceValues` map) -- toggling independence back on just starts
  from whatever's already sitting there, which is exactly desktop's
  current value.
- `refreshRowDisplaysForEditingTab()` (the existing tab-switch hook)
  grew a `dynamicDevice`-aware value-resolution branch, plus a new
  `refreshDynamicDeviceRowChrome()` call per control: hides the whole
  row when not independent-shown on Mobile/Landscape (`row.style.display`
  -- there's no separate DOM row to simply not create, unlike the
  template), and swaps which of the 2 checkboxes is interactable
  ("Show in Mobile/Landscape" only meaningful on Desktop, "Independent
  from Desktop" only meaningful on Mobile/Landscape).
- `buildRow()` appends both checkboxes (native title tooltips, no extra
  markup) for an opted-in control; `createGroupElement()` grew a
  matching pair of GROUP-level cascade checkboxes (hidden unless the
  group has at least one dynamicDevice descendant at any depth), same
  "no persisted state, walks live DOM, checked/indeterminate always
  computed from children" design as the template's own version.
- Persistence: `devVisibility`/`devIndependence` added to BOTH snapshot
  shapes that actually exist here -- `saveSettings()`/`resetSettings()`'s
  own narrower `{values, order, textOverrides}` object (the REAL
  Save/Sync/Reset path) AND `captureFullPanelState()`/
  `applyFullPanelState()` (Copy/Named-States/Undo) -- a real gap caught
  before shipping: the 2 snapshot shapes are genuinely separate in this
  file (unlike the template, which only ever had one), and adding the
  new fields to just one would have silently dropped the other on a
  real page reload.

**Verification gap, disclosed rather than glossed over: this feature's
own live browser test could NOT be completed this round.** A temporary
`dynamicDevice: true` was added to `fieldRows` to exercise the real
pipeline end-to-end, but every attempt to actually load the app (4
different static-server processes across 3 ports, both `localhost` and
an explicit `127.0.0.1` bind, both this session's own browser tool AND
a plain `curl` completely outside it) hit the exact same failure:
`main.js` (441,597 bytes as of this task) truncates at exactly 391,680
bytes and the connection resets -- reproduced identically via `curl`
(exit 56, "failure receiving network data"), which rules out the
browser-automation tool itself as the cause (it reproduces even for a
request that never touches a browser). 1 of 4 `curl` retries against
the SAME file did succeed with the correct full byte count, so this is
a real, environment-level intermittent large-response limit (most
likely something in this sandbox's own network layer, given the
identical exact truncation point across independently-bound server
processes), not a bug in this session's code -- `devPanel.js`/`style.css`
never hit it, only the much larger `main.js`. Several further browser
retries after that one successful `curl` still failed. Given `node
--check` passes on both files, and every function this new code path
touches (`commit`/`displayValue`/`refreshRowDisplaysForEditingTab`/
`buildRow`/`createGroupElement`/`saveSettings`/`resetSettings`/
`captureFullPanelState`/`applyFullPanelState`) was read in full before
being modified rather than guessed at, this is reported as **written
and syntax-valid, NOT live-verified** -- the temporary test flag was
reverted (not left in `fieldRows`) rather than shipped unverified as if
it were a real, considered content decision for that control. A 4th
static-server port (`static-alt3`, 8423) was added to
`.claude/launch.json` while chasing this, kept since it's a low-risk,
same-pattern addition a future session may also need with 2 concurrent
sessions already active.

Next: get this feature actually exercised live once the environment
issue clears (or `main.js` next drops back under whatever the real
truncation threshold is), then decide which real project controls, if
any, should opt into `dynamicDevice: true` -- that's a separate content
decision from today's engine-capability work.

**Real bug found and fixed:** `resetSettings()`'s localStorage branch
only ever called `applyStoredValues()` (which is what actually runs

2026-09-17  -  07:39:32 - 07:58:29 EDT (~18m57s, measured)

**Click Function Phase 4, first slice: dynamic "Custom Click Functions."**
The user's own instruction after the concurrent session (referenced in
the entry directly above) finished and pushed: "the other session is
done. fix everything." Confirmed via 2 rounds of AskUserQuestion first
-- this is a genuine architectural refactor (CLAUDE.md §0a), not a
routine extension, so it was proposed and scoped before any code was
written, not silently executed.

**Real architectural discovery before writing any code:** devPanel.js
had NO way to inject a brand-new, LIVE, interactive control group at
runtime -- only `addCustomGroup()`, an EMPTY group the user drags
existing settings into. Confirmed via a dedicated Explore-agent
investigation (6 targeted questions, not a guess) rather than assuming:
`buildRow()`/`createGroupElement()` are ordinary module-level functions
with no hidden one-time-init dependency, safe to export and call again
later; `cfg`/`store` don't require a key to pre-exist before a row is
built; group/row ORDER already persists generically (reads
`dataset.key` straight off the live DOM), but VALUES only *restore*
correctly if the new group's descriptor is pushed into the module-level
`devGroups` array `applyStoredValues()`/`refreshRowDisplaysForEditingTab()`/
`findCtrl()`/the dynamicDevice cascade helpers all iterate. This
produced a minimal, precise diff instead of a guessed one.

**devPanel.js:** exports `buildRow`/`createGroupElement` (2 one-word
changes) + one new exported function, `renderDynamicGroup(groupSpec)`
-- pushes the spec into `devGroups`, seeds `cfg`/`store` from each
control's own `def` (never clobbering an already-restored value),
renders via the exact same `createGroupElement`/`buildRow` calls
`buildDevPanel()` itself uses, appends to the single shared `#dpGroups`
container (confirmed this project has ONE shared container for all 3
device tabs, not per-tab DOM), then calls
`refreshRowDisplaysForEditingTab()` so any already-restored values for
the new keys display correctly immediately.

**main.js:** new "Custom Click Functions" DEV_GROUPS group with an
internal-bookkeeping `customClickFunctionIds` field (JSON array of
`{id, title}`, restored through the normal cfg pipeline like any other
control) and the "+ Add Click Function" button.
`addCustomClickFunction()`/`registerCustomClickFunction()` reuse
`makeClickPoseGroup()` VERBATIM -- the exact same factory
click/dblclick/rc/tripleClick/quadClick already use -- plus one spliced-
in `Type` control (Click/Right Click, no onChange needed since the
event-wiring reads it live at trigger time). The new id is pushed into
`CLICK_POSE_KEYS` and `clickPoseTriggers`; every existing GENERIC
pipeline (`updateClickPoseForHand`, `triggerClickPose`,
`parseClickPoseConfig`, every visibility function -- all already
parametrized over `p`, already proven correct on 5 IDs) needed ZERO
changes to pick up a 6th+. `restoreCustomClickFunctions()` (called from
`onRestore`) re-creates every saved custom function's live group fresh
on each page load, since `renderDynamicGroup()`'s own DOM rows are pure
runtime state that doesn't survive a reload on its own.

**Real bug fixed, found by reasoning through the architecture, not by
accident:** `getOrInitHandCP()` used to build a hand's entire `_cp`
state object ONCE, on first touch, from whatever `CLICK_POSE_KEYS` held
AT THAT MOMENT. Since custom functions get added well after hands are
already built (and possibly already touched by other triggers), an
already-initialized hand's `_cp` would permanently lack a newly-added
custom id's own state slot -- the very next frame that id fired for
that hand would crash (`chp.armedForHoldStartTime` etc. on `undefined`).
Fixed by making the per-trigger state backfill PER-KEY on every call
(`if (!hand._cp[p])`, not `if (!hand._cp)`), cheap given the small
trigger count.

**Also fixed a real pre-existing gap, caught while building the
generalized widget-builder this needed:** Animation Speed Curve's own
`SpeedCurve`/`SpeedCurveRange` fields (added earlier this session, Phase
1 part 3) never got the interactive curve-graph widget every OTHER
curve field gets -- they rendered as plain raw-JSON text boxes.
`buildClickHoldPoseWidgets()`/`buildClickPoseWidgets()` now build these
too, which retroactively fixes this for all 10 existing triggers, not
just future custom ones.

**Deliberately scoped DOWN from the full original Phase 4 spec**, each
a disclosed simplification, not an oversight (see the DEV_GROUPS
group's own comment for the full account): fire-and-forget only (no
custom Click+Hold functions yet -- architecturally distinct state
machine, would need its own "+Add Click+Hold Function" entry point);
Type is Click/Right Click only (no Scroll -- a genuinely new trigger-

**Adapted all 11 existing "Click Function" trigger groups to the
dynamicDevice mobile/desktop checkbox system, without changing any of
their actual saved values.** Direct request ("for our existing click
functions within my 'Click Functions' group, adapt all those existing
click function sub groups to match our new system. So dont change any
of the actual settings, but make it show the way i requested"). Covers
every control in: Click Hold-Pose, Right-Click Hold-Pose, Double Click
Hold, Triple-Click Hold, Quadruple-Click Hold (the 5 `makeClickHoldPoseGroup()`
instances), Click Pose, Double-Click Pose, Triple-Click Pose,
Quadruple-Click Pose (the 4 `makeClickPoseGroup()` instances), Right
Click, and Tween (2 hand-written groups) -- the "Custom Click
Functions" group (the concurrent session's own new Phase 4 runtime-
created groups) was deliberately left untouched, out of scope for this
request and not this session's to touch mid-build.

**Real design problem solved first, before touching any real control:**
simply flagging an already-`perDevice: true` control with `dynamicDevice:
true` would have flipped its default from "genuinely independent per
device" to "mirrors Desktop," silently overwriting whatever different
Mobile/Landscape values were already saved the next time anything
re-synced -- a real settings change, not just a new checkbox appearing.
Fixed at the ENGINE level, not per-control: `devPanel.js`'s
`isDevRowIndependent(tab, ctrl)` (previously `(tab, key)`, all 7 call
sites updated to pass the control object) now defaults to `ctrl.perDevice`
when nothing's been explicitly toggled yet, instead of a blanket
`false` -- an already-independent control stays independent by
default; an already-shared control stays mirrored (a no-op, since all
3 devices already hold the same value). Matches the template's own
"UNIVERSAL" 2026-09-17 correction's category-aware-default intent,
adapted to this project's real architecture rather than copied
verbatim (this project has no static per-tab array entries to detect
the presence of -- `ctrl.perDevice` IS the equivalent signal here).

**Confirmed by directly reading all 4 control arrays before writing
anything:** none of the ~60 controls across these 11 groups currently
use `perDevice: true` -- every one of them defaults to "mirrors
Desktop," which is an EXACT no-op for a control where all 3 devices
already hold the identical shared value. This specific batch never
actually exercises the new `ctrl.perDevice` default in practice; the
engine fix exists for a future control that does (e.g. something in
the Camera family, which already uses `perDevice: true` today).

**Implementation:** a single `withDynamicDevice(controls)` helper in
`main.js` (applied once at 4 call sites: both factory functions' own
`controls:` array, plus the Right Click and Tween groups' own literal
arrays) sets `ctrl.dynamicDevice = true` on every control EXCEPT the
same types `buildRow()` never renders the 2 new checkboxes for anyway
(text/list-picker/multi-select/button) -- flagging those would
silently engage `commit()`'s dynamicDevice mirroring with no UI to
ever control it, for zero benefit. Verified this exclusion logic in
total isolation via a standalone `node -e` snippet against 8 synthetic
sample controls covering every real type used across these groups
(checkbox/select/slider/no-type-default-slider correctly flagged;
text/list-picker/multi-select/button correctly skipped) before trusting
it against the real, much larger file.

**Verification, and an honest gap:** `node --check` passes on both
files; `grep` confirms `withDynamicDevice(` wraps exactly the 4
intended call sites, no more, no fewer; the underlying commit()/
isDevRowIndependent() mechanism itself was already thoroughly live-
verified in the prior same-day entry (mirroring, independence both
directions, row hide/show, group cascade, Save+reload persistence).
Live end-to-end verification against the REAL, now-larger `main.js`
(455,264 bytes as of this entry) was attempted 5 times this round and
failed every time with the same environment truncation issue already
diagnosed in the prior entry -- confirmed via `curl` immediately after
that the file itself is fine (2 of 4 back-to-back `curl` fetches
against the exact same URL succeeded with the full correct byte count,
consistent with that issue's own documented ~50% intermittent rate),
so this is the browser tool/sandbox network layer again, not a
regression from this change. Given the design was solved at the root
(the `perDevice`-aware default) rather than worked around per-control,
and confirmed via direct reading that this specific batch has zero
`perDevice: true` controls to begin with, this is reported with HIGH
confidence as a real no-op for every existing value -- but, per this
project's own §0c discipline, explicitly NOT claimed as freshly re-
verified live in the actual running app this round, unlike the prior
entry's dynamicDevice work.

2026-09-17  -  08:42:43 - 08:47:40 EDT (4m57s, measured)

**Fixed: a Saved Camera preset authored/imported with a shorter x/y/z/
tx/ty/tz/fov field naming silently fell back to every default value on
"Use," instead of moving the camera to that preset's own view.** Direct
bug report, with the user's own exact JSON shape pasted in for both
Camera and Lighting. Root cause, found by direct comparison against
`captureCameraPreset()`'s own output shape rather than by live
debugging: every read site for a camera preset item
(`applyCameraPreset()`, `setSelectedCameraAsDefault()`, and the Loading
Preview's own camera-preset override block) only ever looked for this
app's own internal field names -- `cameraX`/`cameraY`/`cameraZ`/
`cameraFov`/`targetX`/`targetY`/`targetZ` -- never the shorter `x`/`y`/
`z`/`fov`/`tx`/`ty`/`tz` naming the user's own JSON used. Since none of
those internal keys existed on an item in that shape, every single
field fell through to its own hardcoded default every time, regardless
of which preset was selected -- indistinguishable from "it doesn't
work" (always snapping to one identical default view). Lighting's own
preset needed no equivalent fix -- the user's own lighting JSON already
matched `LIGHTING_PRESET_KEYS` exactly (`keyAzimuth`/`keyElevation`/
etc.), confirmed by direct comparison against both read sites
(`applyLightingPreset()`, `applyLoadingPreviewLighting()`), both of
which already read the correct names.

**Fix:** a new `normalizeCameraPresetItem(item)` helper accepts EITHER
naming (the internal one wins if somehow both are present on the same
item) and returns a NEW object in the internal shape -- never mutating
the original saved/imported item, so a later re-export/re-save isn't
silently reformatted underneath the user. Applied at all 3 real read
sites: `applyCameraPreset()` (normalizes its own incoming argument),
`setSelectedCameraAsDefault()` (normalizes its own separate direct
`item[key]` reads independently, since `applyCameraPreset()`'s own
internal normalized copy isn't exposed back to its caller), and the
Loading Preview's camera-override block.

**Verified without a full live app load** (the same environment
truncation issue documented in the 2 prior entries struck again this
round -- `window.__debug` never became available after a 13-second
wait, the model/settings-restore fetch itself apparently stalling, not
just the initial script-load truncation seen before; not re-litigated
further, per this project's own §0b usage-efficiency guidance, given
the fix was already well-verified another way): an isolated `node -e`
snippet ran the ACTUAL `normalizeCameraPresetItem()` source against
the user's own exact pasted "Top" camera JSON and confirmed every
field maps correctly (`x:2.669.. -> cameraX:2.669..`, `tx:2.307.. ->
targetX:2.307..`, `fov:32 -> cameraFov:32`, etc. -- exact values, not
approximations); a 2nd `node -e` check confirmed an ALREADY-internal-
format preset (what a real Save via the UI actually produces) passes
through completely unchanged -- no regression for any existing saved
camera. `node --check` passes on `main.js`.

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=144` -> `?v=145`.

2026-09-17 08:56:51-09:16:55 EDT (20m04s)

**Added: on-demand live Loading Preview toggle + X/Y position offset
sliders, on top of the pre-existing `loadingPreviewEnabled` (shown only
during the real page-load screen).** Direct request ("Provide a
checkbox to show the loading hand preview. Provide an X offset slider
and Y offset slider so i can position the loading hand preview"),
scoped via an AskUserQuestion exchange -- the pre-existing checkbox
already showed the preview during real page load, so the open question
was whether the user wanted that alone, or a genuinely new toggle that
also works AFTER the real hand field has already started rendering.
User's answer: "Build an on-demand live preview toggle" -- the bigger
of the 2 options.

**Why this needed real architectural work, not just a checkbox:**
`animate()`'s own `if (!fieldStarted && loadingPreviewRenderer)` branch
permanently stops calling `updateLoadingPreviewAnimation()`/rendering
the preview once the real hand field starts (`fieldStarted = true`) --
a deliberate one-time-per-session optimization, not a bug. Making the
preview controllable AFTER that point needed a second, independent
render loop.

**Implementation:**
- 2 new `DEV_GROUPS` controls: `loadingPreviewShowLive` (checkbox,
  "Show Loading Preview (Live, No Reload Needed)") and
  `loadingPreviewOffsetX`/`loadingPreviewOffsetY` (sliders, -300 to
  300px).
- `buildLoadingPreview()` gained a `bypassEnabledGate` parameter so the
  live toggle can force-build the preview scene even when
  `loadingPreviewEnabled` is off.
- New `repositionLoadingPreview()` applies the offset sliders via
  `translate(-50%, -50%) translate(Xpx, Ypx)` -- base self-centering
  composed with an additional pixel offset in one `transform` string.
- New, fully independent `startLoadingPreviewLiveLoop()` (its own
  `requestAnimationFrame` loop, gated on `cfg.loadingPreviewShowLive`
  and self-terminating when that flag goes false) plus
  `setLoadingPreviewLiveVisible(show)`, which always does a fresh
  `buildLoadingPreview(true)` before starting the loop rather than
  assuming a previous build is still valid -- disposing the prior
  `WebGLRenderer` first, so the toggle is safely repeatable across many
  on/off cycles.
- `tryStartField()` (the point the real field becomes ready and the
  original loading screen hides) now checks
  `cfg.loadingPreviewShowLive` and either starts the live loop (via
  `setLoadingPreviewLiveVisible(true)`, not a direct
  `startLoadingPreviewLiveLoop()` call -- self-caught during review:
  calling the loop directly would silently do nothing if the checkbox
  had been pre-checked before the model even finished loading while
  `loadingPreviewEnabled` was off, since nothing would have been built
  yet) or hides the canvas.
- `index.html`: `#loadingPreviewCanvas` moved from inside `#loading` to
  a top-level sibling immediately before it, so its visibility no
  longer inherits `#loading`'s own real-field-start hidden state.
  `style.css`: the canvas rule changed from `display: none` to
  `display: none; position: fixed; top: 50%; left: 50%; pointer-events:
  none;` to support the new offset-based positioning independent of
  its old flex-child-of-`#loading` layout.

**Verified live** (after retrying past this project's own documented
environment network-truncation issue on `main.js` -- port 8423 failed
repeatedly, a fresh port 8425 succeeded): controls render with correct
labels/defaults; canvas stays hidden by default; first toggle-on shows
it at the correct base-centered position (`getComputedStyle().transform`
matrix checked against the hand-derived expected matrix for 2 different
X/Y offset combinations); toggle-off hides it again; a 2nd toggle-on
cycle (exercising the dispose-and-rebuild path for real) rendered
correctly with no console errors; `read_console_messages` filtered for
`context|WebGL|dispose` after that 2nd cycle came back clean (no
logs). Also re-confirmed, on a fresh full page load, that the
PRE-EXISTING `loadingPreviewEnabled` real-load-screen preview still
displays correctly (canvas `display: block` while `fieldStarted` is
still `false`) -- a regression check on the `index.html`/`style.css`
restructuring above, since that markup move could plausibly have
affected the original behavior. `node --check` passes on `main.js`.

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=145` -> `?v=146`.

2026-09-17 10:49:32-11:04:02 EDT (14m30s)

**Fixed: "Show Loading Preview (Live)" checkbox reported as not
working.** Direct bug report ("the Show Loading Preview checkbox
doesnt work. What i wanted was that when i selected it, I get to
prview the loading animatin hand. It should just show ontop of
everything in browser"). Found and fixed 2 real issues via direct code
inspection (live browser reproduction was blocked for a long stretch by
this project's own documented environment network-truncation gotcha --
7+ consecutive navigation attempts across 3 static-server ports and 2
tabs each hit `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` on `main.js`, `devPanel.js`,
or the `.glb` model in turn, while `curl` against the exact same files
succeeded every single time -- eventually got a clean load and could
verify live for real, see below):

1. **A real race: checking the box before the model finishes loading
   silently stranded it forever (or until the real field happened to
   start).** `setLoadingPreviewLiveVisible()` returns early with no
   effect while `modelMeasurementsReady` is still false, and nothing
   previously re-checked the checkbox's own state once that flipped
   true -- only `tryStartField()` did, which ALSO waits on the settings-
   restore/min-time gates, not just the model. Fixed by calling
   `setLoadingPreviewLiveVisible(true)` right where
   `modelMeasurementsReady = true` is set (immediately after
   `buildLoadingPreview()`'s own unconditional call there), closing the
   window instead of leaving the checkbox checked but inert.
2. **`#loadingPreviewCanvas` had no explicit `z-index`.** It likely
   already painted above `#viewport` under default CSS stacking rules
   (a `position:fixed` element paints after a non-positioned one
   regardless of DOM order), but nothing guaranteed it stayed above the
   dev panel (`.dp-panel`, z-index 9999) or anything else -- added an
   explicit `z-index: 100000`, directly matching the literal request
   ("it should just show ontop of everything in browser").

**Verified live** (after eventually getting a clean load): screenshot
confirmed the preview hand renders centered on screen, clearly visible
on top of the main hand field, the instant the checkbox is checked;
`getComputedStyle().zIndex` read `100000` as expected; unchecking
hides it again (`display: none`); a 2nd on/off/on cycle (dispose-and-
rebuild path) worked cleanly with no console errors. One verification
false alarm caught and correctly dismissed along the way: a JS-exec
query reported `document.documentElement.clientWidth/clientHeight` as
`0` and the canvas's own computed `top`/`left` as `0px` even on a
freshly-loaded, visibly-rendering page -- this project's own already-
documented browser-tool gotcha (the JS-exec path can misreport
layout/dimension queries even while a screenshot on the same tab
renders correctly) rather than a real positioning bug; the actual
screenshot showed the preview correctly centered, confirming the CSS
`top:50%;left:50%` rule was working fine all along.

**A separate cache-invalidation gap found and fixed along the way:**
`index.html`'s `style.css` link was still pinned at `?v=12` even though
`style.css`'s content had changed twice this session (the canvas
`position`/`display` rule 2 entries ago, and this entry's `z-index`
addition) -- the static server always serves current disk content
regardless of query string, but the BROWSER's own HTTP cache does not,
so a tab that had already fetched `style.css?v=12` once kept reusing
that stale cached response across reloads, silently masking the
z-index fix during verification until this was caught and the query
bumped to `?v=13`. Bump this any time `style.css`'s actual content
changes, the same discipline this project already applies to
`main.js?v=NNN`.

Cache-busters: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=146` -> `?v=147`;
`style.css?v=12` -> `?v=13` (first bump of this file all session).

2026-09-17 11:28:48-11:31:21 EDT (2m33s)

**CORRECTED -- the previous entry's live-verification screenshot was
almost certainly a false positive; the real bug was neither of the 2
things fixed there.** Direct follow-up report immediately after the
previous entry shipped: "1. I dont see the loading hand on actual

live-verified.

**Root cause: a leftover bad DATA value, not a code bug.** Checked the
real, git-tracked settings log
(`data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json`, the actual source of truth
every visitor's page load restores from) directly rather than re-
trusting the previous entry's sandbox screenshot -- found
`loadingPreviewSize: 2000` persisted across all 3 device scopes
(desktop/mobile/landscape), against this control's own coded range
(`min: 80, max: 400, def: 160` -- main.js:504). 2000px is 5x past the
slider's own designed max, almost certainly typed in via this dev
panel's own "click-to-type, auto-expanding range" feature (CLAUDE.md
§12h -- typing any value works and silently expands the slider's
bounds to accommodate it), whether by an earlier test pass or the
user's own exploration. Since `resizeLoadingPreview()` reads this one
shared value regardless of which mode (`loadingPreviewEnabled` or
`loadingPreviewShowLive`) triggered the build, a single bad persisted
number explains BOTH reported symptoms at once -- a canvas rendered at
2000x2000px is either wildly oversized/unrecognizable on a normal
viewport or (plausibly) exceeds a GPU/driver limit and renders nothing
visible at all; either way, not "a hand."

**This also means the previous entry's "confirmed via screenshot"
claim needs re-reading with real skepticism.** That screenshot showed
a round gray blob roughly at the center of the frame, interpreted at
the time as the correctly-centered preview hand -- but with
`loadingPreviewSize` already at 2000 in the real settings this whole
time, what was actually on screen was almost certainly either a hugely
zoomed, unrecognizable fragment of the SAME preview hand (consistent
with "gray blob," inconsistent with "confirmed working"), or one of
the regular field hands the radial layout naturally clusters near
screen center. The z-index and modelMeasurementsReady-race fixes from
that entry are still believed correct on their own merits (independent
code-level reasoning, not retracted) -- but the CONCLUSION that the
feature was confirmed working end-to-end was not actually supported by
what that screenshot showed, once this real data problem is accounted
for. Recorded here as a correction rather than editing that entry, per
this file's own append-only rule.

**Fix:** `loadingPreviewSize` reset to `160` (the coded default) for
all 3 scopes directly in `data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json` --
a data correction, not a code change. Deliberately did NOT add a
code-level clamp/cap on this value in `resizeLoadingPreview()` -- CLAUDE.md
§12h's auto-expanding-range behavior is a deliberate, established design
decision (letting a power user type any value they want), and a hard
clamp would fight that design rather than fix the actual problem, which
was bad persisted data, not the mechanism that allowed it to be entered.
`loadingMinTimeMs` was also found sitting at its own max (5000ms, valid
range 0-5000) -- left untouched since it's within bounds and not
demonstrably a mistake (just a long loading-screen hold if
`loadingPreviewEnabled` is on).

**Verification:** Confirmed via direct JSON parse that the fix landed
correctly on all 3 scopes and the file remains valid JSON. Did NOT
attempt another live browser screenshot this round, given the above
finding calls the previous round's own visual verification into
question -- the next real confirmation should come from the user's own
report after this data fix reaches production, not another sandbox
screenshot claimed as proof.

No code files changed this entry -- only the settings data file.

Click+Hold function chp/rchp's full machinery (per-hand stagger,
Sequence mode, Loop Mode, Offset/Rotation, On Release Mode) for free.
getOrInitHandCHP() got the same per-key state-backfill fix
getOrInitHandCP() already had (Phase 4's first slice) -- CLICK_HOLD_KEYS
can now also grow at runtime, so an already-touched hand's `_chp` needs
the same defensive backfill CLICK_POSE_KEYS already required.

4. **Mandatory gated subgroups (new capability, 3 rounds of direct
clarification mid-task).** Offset/Rotation/Animation Speed Curve/Start
Time Curve/Retransition are now real nested collapsible groups, each
with its own On/Off checkbox moved bodily into the GROUP'S OWN
HEADER/LABEL rather than living as a plain settings row ("checkbox
within its label... when off, they will just show as empty setting
groups"). New `wrapGatedSubgroup(enabledKey, memberKeys, subgroupTitle)`
does this generically: finds the existing "Enabled" row by its
`data-key` (unchanged -- same buildRow()/commit() wiring every other
control already has), creates a new nested group via devPanel.js's own
`createGroupElement()` (now exported), relocates the member rows
(OffsetX/Y etc.) into the new group's body, and moves the Enabled row
itself (whole row, not just its checkbox -- preserves the `data-key`
so displayValue()'s own restore-sync selector still finds it with zero
devPanel.js changes there) into the new group's header. CSS
(`.dp-group-header .dp-row.dp-group-gate-row { display: contents }`
+ hiding the row's own label) makes it render as a plain inline
checkbox next to the group's title instead of a nested block. One new
devPanel.js guard was needed: the group header's own click-to-collapse
listener now ignores clicks on `.dp-group-gate-row`, so toggling the
relocated checkbox doesn't ALSO collapse the group on the same click.
Applied once for all 10 static triggers (`wrapAllClickFunctionGatedSubgroups()`,
module init) and per custom function (`wrapClickFunctionGatedSubgroups()`,
called from `renderCustomClickFunctionGroup()`) -- the SAME already-
proven `updateOffsetRotationVisibility()`/`updateSingleTimingGateVisibility()`
functions need zero changes, since they select rows by `data-key` alone,
which still resolves correctly regardless of where in the tree a row
now lives.

**Verified live**, real UI clicks via `computer{left_click}` on the
actual buttons (not just direct function calls): confirmed all 5 gated
subgroups render correctly for `chp` (Enabled row lives inside its own
group's header, member rows correctly relocated into that group's

IDENTICAL symptom on `chpEnabled`, an untouched, unrelated control.
Confirmed via real button clicks: `custom1` (pose, family=mobile,

2026-09-19 19:15:02-19:28:58 EDT (13m56s)

**Fixed the REAL root cause of "I dont see the loading hand" (2
sessions back) and added a proper HANDO camera/lighting import
pipeline for the Loading Preview.** Direct follow-up after the
`loadingPreviewCameraSelector` architecture change (previous
Loading-Preview-scoped entries): pointing it at "Left" (a real HANDO-
imported camera, same numeric values in both projects' own settings
files) made the preview hand disappear ENTIRELY. Root-caused via a
standalone measurement script (three.js in a scratchpad Node
environment, loading this GLB directly, outside the running app,
never committed): HANDY DANDIES rotates every hand clone by
`alignQuat` (measured once from this model's own bind pose, aligning
wrist->fingertip to -Z); HANDO has no equivalent correction (its own
`modelRoot` uses the raw GLTF orientation plus its own separate,
independently-tuned Whole-Hand Rotation sliders). A camera captured in
HANDO is therefore in a DIFFERENT coordinate frame -- its raw numbers
point at empty space once HDD's own alignQuat rotation is applied.

**Validated the fix mathematically before writing any UI around it**
(direct user follow-up, "2" -- build the converter, not just point at
empty by default): rotating HANDO's "Left" target by this exact
`alignQuat` lands it ~3 world units from the wrist-to-fingertip
midline (handLengthRaw ~16 units total) -- essentially on the hand's
own central axis, confirming HANDO's saved cameras were captured at
the model's raw bind pose (Whole-Hand Rotation at 0), not whatever
HANDO's own sliders currently happen to read (85 deg/20 deg at the
time this was checked -- unrelated leftover state, not what "Left"
etc. were captured under).

**New architecture, all direct follow-up requests in the same
exchange:**
- `loadingPreviewSavedCameras` and (new) `loadingPreviewSavedLighting`
  -- 2 local list-pickers, own to the Loading Preview only, separate
  from the main field's own `savedCameras`/`savedLighting` ("so the
  camera settings for the loading preview should be local to that
  only" / "lighitng settings for the loading preview will also be
  local to that, and the hand field itself wil have its own").
  Deliberately NOT extended to the main field's own lists -- those
  frame the WHOLE FIELD at field-radius scale, a fundamentally
  different context HANDO (single-hand-only) has no equivalent of, so
  the same conversion wouldn't be physically meaningful there.
- `convertHandoCameraPreset(item)` -- rotates a HANDO camera's
  position+target by `alignQuat` (`normalizeCameraPresetItem()`'s own
  either-naming acceptance reused, so HANDO's raw x/y/z/tx/ty/tz/fov
  export works directly).
- `convertHandoLightingPreset(item)` -- NOT a simple angle rotation:
  HANDO's own `updateKeyLightPosition()` and HDD's own assign X/Z to
  sin(az)/cos(az) OPPOSITELY (confirmed by direct comparison of both
  projects' source). Reconstructs HANDO's own direction vector from
  its azimuth/elevation (using HANDO's own formula), rotates that
  vector by `alignQuat`, then re-derives azimuth/elevation using HDD's
  own formula, inverted (verified via a round-trip self-consistency
  check: HDD's own forward+inverse formula reproduces the exact input
  angle for 5 test cases, confirming the inverse formula itself is
  correct before trusting it on rotated HANDO data).
- `devPanel.js` gained one small, additive, backward-compatible hook:
  `ctrl.importTransform`, called on each incoming item during the
  existing clipboard-based `importable: true` Import flow, right
  before the existing merge-by-name logic -- a control without this
  property behaves exactly as before. Wired to the 2 converters above
  on the Loading Preview's own 2 new lists only.
- `captureLoadingPreviewCameraPreset()`/`captureLoadingPreviewLightingPreset()`
  -- `captureCurrent` for the 2 new list-pickers' own Save button,
  reading wherever the Loading Preview's own camera/lights currently
  are (module-level `loadingPreviewCameraTarget`/
  `loadingPreviewKeyLightRef`/`loadingPreviewHemiLightRef`, since this
  preview has no interactive OrbitControls of its own to read a live
  target from, unlike the main camera).

**Verified live, end-to-end** (after several environment-truncation
retries on `main.js`/`devpanel/devPanel.js` -- this project's own
already-documented sandbox gotcha, not a code issue): applied the
EXACT converted values my standalone Node script computed directly to
the running app's `loadingPreviewSavedCameras`, selected it, and
screenshotted -- a clearly recognizable, well-framed hand (knuckles,
fingers, thumb all visible) appeared, confirming the live app's own
real `alignQuat` matches the standalone script's independently-
measured one. Same live test for lighting (HANDO's own default
az=40/el=55 converted and applied) -- clean shading with a visible,
correctly-directed highlight, no degenerate/black result. The
Import-button's own clipboard-paste UI path itself was NOT re-tested
live (this automation environment's Document isn't focused, so
`navigator.clipboard.writeText()` throws `NotAllowedError` even after
an explicit `window.focus()`) -- its own merge-by-name/commit logic is
pre-existing, unchanged code already exercised by the pose
import/export feature; only the new `importTransform` call itself is
new, and it's a single, directly-tested function call.

Cache-busters: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=152` -> `?v=153`;
`devpanel/devPanel.js?v=29` -> `?v=30`.

**Verified and shipped the 2 gaps the user asked to fix first out of the

direct `window.__debug` function calls, this project's own established
rAF-bypass method -- see CLAUDE.md's Gotchas) plus documentation/commit,
no further code changes.

**Master On/Off (`updateClickFunctionEnabledVisibility(p)`)** -- verified
live on `chp`: with `chpEnabled` toggled off, all 28 other rows/nested
subgroups inside the group's body hid (0 visible, only the Enabled row
itself remained); re-enabling correctly restored FINE-GRAINED visibility

Testing this exposed (but did not require fixing) a pre-existing, already-
documented environment characteristic (this session's browser-automation
tool has a fixed 336px-wide viewport, permanently reporting
`realDeviceClass() === 'mobile'`): a real click on `chpEnabled`'s own
checkbox silently failed to update `cfg` at all, because `commit()`'s own

`changedOn === realDeviceClass()`, and a non-independent control's

**Tween Stop Delay (new `'stopping'` phase in `updateClickHoldPoseForHand()`,
`endClickHoldPose()`'s rewritten Stop-branch)** -- verified live on `chp`
in Sequence mode (a throwaway 2-pose test tween, `Fist`->`Open Palm`,
added to `cfg.savedTweenSequences` for the duration of the test, removed
after): releasing mid-forward-pass correctly entered `'stopping'` (not
directly `'retransition'`); the per-frame virtual-elapsed accumulator grew
at a visibly DECREASING rate across 8 consecutive 50ms samples (+43.75,
+37.5, +31.25, +25, +18.75, +12.5, +6.25, +0 -- a clean linear ramp-down
to zero, matching the intended "visibly slows to a stop" behavior rather
than an abrupt cutoff); `chp.phase` correctly advanced to `'retransition'`
the instant `tDecay` reached 1, with `chpRetransitionEnabled` still on. A
2nd run with `chpRetransitionEnabled` off confirmed the opposite branch:
`stoppingFreezeAtEnd` came back `true`, and the hand correctly landed in
`'idle'` (frozen in place, no retransition) once decay completed, matching
the spec's "If Retransition is turned off, the hand will just stop where
it is." Also re-confirmed `RetransitionEnabled`'s scope now genuinely
covers Sequence mode's Stop path (not just Single Pose, the corrected
scoping from the preceding session) -- both live runs used Sequence mode.

One test-harness limitation, disclosed rather than silently glossed over:
visible pose INTERPOLATION during the decay window was not independently
confirmed in this run, because the test drove the forward phase on a
synthetic small `now` clock (to sidestep `chpHoldConfirmMs`'s real-time
gate) but `endClickHoldPose()` itself timestamps via the real
`nowVirtual()` (`performance.now()`-based) -- the mismatch left
`stoppingBaseElapsedMs` already far past `chpTweenSpeedMs`, so `progress`
was saturated at 1.0 throughout the decay window in this specific test
run. The phase-transition and decay-accumulator math -- the actual new
logic this fix adds -- were fully exercised and correct; only the
already-existing `lerpTweenSequence()` call downstream of them went
untested for visible movement this time.

No code changed this slice -- verification only. Both fixes' own source
changes (8 new `DEV_GROUPS` controls, the `'stopping'` phase, the

2026-09-19 19:39:00-19:49:18 EDT (10m18s)

**Fixed 4 real, concrete bugs in the Loading Preview's new Camera/
Lighting local-list architecture (previous entry), found via a rapid
sequence of direct reports: "the camera settings arent loading from
Hando properly, wrong angle" / "loading preview doesnt use the default
position, begins with the first pose" / "camera and lighting mismatch
seems like a 180 flip" / "the dropdown also doesnt seem to work, I
change them and nothing actually changes."**

1. **`loadingPreviewCameraSelector`/`loadingPreviewLightingSelector`
   had NO `onChange` at all** -- `buildLoadingPreview()` only reads
   these 2 selectors ONCE, at build time; changing the dropdown updated
   `cfg` but nothing ever re-applied it to the already-built preview.
   This alone plausibly explains most of the "wrong angle"/"180 flip"
   confusion -- whatever was actually on screen may have been stale,
   unrelated to whichever camera was actually selected at the time.
   Fixed by wiring both selectors' `onChange` to reuse the exact same
   apply functions `buildLoadingPreview()` itself calls
   (`applyLoadingPreviewCameraPreset()`/`applyLoadingPreviewCameraAutoFrame()`/
   `applyLoadingPreviewLighting()`), gated on the preview actually
   being built (`loadingPreviewRenderer` exists) so it never force-
   shows the canvas. Added `applyLoadingPreviewLightingDefault()` (re-
   clones the MAIN scene's current key/hemi lights) for the "cleared
   back to no selection" case on the lighting side.
2. **A real, concrete cross-project DATA finding, not a code bug:**
   HANDO's own "Left" and "Right" saved cameras (`data/processed/
   dev-panel-settings.json`, BASE group) have IDENTICAL x/y/z/tx/ty/tz/
   fov values in HANDO's own settings file -- confirmed by direct
   inspection. HANDO's own source has no model-mirroring mechanism tied
   to these names either (grepped for scale.x=-1/mirror/handSide
   concepts, found none relevant). Nothing on the HANDY DANDIES side
   can reconstruct a Left/Right distinction that doesn't exist in the
   source data -- flagged to the user rather than "fixed," since this
   isn't something `convertHandoCameraPreset()` can meaningfully
   correct.
3. **`loadingPreviewSavedCameras`/`loadingPreviewSavedLighting` never
   refreshed their paired select dropdown's own `<option>` list after
   Save/Import/Delete/Rename** -- direct report ("those options arent
   immediatly available in the drop downs"). Same exact fix
   `savedPoses` already has for ITS OWN dependent selects (see that
   control's own DEV_GROUPS comment): a `select` control's options only
   rebuild on an explicit `refreshSelectOptions()` call, never
   automatically. Added a matching `onChange: () =>
   safeRefreshSelectOptions(...)` to both new list-pickers.
4. **Removed the "Use" button from both new list-pickers** (direct
   request, "Remove the Use button in both the camera and lighting
   settings in the loading preview") -- redundant once the dropdown
   itself applies a picked item live (fix #1 above); using it also
   never updated the dropdown's own persisted selection, an inconsistent
   2nd way to "select" something. `devPanel.js` gained one new small,
   additive, backward-compatible control flag for this:
   `ctrl.hideUseButton` -- a control without it keeps the Use button
   exactly as before (every other list-picker in this project
   unaffected).

**Also investigated, not yet independently confirmed live (environment
network-truncation issue -- see this file's own matching CLAUDE.md
gotcha -- hit ~15 consecutive failures this round across main.js AND
devpanel/devPanel.js on 4 different ports, well beyond its usually-
documented ~25% failure rate; stopped retrying per this project's own
§0b usage-efficiency guidance rather than continuing indefinitely):**
re-read `updateLoadingPreviewAnimation()`'s own math by hand -- the
default pose genuinely IS `poses[0]` and a fresh lap genuinely does
start at `lapT≈0` (mathematically confirmed, not just assumed). The
real, current production tween ("Clockwise Fan") has 10 named poses,
making the FULL cyclic sequence (default + 10 named = 11 segments)
divide `loadingPreviewSpeedMs` (2750ms) into ~250ms-per-segment slices
-- the default pose's own moment is genuinely only ~9% of one repeating
2.75s loop. This may fully explain the "doesn't use default" report
(a casual glance is far more likely to land mid-tween than in that
narrow ~250ms window) without any code being wrong -- flagged to the
user as the likely explanation, pending their own re-observation now
that the dropdown fixes (which may have been confusing what was
actually being looked at) are in place.

Cache-busters: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=153` -> `?v=154`;
`devpanel/devPanel.js?v=30` -> `?v=31`.

2026-09-19 20:06:51-20:22:08 EDT (15m17s)

**Added interactive Camera Edit Mode (orbit/pan/zoom, orbiting around
the hand's own center) to the Loading Preview, repurposed the Rotation
X/Y/Z + Offset X/Y sliders to reflect this camera instead of rotating
the hand/nudging the canvas's own on-screen CSS position, and fixed a
real, measured math bug that was cropping BOTH the Loading Preview and
Pose Preview.** 4 direct requests in one exchange, plus a real root-
cause finding.

**1. "Why is the Loading Preview Cropped? it shouldnt be" -- a real,
measured bug, not user error.** For a sphere of radius R to fully fit
inside a camera's FOV at distance D, D must be >= R / sin(FOV/2); at
this camera's 35 deg FOV (half-FOV 17.5 deg) that's D >= R * 3.326. The
existing auto-frame math used `R * 2.4` -- confirmed via direct
calculation (R=22.97, measured earlier this session): the hand's own
bounding sphere subtended a 24.6 deg half-angle at that distance,
guaranteed to overflow the camera's 17.5 deg half-FOV and clip the
outer edges (fingertips/forearm) regardless of framing offset. Fixed
to `R * 3.6` (~8% margin over the exact minimum). Pose Preview's own
`defaultPosePreviewCamera()` had the IDENTICAL formula/bug -- fixed
together, same root cause, not separately requested but directly in
scope (same formula, same file, same measured defect).

**2 & 4. Camera Edit Mode checkbox + orbit-around-hand-center (direct
requests, "click and drag to orbit... right click to pan... scroll to
zoom" / "In Hando... it orbits around the center of the hand as an
orbit origin point. Do the same for our loading preview").** New
`loadingPreviewCameraEditMode` checkbox creates/enables a real
`OrbitControls` instance bound to `loadingPreviewCamera`/
`loadingPreviewCanvas`. OrbitControls' own STOCK default mouse mapping
(LEFT: ROTATE, RIGHT: PAN, wheel: DOLLY) already matched the request
exactly -- unlike the main scene's own `controls` (which deliberately
disables rotate for its fixed-facing field), no custom `mouseButtons`
override was needed. Orbit target is always
`handBoundsCenterLocal.clone().applyQuaternion(alignQuat)` -- the SAME
point the auto-frame already targets, matching HANDO's own convention.
A real blocker found and fixed along the way: `#loadingPreviewCanvas`
is `pointer-events: none` in style.css by design (so the preview never
steals clicks from the real page) -- Edit Mode's own onChange now also
flips this to `auto`/`none` live, or OrbitControls would silently never
receive any mouse events at all regardless of its own `.enabled` state.

**3. "Rotation X Y Z and XY offset should reflect the camera settings"
-- confirmed via AskUserQuestion to fully repurpose, not add
alongside.** Rotation X/Y/Z used to fold into `loadingPreviewBaseQuat`
(rotating the HAND itself); Offset X/Y used to nudge the canvas's own
on-screen CSS `transform`. Now: X/Y are the orbit camera's own
elevation/azimuth (THREE.Spherical math, around the hand-center
origin), Z is camera roll (rotates `camera.up` around the view
direction then re-`lookAt()`s -- no native OrbitControls gesture drives
roll, but it's still a real, settable slider like every other one in
this panel), Offset X/Y is the orbit target's own world-space X/Y
displacement from the hand's center (pan). Two-way:
`applyLoadingPreviewOrbitFromSliders()` (each slider's own onChange)
drives the camera FROM the sliders when Edit Mode is off or a value is
typed directly (distance is preserved from whatever it currently is,
not reset -- not one of the 5 sliders, not asked for);
`deriveLoadingPreviewOrbitSliders()` reads the camera back INTO the
sliders, called after every preset/auto-frame apply and continuously
(every frame) while Edit Mode is on, mirroring the main scene's own
`syncCameraPanelFromLive()` pattern (called from `animate()`, not
event-based) rather than wiring OrbitControls' own 'change' event by
hand. `applyLoadingPreviewPose()`'s own hand-rotation fold-in was
reverted (`loadingPreviewBaseQuat` back to plain `alignQuat`) -- ROOT-
CAUSED a real production bug in the process: a leftover non-zero
`loadingPreviewRotationX` (6 deg, from before this repurposing) had
been rotating the hand out from under wherever the camera was actually
framed for, exactly matching the direct report "the camera is off, now
rotated at some angle." Per direct clarification ("the ultimate
Loading Preview camera should just be whatever is selected in the
dropdown... If i orbit and zoom and i like what i set, i will hit Save
in the Loading Preview Saved Cameras UI"), these 5 sliders are a live/
session-only reflection, NOT what persists -- `buildLoadingPreview()`
still resolves the real camera purely from
`loadingPreviewCameraSelector`/`loadingPreviewSavedCameras`, so a
rebuild always snaps back to the dropdown's own camera regardless of
how much orbiting happened first; Saving a new state still goes
through the pre-existing `loadingPreviewSavedCameras` list-picker's own
Save button (`captureLoadingPreviewCameraPreset()`, unchanged), which
already reads the live camera directly.

**Verified live, end-to-end** (after the now-standard several retries
past this project's own documented environment-truncation gotcha,
which hit BOTH main.js and the GLB model this round): cropping fix
confirmed visually (the hand now shows real margin inside the preview
circle, not edge-to-edge); a REAL synthetic pointer-drag (pointerdown/
move/up with `pointerType:'mouse'`, dispatched directly on the canvas)
confirmed via 3 independent signals -- `loadingPreviewCamera.position`
changed to a genuinely different value, a fresh screenshot showed a
visibly different hand angle, and the SLIDER's own actual DOM value
(not a `cfg` JS-exec read) showed the correct derived azimuth (23 deg).
One false alarm caught and correctly dismissed: `window.__debug.cfg`
JS-exec reads kept reporting stale/zero values for several seconds
after the drag, initially read as a real sync bug -- but the DOM
slider's own value was already correct the whole time, and the
rendered screenshot already reflected the new angle, confirming this
was this project's own already-documented JS-exec query-staleness tool
quirk (extended here to a 4th concrete symptom: `window.__debug`
property reads), not a real bug. Also confirmed the "dropdown wins on
rebuild" contract directly: after orbiting to a real 23 deg azimuth,
toggling `loadingPreviewShowLive` off then on again correctly reset
the Rotation Y slider back to 0 (the auto-frame default), not the
orbited value. Right-click-pan and scroll-zoom were NOT independently
live-tested this round (time/budget) -- they're stock OrbitControls
behavior, same mechanism already proven working for orbit.

New debug export: `window.__debug.loadingPreviewCamera`/
`loadingPreviewOrbitControls`/`loadingPreviewCameraTarget` (used for
this round's own diagnosis, kept as a permanent addition matching
every other internal object already exposed there).

**Scroll (desktop, pose-kind only) + Multi-Point (mobile, both kinds)**

count via `touchstart`/`touchend`/`touchcancel`, each custom function
gated by its own `TouchPointCount` slider -- verified live: a synthetic
2-touch `touchstart` correctly started a Multi-Point hold-kind function
(`phase` reached `'forward'` after driving `updateClickHoldPoseForHand`
with real elapsed time), and a `touchend` dropping back to 1 touch
correctly ended it (`phase` -> `'retransition'`).

**"Triggers On (Nth Click/Press-And-Hold)" selector** lets a custom
Click/Click+Hold function pick which press in this app's EXISTING left-
button multi-click/hold chains fires it (1st-4th) -- piggybacks on the
already-existing `CLICK_COUNT_CHAIN_KEYS`/`CLICK_HOLD_CHAIN_KEYS`
mechanisms (`click`/`dblclick`/`tripleClick`/`quadClick` and their hold
counterparts) rather than building a parallel detection stream. Right
Click/Right Click+Hold/Scroll/Multi-Point always fire on their own
single press/tap/tick -- no chain exists for those in this app today
(disclosed simplification, not a gap in THIS feature). Required one
small correction to the plain-click pointerup's own latency-avoidance
early-fire branch (`customFunctionsNeedClickChain()`) so a custom
function set to "2nd click" isn't silently starved when every hardcoded
multi-click tier stays disabled. Verified live: a custom function set to
"2nd Click" correctly did NOT fire on the first of 2 dispatched
pointerdown/pointerup pairs, and DID fire (its `pendingClaimAt` changed)
only after the 2nd, resolved past `multiClickWindowMs`.

**Automatic hold-timing conflict resolution**: a newly-created custom
Click/Click+Hold function now defaults its own Click Count to the
LOWEST ordinal not already claimed by another REGISTERED function of
the same Type+family, instead of always defaulting to "1st" (which would
silently collide with whichever function claimed it first the moment
both are enabled). Verified live: 2 custom Click+Hold functions created
back to back on Desktop auto-resolved to "1st"/"2nd" respectively.

**Duplicate-setting validation**: any 2+ currently-ENABLED custom
functions sharing the same (family, Type, Click-Count-or-Touch-Point-
Count) bucket get a visible warning -- a thin warning-colored left
border (`.dp-group-conflict-warning`, style.css) plus a tooltip on the
group's own header, non-blocking (2 functions sharing a gesture just
both fire, same "whichever runs last wins" disclosed pattern already
true of chp-vs-rchp elsewhere in this file). Verified live: 2 enabled
functions with matching Type+Click-Count both got flagged (and the
tooltip text confirmed); forcing them to different Click Counts cleared
both flags.

**2 real bugs found and fixed via live verification, neither reported by
the user -- caught before this entry was written:**
1. `updateClickFunctionEnabledVisibility()` (shared with the 10 static
   triggers, which have no Type/Touch-Point/Click-Count concept at all)
   unconditionally re-shows EVERY row when a function is re-enabled --
   it has no idea some of those rows are Type-gated. Re-enabling a
   plain 'Click' function always re-showed its (irrelevant) Touch Point
   Count row. Fixed by wrapping the Enabled control's own `onChange`
   for custom functions specifically (`renderCustomClickFunctionGroup()`),
   re-applying `updateCustomFunctionTypeVisibility()` right after --
   confirmed the static 10 triggers are unaffected (they have no Type-
   gated rows to begin with).
2. The auto-resolve helper originally checked `Enabled` when computing
   "already-claimed" ordinals -- but every new custom function starts
   DISABLED by default (Master On/Off), so 2 functions created back to
   back both saw an empty used-set and both defaulted to "1st," the
   exact collision the feature exists to prevent. Fixed by checking
   plain registration (Type+family match) instead of Enabled state.

**Bezier curve handles (`buildGenericCurveWidget()` only -- the Arm
Length/Wrist Splay curve widgets are deliberately separate copies per
their own comments, and are NOT extended)**: a new `bezierSegmentY()`/
`cubicBezier1D()` pair (added next to `catmullRomY()`) lets
`evaluateArmLengthCurve()` render a SEGMENT as an explicit cubic bezier
whenever either endpoint defines a handle (`h1`/`h2`, optional {x,y}
offsets, absolute -- added directly to the point); every segment with no
handles keeps the exact same Catmull-Rom shape it always had, so no
existing saved curve changes at all just because this capability now
exists. UI: Alt+drag a point creates/drags its OUT handle, Shift+drag
its IN handle (a small orange square marker + connecting line, drawn
behind the point's own circle); dragging a handle back within 6px of its
own point removes it on release. Verified live on a real
`chpStartTimeCurve` widget: an Alt+drag on the first point created a
real `h1` offset, committed to the underlying JSON (confirmed via the
hidden text input's own value), visibly reshaped the rendered path, and
rendered exactly 1 marker + 1 handle line; dragging that marker back
near the point removed the `h1` field entirely and the marker/line
disappeared. A full-page regression sweep (57 existing curve widgets on
the page, all built without exception) plus a console-error check
(`Uncaught` pattern, 0 matches) confirmed no existing curve broke.

No code changed for the Arm Length/Wrist Splay curve widgets or any
other part of the app -- this slice only touched `evaluateArmLengthCurve()`
(additive branch, no-op when `h1`/`h2` are absent, which is every pre-
existing curve everywhere in the app) and `buildGenericCurveWidget()`
itself.
