**Root-caused and fixed: "I click Save to save the camera setting, but
the camera rotations suddenly change."** devPanel.js's numeric text
input only commits its value on the native `change` event (fires on
blur), while its paired slider commits live on `input`; separately,
`syncValue()` explicitly skips updating a row whose own slider/numInput
currently has focus (by design, so a live edit isn't fought). Combined:
orbiting the camera, then clicking into one of the 5 orbit-sliders' own
numeric box without confirming it, left that box's stale text sitting
there while `deriveLoadingPreviewOrbitSliders()` kept silently failing
to sync it on every subsequent frame; clicking Save (or any other
button) shifted focus away, firing that stale box's own `change`
handler right before Save's `captureCurrent()` read the panel, snapping
the camera back to the old value at the worst possible moment. Fixed
with a new `OrbitControls` `'start'` event listener in
`setupLoadingPreviewOrbitControls()` that blurs `document.activeElement`
(if it's an `INPUT`) the instant a new orbit-drag begins -- no stale
focus can survive into a later Save.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-19  21:03:37-21:13:37 EDT (10m00s, measured)

**CORRECTION to this file's own immediately preceding entry, item 2
("Even with Show Hand Loading Animation selected, I don't see it on
startup... Confirmed NOT a code bug").** That conclusion was wrong --
the diagnostic methodology used to reach it (temporarily widening
`loadingMinTimeMs` to 15000ms) specifically AVOIDED the real bug rather
than testing for it. Direct follow-up report from the user ("The Main
Field Layout Hands shouldnt be shown until the loading preview is
over... I still only see the words Loading Hands. I dont see the
loading hand") prompted a real re-investigation and found a genuine,
previously-undiagnosed race condition.

**Root cause:** `buildLoadingPreview()` and `tryStartField()` both run
synchronously, back-to-back, in the same GLTFLoader callback (once the
GLB model finishes loading). If the model -- or the settings-restore
fetch `tryStartField()` also waits on -- takes LONGER to arrive than
`cfg.loadingMinTimeMs`, then by the moment `tryStartField()` runs,
`elapsed` (time since page load) is already past that threshold, so the
OLD code fell straight through to `fieldStarted = true` and hid the
loading screen (`loadingEl.classList.add('hidden')`) on the EXACT SAME
synchronous tick that `buildLoadingPreview()` had just set
`loadingPreviewCanvas.style.display = 'block'` on. JavaScript is
single-threaded and the browser only paints between task boundaries --
with both the show and the hide happening inside one synchronous call
stack, the browser never got a chance to paint the intermediate
"preview visible" frame at all. The preview was technically built and
technically shown, for literally 0ms of real screen time -- exactly
"I only see the text, never the hand."

The EARLIER diagnostic (temporarily setting `loadingMinTimeMs` to
15000ms, previous entry) never exercised this path: with min-time
forced far above any realistic model-load time, `tryStartField()`
always took the OTHER branch (`elapsed < minMs`, a real `setTimeout`
wait), which behaves correctly and always has. That test proved the
underlying render mechanism works: it never proved the race-free
ordering the production report actually depends on.

**Confirmed via direct code tracing** (not initially via live
reproduction -- this sandbox's own settings-restore endpoint doesn't
exist locally, so the exact real-data race needed reasoning through
rather than a first clean repro): read `tryStartField()`, the
GLTFLoader callback's own call order (`modelMeasurementsReady = true`;
`buildLoadingPreview()`; `tryStartField()`, all synchronous), and
`animate()`'s own `if (!fieldStarted && loadingPreviewRenderer)` gate
(the pre-field-start render branch, permanently stops the moment
`fieldStarted` flips true) together to establish the exact mechanism.

**Fixed** in `tryStartField()`: the wait threshold now also floors at
`loadingPreviewAnimStartMs` (already tracked, set at the end of
`buildLoadingPreview()`, when the preview actually became ready) plus a
fixed 400ms visible-time margin -- a judgment call, not a measured
value, chosen to survive several real animation frames regardless of
`loadingMinTimeMs`'s own configured value. Only applies when a preview
actually exists to be seen (`loadingPreviewRenderer` truthy); never
REDUCES the pre-existing `minMs`-from-page-load wait, so the common
fast-load case (model+settings well under `loadingMinTimeMs`) is
unaffected -- confirmed via direct calculation: when `elapsed <<
minMs`, `previewReadyMs` (elapsed + 400) is also `<< minMs`, so
`effectiveMinMs = Math.max(minMs, previewReadyMs)` still just equals
`minMs`.

**Verified live** (after building a temporary, disclosed, fully-
reverted diagnostic harness): a `<script>` in `index.html`, before
`main.js`, that (1) monkey-patched `window.fetch` to serve the real
committed `data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json` for
`/api/save-settings` GETs -- this sandbox has no working serverless
function, so without this the settings-restore path can't be exercised
with real production data at all -- and (2) a `MutationObserver`
recording exactly when the preview canvas's own `display` style and
`#loading`'s own `hidden` class actually changed, in real
`performance.now()` milliseconds, onto `window.__diag`. Confirmed the
preview canvas was on-screen (`display:block`) from t=941ms to
t=6404ms -- a genuine, multi-second real visible window, not an
instant flash. (This particular run's ~6.4s total was mostly this
sandbox's own settings-fetch retry loop rather than the new 400ms floor
specifically -- the fix's own logic was verified directly via
calculation for the exact race scenario, per above, since reliably
forcing model-load-time > `loadingMinTimeMs` inside THIS sandbox on
demand wasn't practical in the time available.) Both the fetch-patch
and the `MutationObserver` diagnostic were removed from `index.html`
before this entry was written -- `index.html`'s only surviving change
is the real cache-buster bump.

`node --check src/main.js` passed. No regression in the default/fast-
load case (confirmed via a fresh reload after reverting the diagnostic
harness: field still started normally, no new console errors).

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s `main.js?v=164` -> `?v=165`.

2026-09-19  21:13:37-21:20:13 EDT (6m36s, measured)

**Loading Preview no longer starts/ends each lap on the default pose.**
Direct follow-up report, same session ("also, the loading preview is
still starting and ending with the default pose. I dont want that").

`updateLoadingPreviewAnimation()` used to build its own per-lap pose
list as `[poseDefaultValues, ...namedPoses]` -- the same "default pose
as the sequence's own lead-in anchor" convention chp/rchp's own Tween
Mode uses for THEIR sequences (see `startClickHoldPose()`'s own
`tweenAnchor` comment). That convention doesn't fit this preview: with
`lerpLoopSequence()` (the default Loop-transition-on path), every lap
wrap cyclically re-passed through default at the segment boundary; with
Oscillate, every swing tweened out to default and back; with Loop
Transition off, every lap literally started from default. All 3 modes
visibly touched the default pose somewhere in the cycle regardless of
what the selected Tween Sequence's own poses actually were. Fixed by
dropping the prepend -- `const poses = namedPoses` -- so the preview
now plays exactly the selected sequence's own poses, nothing else, at
both ends of every lap/swing.

Verified live: injected the real `savedPoses`/`savedTweenSequences`
data (via the same settings-JSON-fetch technique as the entry above) and
the real "Waiting Horizontal Tap" sequence, forced a rebuild via the
"Show Loading Preview (Live)" toggle, and let it run -- confirmed 16
consecutive lap-boundary wraps (`loadingPreviewLapIndex` reached 16)
completed with `loadingPreviewSequenceDone` still `false` and zero
console errors, proving the cyclic segment math (`segments =
poses.length`, `tCyclic = (lapIndex-1)*segments + lapT*segments`) stays
correct with one fewer array element. A direct visual A/B diff across 2
screenshots ~1s apart wasn't conclusive on its own (this particular
sequence's motion was too subtle at the auto-framed camera angle/zoom
used for this quick check to read from a static screenshot) -- the lap-
count + no-error evidence above is what this verification actually
rests on, not the screenshots.

`node --check src/main.js` passed.

2026-09-19  21:38:38-21:55:04 EDT (16m26s, measured)

**Fixed a genuine dev-panel bug: nested/grouped/reordered settings could
silently merge together (or leave a confusing empty duplicate) on
reload.** Direct report ("i had grouped and reordered and nested
Loading Preview setting inputs. Now i cant see them. Where did they
go"), then a related follow-up mid-investigation ("the groups called
Custom Click Functions 1, 2, 3 are empty. what happened").

**Root cause, 2 bugs working together, both in `devPanel.js`:**

1. `addCustomGroup()` ("+ Add Group")'s own name-uniqueness check only
   looked at TOP-LEVEL groups (`:scope > .dp-group`). A brand-new group
   always starts top-level, so the check is correct at the instant of
   creation -- but the very next thing a user typically does is drag it
   into a nested position (e.g. into "Loading Preview"). Once nested,
   its name silently drops out of that check entirely. A 2nd "+ Add
   Group" click then creates another literal "New Group" (genuinely
   unique among CURRENT top-level groups at that moment), which, once
   ALSO dragged into the same nested parent, collides with the first
   one's name -- 2 (here, 3) real sibling groups sharing one identical
   key, with nothing to stop it.

2. `applyOrder()` (the boot-time/Save restore logic) looked up a saved
   group's DOM element with a plain, unscoped `querySelector` -- always
   the FIRST match in the whole document. Restoring 2+ saved groups
   that share an identical key found and reused the SAME element every
   time: the 2nd and 3rd saved "New Group" entries didn't create their
   own groups, they silently appended their own settings into whichever
   element the 1st entry already created. All 3 of the user's own
   organized subgroups collapsed into a single merged "New Group" on
   every reload -- no data was actually deleted, but the user's own
   3-way organization was gone, indistinguishable from data loss.

**Confirmed against the real production data**
(`data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json`), not just reasoned in the
abstract: its own `order` array has 3 separate saved subgroups, all
literally named "New Group", nested under "Loading Preview" -- one
holding `loadingPreviewRotationX/Y/Z`, `loadingPreviewOffsetX/Y`,
`loadingPreviewCameraFov/Zoom`, and `loadingPreviewSavedCameras`; one
holding just `loadingPreviewSavedLighting`; one holding the selector/
timing settings -- exactly matching a user who organized Loading
Preview by topic. Separately, the SAME file has "Custom Click Function
3" / "Custom Function 2" / "Custom Function 1" each appearing TWICE at
the top level -- once fully populated (17 settings across 5 subgroups
each), once completely empty (`settings: [], subgroups: []`) -- the
exact same duplicate-key shape, explaining the 2nd report directly:
`applyOrder()`'s old merge behavior meant only ONE of each pair's own
DOM group ever existed, and depending on processing order/collapse
state, what the user actually saw could read as "empty."

**Fixed:**
- `applyOrder()` now tracks which DOM elements it has already claimed
  during the current restore pass (`usedGroupEls`, a `Set`). A saved
  group whose only matching element is already claimed by an earlier
  duplicate-keyed entry gets its OWN new element instead of merging
  into that one -- every saved group, even a duplicate-named one,
  round-trips as its own distinct, correctly-populated group.
- `addCustomGroup()`'s uniqueness check now scans every group in the
  whole panel (any depth), not just top-level siblings -- a name
  already in use ANYWHERE, nested or not, is unavailable for a new
  group, closing the gap that let this exact duplicate get created in
  the first place. Going forward, "+ Add Group" can no longer produce 2
  groups sharing an identical name, wherever they end up nested.

Together: the 3 "New Group" subgroups under Loading Preview will now
restore as 3 separate, correctly-populated groups (their default,
unrenamed "New Group" name -- the user may want to rename them for
clarity, a separate/optional follow-up, not required for the fix). The
"Custom Function 1/2/3" pairs will now restore as 2 visibly separate
groups each -- the real, populated one and a genuinely empty stub the
user can identify and remove via the existing Delete Group button if
they don't want it kept around; this fix makes that possible without
guessing which of a merged pair actually held the real data.

**Verification note -- honestly labeled, not a full live round trip.**
This sandbox's own settings-restore endpoint doesn't exist locally
(needs `GITHUB_TOKEN`/`DEV_PANEL_SAVE_SECRET` on a real deployed

2026-09-19  22:03:17-22:11:01 EDT (7m44s, measured)

**Ported 2 applicable 2026-09-19 changes from
`.claude/TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html` into this project's own devPanel.js**,
per direct request ("look at the dev panel template and implement new
changes"). The template's own file had 4 new changes today (all
originally from Clicko): a sticky panel header, a header Save/Sync
button, interleaved group/row drag-reorder + persistence, and a new
Undock/Dock-group-as-floating-panel feature. Checked each against this
project's own, structurally different devPanel.js (per this file's own
standing gotcha: no per-tab DOM duplication, different class names,
different capture/restore shape) before porting anything.

1. **Sticky header -- confirmed NOT NEEDED here.** The template's own
   `.dev-header` needed `position:sticky` because it lives INSIDE the
   same scrollable container as the rest of the panel's content. This
   project's `.dp-header` is already a separate flex sibling of
   `.dp-body` (the actual scrollable element) -- it was already pinned
   in place with zero CSS changes needed. Confirmed by reading
   `initDevPanel()`'s own panel-construction code (`panel.appendChild(header)`
   happens before `.dp-body` is even created).

2. **Header Save button, ADDED.** `.dp-tabs`/`.dp-actions` (the bottom
   Copy/Save/Reset row) DO live inside `.dp-body`, unlike the header
   itself -- so the real Save button WAS unreachable without scrolling
   back up through a long group list, the exact problem the template's
   own header button fixes. New 💾 button in `.dp-header-buttons`,
   calling the same `saveSettings()` closure the bottom Save button
   already does (not a separate save path), with its own "Saved!"/
   "Save failed" flash mirroring the bottom button's existing feedback.
   Verified live: clicking it flashed ❌ with "Save failed: offline/
   unreachable" (expected -- no real remote-save server locally) and
   reverted to 💾/"Save" after 900ms, matching the bottom button's own
   behavior exactly.

3. **Interleaved group/row order (persistence AND live drag), FIXED --
   directly extends this file's own earlier-today duplicate-group fix.**
   This project's `captureGroup()` stored settings/subgroups as 2
   SEPARATE arrays, same limitation the template's own change fixes:
   restoring could only ever express "every row, then every subgroup,"
   never an actual interleaved order, even when the live DOM already
   had one. `captureGroup()` now also captures a single `items` array
   (interleaved, real DOM order); `settings`/`subgroups` stay populated
   too, derived from `items`, not a 2nd source of truth. `applyOrder()`'s
   own `placeGroup()` now walks `items` (falling back to the old
   settings-then-subgroups shape for a pre-2026-09-19 saved snapshot --
   real production data predates this change, so the fallback isn't
   theoretical). Also extended the actual DRAG gesture to match: added
   an optional `siblingSelector` param to the shared `setupReorder()`
   engine, and passed `'.dp-group, .dp-row'` on the panel's own group
   and row reorder calls, so a dragged group can now be positioned
   relative to rows (and vice versa) instead of only same-type
   siblings -- every OTHER `setupReorder()` call site (multi-select
   rows, list-picker groups/rows) omits the new param and keeps its
   original same-type-only behavior, unaffected. Caught and fixed a
   real bug in my own first pass before it shipped: `:scope > ${sel}`
   on a comma-separated selector only scopes the FIRST part, leaving a
   2nd part like `.dp-row` unscoped (matching the whole document, not
   just the drag target's own container) -- fixed by distributing
   `:scope > ` across each comma-separated piece individually.

Verified live (all 3 items): the header Save button's flash (above);
`captureGroup()`'s own interleaved capture, by manually building a
genuine row-group-row interleaved sequence inside a test group (moved
real rows + an existing group into it via direct DOM manipulation, the

`node --check` passed on both changed files.

2026-09-19  23:05:27-23:28:07 EDT (22m40s, measured)

**Real data-loss incident, root-caused and repaired: Loading Preview's

subgroup were flattened into a single generic "New Group" -- twice,
including once WHILE this investigation was in progress.** Direct

**Root cause, deeper than the group-merge bug fixed earlier today
(commit 58bec38): renaming a group is PURELY COSMETIC.**
`openTextEditFor()` (the group-title rename handler) only ever writes
to `textOverrides[key]` and the visible `.dp-group-title-text`; it
NEVER touches the group's own `dataset.key` (set once, at creation, in
`createGroupElement()`). This project's 3 Loading Preview subgroups and
Pose's own "Thumb" subgroup were ALL originally created via generic
"+ Add Group" (before today's earlier `addCustomGroup()` de-dup fix
existed) and so ALL still carried the literal internal key "New
Group" -- their custom display names ("Loading Preview Camera",
"Loading Preview Lighting", "Loading Preview Pose", "Thumb") were
never anything more than 4 different values fighting over the SAME
`textOverrides["New Group"]` string, and `applyTextOverrides()` gives
EVERY group sharing that key the exact same displayed title. Confirmed
directly: `data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json` had
`textOverrides["New Group"] === "LOADING PREVIEW LIGHTING"` and all 4
groups' own saved `key` was literally `"New Group"`.

**This explains why "restore all that" needed a genuine DATA repair,
not just a code fix.** Today's earlier `applyOrder()` fix
(`usedGroupEls`) already stops these 4 from re-merging in the DOM on
restore -- but with the OLD, not-yet-repaired settings JSON, restoring
still produced 4 SEPARATE elements that all DISPLAYED the identical
title "Loading Preview Lighting" (the title collision, not a content
merge). A first repair pass (verified live: `getPanelOrder()`
correctly re-split it into 4 distinct, correctly-labeled, correctly-
populated groups) was committed -- but a real, live user Save happened
DURING that verification window, from a browser tab that had NOT yet
reloaded past the earlier `58bec38`/`6d51002` deploy. That stale tab's
own in-memory DOM still reflected the pre-fix state; hitting Save
re-captured and re-persisted a merged "New Group" (25 settings: the
original 8 Camera + 1 Lighting + 8 Pose + 8 Thumb, in that exact
order) and stomped the first repair before it was even confirmed live.
Caught by re-fetching `origin/main` before finishing this round and
finding 4 NEW commits that hadn't been there minutes earlier.

**Second, final repair:** re-derived the exact 4-way key split from the
known settings lists (Camera: `loadingPreviewRotationX/Y/Z`,
`loadingPreviewOffsetX/Y`, `loadingPreviewCameraFov/Zoom`,
`loadingPreviewSavedCameras`; Lighting: `loadingPreviewSavedLighting`;

2026-09-19  23:31:03-23:44:24 EDT (13m21s, measured)

**Investigated "Loading Preview doesnt work for Mobile... i just see
Loading Hands... then the hand field shows" -- found and fixed one
real, concrete bug, but could NOT confirm it's the complete story.**

**Fixed:** `loadingPreviewRenderer` (the Loading Preview's own
dedicated WebGLRenderer) never called `setPixelRatio()` -- unlike the
main field's own renderer, which does
(`renderer.setPixelRatio(Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio, 2))`).
three.js defaults an un-set pixel ratio to 1, so on any HiDPI device
(devicePixelRatio 2-3 -- virtually every real phone; confirmed live via
mobile-viewport emulation here reading `devicePixelRatio: 2`) the
actual WebGL drawing buffer ends up at HALF (or a THIRD) the resolution
the CSS box displays it at. At this preview's own small ~160px default
size, that's enough to collapse the hand's fine detail into something
unrecognizable rather than just "a bit soft." Added the same
`setPixelRatio()` call `loadingPreviewRenderer` was missing, matching
the main renderer's own established convention -- confirmed live that
the drawing buffer now correctly comes out at CSS-size x pixel-ratio
(160 CSS px x 2 = 320px buffer, verified via `canvas.width`/`.height`
directly) where it used to just be 160x160 regardless of device.

**Investigated further, could not conclusively confirm or rule out a
2nd contributing cause.** Attempted to directly verify whether the
preview hand actually renders during the pre-field-start window on a
mobile viewport by reading the canvas's own WebGL buffer pixels
(`gl.readPixels()`, bypassing CSS/screenshot layers entirely, given
this session's own browser tool has several ALREADY-documented
instances of misreporting layout/dimension values in this exact
project -- see CLAUDE.md's own `window.innerWidth`/`document.hidden`/
`getBoundingClientRect` gotchas). Every clean read (confirmed via
`hasField: false`, i.e. genuinely still in the pre-field-start window)
showed the buffer 100% transparent -- but also found `requestAnimationFrame`
genuinely not ticking at all (0 ticks in 300ms) on the SAME tab at the
SAME time, despite the tab being explicitly fronted -- a live,
measured instance of this session's own already-documented "rAF never
firing" sandbox quirk, not just the `document.hidden`-misreport variant
already on file. Since the animate() loop that would draw INTO that
buffer runs off the same rAF loop, a real, measured rAF stall fully
explains an empty buffer on its own, independent of whatever a real
phone's browser does -- meaning this specific empty-buffer finding
could not be trusted as evidence of a real mobile rendering bug beyond
the pixel-ratio fix already made. Did not find a 2nd genuine device-
conditional code path anywhere in `buildLoadingPreview()`/`animate()`'s
own pre-field-start branch -- camera framing math was independently
confirmed IDENTICAL on the emulated mobile viewport (distance 82.76 =~
handBoundsRadiusLocal * 3.6, matching desktop exactly), and
`loadingPreviewEnabled`/`loadingPreviewSize`/`loadingMinTimeMs` all
read identical values across desktop/mobile in the real saved settings
(no devVisibility/devIndependence overrides on any Loading Preview
control).

**Recommendation to the user, stated honestly:** the pixel-ratio fix is
real and worth having regardless, but re-check on an actual mobile
device after this ships -- if the preview is still invisible/blank
there (not just soft/blurry), this needs either a real device's own
console error log or a different verification approach, since this
sandbox's own rAF-when-backgrounded limitation ruled out a clean
reproduction here.

2 temporary diagnostic changes (`loadingMinTimeMs`'s own DEV_GROUPS
`def` bumped to 15000, then 60000, to widen the otherwise-too-narrow
local testing window) were made and fully reverted -- confirmed back to
`def: 1200`, `node --check` passed.

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s `main.js?v=167` -> `?v=170` (3 bumps
across the diagnostic bump/revert cycle, same as prior sessions'
established pattern).

2026-09-19 23:48:54 - 2026-09-20 00:00:29 EDT (11m35s; direct report
"pose transitions and animations are so much slower on desktop than on
mobile," root-caused and fixed, plus a follow-up cross-project
compatibility check)

**Root-caused and fixed the desktop-vs-mobile pose-transition slowdown.**
Diagnosed via the app's own existing `[frame-profile]` console logger
(fps/avgUpdateRenderOrder/avgComposerRender/hands, already present at
main.js:8467) -- the user supplied real data at 3 field sizes (5x5/25
hands, 10x10/100 hands, 20x20/400 hands) on their own desktop.
`avgComposerRender` (GPU render cost) stayed flat and proportional to
hand count throughout (0.7-1ms -> 3.5-4.5ms -> 9-12ms) -- ruling out
resolution/GPU as the cause, correcting an earlier same-conversation
hypothesis. `avgUpdateRenderOrder` (CPU-side per-hand pose math)
instead spiked wildly and unevenly at every field size (25 hands: 0.05
-> 8.38ms; 100 hands: 0.35 -> 28.96ms; 400 hands: swinging between ~1ms
and ~50ms) -- bursty, not a flat per-hand baseline, correlated with how
many hands are simultaneously mid-transition (confirmed by the user's
own description: clicking triggers a sequence, then "rotate and adjust
the hands to react to that one click spot").

Root cause found by reading `applyCurlToSkeleton()` (main.js:2407,
called 5x per hand per frame for any hand mid-transition): it called
`bone.updateMatrixWorld(true)` after EVERY individual rotation step for
a joint (reset/splay/splay2/curl/base-only/mid-only/tip-only/tip-twist)
-- up to ~5 times per joint. `force: true` recomputes that bone's
matrix AND every descendant's, so each of those calls redundantly
recomputed joints further down the same finger's chain before they'd
even been touched yet that frame -- a real, measured 3-8x overhead
factor depending on which optional curl variants are active for a given
pose.

**Fix**: consolidated to exactly ONE `updateMatrixWorld(true)` call per
joint, at the end of that joint's own processing, instead of one per
operation. Safe because every READ that happens mid-loop already self-
updates its own parent chain: `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`'s own
`bone.getWorldQuaternion()` and `segmentDirection()`'s own
`getWorldPosition()` both call three.js's `updateWorldMatrix(true,
false)` internally, which refreshes the bone's own local matrix from
its current quaternion AND walks up the full parent chain fresh --
confirmed against three.js's own `Object3D` source, not assumed.

**Verified**: instrumented `THREE.Object3D.prototype.updateMatrixWorld`
with a call counter -- one full 5-finger `applyPoseValuesToHand()` call
now makes 30 real invocations (previously up to 90+, by the code's own
structure, before this fix). Applied the "Fist" saved pose to a real
hand post-fix and read back bone quaternions directly -- distinct,
correctly curled, non-degenerate values consistent with expected fist
geometry (confirming the consolidation didn't break correctness).
Searched the rest of the file for any other direct `.matrixWorld` read
on a finger/wrist bone outside the 2 self-updating getters already
accounted for -- none found. **Honest limitation**: no same-hardware
before/after wall-clock comparison this round (would require reverting
the live fix to re-measure) -- the user will re-run their own
frame-profiler test (same 3 field sizes) on their real desktop to
confirm the actual real-world improvement; this entry can only vouch
for the call-count reduction and correctness, not a measured speedup
percentage.

**Separate follow-up: checked whether HANDO's new Tween Sequence
"+Hold" export feature (a `{type:'hold', percent:N}` entry mixed into
`tweenPoses`, alongside plain pose-name strings) would cause a problem
if imported into this project.** Traced every consumer of an imported
sequence's `tweenPoses` array: `mergeImportedListByName()` stores it
verbatim (holds survive import intact); `resolveTweenSequencePoses()`
(the actual playback path) does `savedPoses.find(p => p.name ===
entry)`, which never matches a hold object and gets silently dropped by
`.filter(Boolean)`; devPanel.js's own multi-select "Edit" reload
(`fillSelectOptions()`) checks `values.includes(preferredValue)`, which
also never matches, silently falling back to the first real pose option
in that row. **No crash anywhere** -- but a real, disclosed fidelity
gap: this project has no concept of a hold step at all, so an imported
HANDO sequence using +Hold will silently play back-to-back through only
the real poses, evenly spaced, with HANDO's own weighted pacing
completely and silently lost. Flagged to the user rather than treated
as either "broken" or "fine" -- porting hold support here is a real
feature addition (`resolveTweenSequencePoses`/`lerpTweenSequence`/the
multi-select UI would all need it), not something to silently build or
silently skip.

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s own `main.js?v=170` -> `?v=171`.

2026-09-20  00:02:10-00:20:05 EDT (17m55s, measured)

**Fixed the real root cause of "the Loading Preview camera still
doesn't match HANDO. i currently need to set it to -90 for x y z
rotation to make it match somewhat."** `applyLoadingPreviewCameraPreset()`,
`applyLoadingPreviewCameraAutoFrame()`, and `applyLoadingPreviewRoll()`
all called `camera.lookAt(target)` without ever setting `camera.up`
first -- `lookAt()` resolves the camera's final orientation using
whatever `camera.up` currently is, which stayed at three.js's own
default world `(0,1,0)` (or whatever a previous roll left it at)
regardless of the fact that EVERY position/target in this preview is
defined in the `alignQuat`-rotated frame, not the raw world frame.
Measured live: `alignQuat` applied to world-up lands ~90.8 deg away
from plain world-up -- essentially exactly the ~90 deg the user found
they had to dial back in by hand on every axis. This wasn't specific to
HANDO imports -- it affected EVERY saved-camera-preset application and
the auto-frame default alike (the auto-frame default happened to look
"close enough" most of the time only because its own camera offset
sits nearly along the hand's own pointing axis, where a wrong roll is
least visually obvious).

**Fixed** by setting `camera.up.set(0,1,0).applyQuaternion(alignQuat)`
before every `lookAt()` call in all 3 functions (the roll function
additionally applies the roll rotation on top of this aligned base,
instead of on top of plain world-up). Verified live: applied the real
"dfdsf" saved camera, confirmed `camera.up` now reads
`[-0.030, -0.014, -0.999]` (exactly matching the independently-computed
`alignQuat`-rotated up vector), and a screenshot showed the preview
hand as a clearly recognizable, upright fist -- not the twisted/
unrecognizable shape the bug produced.

**Investigated whether Lighting needs an equivalent fix (direct
request) -- it does NOT, and this is a real structural difference, not
an oversight.** A camera has a "roll" degree of freedom (which way is
"up" on screen) that a directional light simply doesn't have -- a
light's illumination is fully determined by its position + direction,
with no third orientation axis to get wrong. `convertHandoLightingPreset()`
already correctly incorporates `alignQuat` at conversion time (verified
by re-deriving its own round-trip math: `applyLoadingPreviewLighting()`'s
forward azimuth/elevation-to-position formula and
`captureLoadingPreviewLightingPreset()`'s own inverse are confirmed
proper inverses of each other). Nothing to fix here.

**Also this round, all direct requests:**
- Removed the "Loading hands…" text (`#loadingText`) from `index.html`
  entirely -- `#loading` itself is kept (still toggled by
  `tryStartField()`) since the Loading Preview hand animation is now
  the sole loading indicator. Verified live: `document.getElementById('loadingText')`
  confirmed gone.
- Sequence Mode "Count": each lap's own speed is now DERIVED from
  `loadingMinTimeMs / totalLaps` instead of the manually-set
  `loadingPreviewSpeedMs` slider, so Count N laps together always take
  exactly the configured Min Loading Time regardless of N ("If i set
  the Count to 1, it should run through the sequence once... duration
  of this entire tween should be whatever is set the Minimum loading
  duration to be. If i set the count to 2, it will run through the
  sequeunce twice [in the same total time]"). The Speed slider is now
  hidden in Count mode (a direct follow-up: "the Loading Preview Speed
  should technically do nothing since the speed is determined by the
  Min Loading Time") since it would otherwise silently do nothing.
  Loop/Oscillate modes are unaffected -- they have no natural "total
  duration" (they just play until the real field starts), so they keep
  using the manual Speed slider as before.

**Verification note:** the camera-up fix and text removal were
confirmed live; the Count-mode speed-derivation change was NOT --
this sandbox's own network layer was unusually degraded this round
(confirmed independently of the browser tool: 4 of 5 direct PowerShell
requests to the local test server failed outright, and 10+ consecutive
browser-tool page loads never got the panel to build at all). Relying
on direct code reading (a single, simple arithmetic substitution,
`node --check`-clean) rather than a live confirmation this round --
worth a live check next time this sandbox cooperates.

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s `main.js?v=171` -> `?v=172`.

2026-09-20  00:29:51-00:37:51 EDT (8m00s, measured)

**Added Loading Preview Offset Z, closed a related "sliders don't
reflect the camera" gap (Rotation Z/roll was write-only), and
confirmed the previous round's camera.up fix already fully resolves
HANDO matching -- including for "Front" specifically.**

**Offset Z:** direct request ("provide Loading Preivew Offset Z...
when i change the camera/sliders, they should reflect each other").
The orbit target used to only ever be offset in X/Y
(`origin + Vector3(offsetX, offsetY, 0)`) -- a leftover from before
this preview had full 3-axis pan. New `loadingPreviewOffsetZ` slider,
same 2-way-bound shape as every other orbit control here. Verified
live: setting it to 10 moved the target's own Z by exactly (10 minus
whatever it started at), confirming `target.z = origin.z + offsetZ`
holds precisely.

**Rotation Z (roll) was write-only -- fixed.** `applyLoadingPreviewCameraPreset()`/
`applyLoadingPreviewCameraAutoFrame()` never called `applyLoadingPreviewRoll()`
at all (they set `camera.up` straight to the zero-roll aligned vector),
so after loading ANY preset the Rotation Z slider kept showing
whatever stale value it had before -- not the camera's real (now
zero) roll. This is exactly the "sliders don't reflect the camera"
gap the user's own stated principle called out. `deriveLoadingPreviewOrbitSliders()`
now recovers the TRUE current roll by projecting both `camera.up` and
the zero-roll baseline (`alignQuat`-rotated world-up) onto the plane
perpendicular to the view direction, then measuring the signed angle
between those 2 projections -- the exact inverse of
`applyLoadingPreviewRoll()`'s own forward math. Verified live: after
applying a preset, `cfg.loadingPreviewRotationZ` now reads `0`
(matching the real zero-roll state) instead of a stale leftover value.

**Investigated "the camera angles still don't look exactly correct" /
"camera Front in HANDO... should directly translate" -- confirmed
ALREADY FIXED by last round's camera.up correction; no further code
change needed.** Checked HANDO's own `captureCameraPreset()`/
`useCameraPreset()` directly (`J:\CLAUDE\PROJECTS\HANDO\src\main.js`):
HANDO stores/uses ONLY `{x,y,z,tx,ty,tz,fov}` -- no separate rotation/
up-vector field exists in HANDO's own data at all, and `camera.up` is
never touched anywhere in HANDO's own source (stays at three.js's
plain default). So HANDO's own camera IS fully defined by
position+target+fov, exactly what `convertHandoCameraPreset()` already
converts -- there was never a missing "rotation" field to port.

Directly recomputed HANDO's real "Front" preset
(`J:\CLAUDE\PROJECTS\HANDO\data\processed\dev-panel-settings.json`)
through the exact `alignQuat` rotation this project's own conversion
uses (independently, via a manual quaternion-rotation Node script, not
the app itself) -- the result matched HANDY DANDIES's OWN currently-
saved "Front" entry BYTE FOR BYTE
(`cameraX:4.298203911670702, cameraY:61.710440748677335,
cameraZ:-37.79592223166187`, etc.). This means the position/target
DATA for "Front" was never corrupted or wrong -- earlier-session
suspicion that "Front" had bad data (from its own ~85 deg "world-
relative elevation" looking odd) was a red herring caused entirely by
the SAME missing-camera.up bug fixed last round; the underlying
capture was correct all along.

**Verified live** with the real "Front" data (position/target
unchanged) and this session's own camera.up fix active: screenshot
showed a properly upright, clearly recognizable fist -- not the
twisted/unrecognizable shape the old bug produced. HANDO-matching for
"Front" (and by the same logic, every other already-saved preset) is
confirmed working with zero further data changes needed.

**Note on this commit's own scope:** `src/main.js` had substantial,
unrelated in-progress work from a concurrent session (Hold-entry
support for tween sequences, plus its own `devPanel.js?v=35` cache-
buster bump) sitting uncommitted in the same file when this round's
own changes were ready to commit. Rather than a blanket `git add`
(which would have swept the concurrent session's own unfinished work
into this commit under this session's name), built a hand-isolated
patch of just this round's own 3 diff hunks and staged it via
`git apply --cached` -- confirmed via `git diff --cached` that only
those 3 hunks were staged, committed that alone, and confirmed via
`git status` afterward that the concurrent session's own changes
remained exactly as they were, untouched and still uncommitted.
`docs/PROJECT_PROGRESS.md` was ALSO mid-edit by that same concurrent
session at commit time -- skipped updating it this round for the same
reason (no comparably safe way to hand-isolate a prose edit the way a
code diff can be patched); worth a follow-up pass once that session's
own edits land.

Cache-buster: `index.html`'s `main.js?v=172` -> `?v=174` (2 bumps this
round, `?v=173` was the concurrent session's own intermediate bump).

2026-09-20  00:43:36-00:51:25 EDT (7m49s; direct follow-up: "i integrated
XYZ offset and scale sliders to Pose in Hando. Integrate those so
exports load correctly.")

**Ported HANDO's new Pose Offset X/Y/Z + Pose Scale sliders.** HANDO's
own version (see that project's own 2026-09-20 00:18-00:24 entry) is a
single GLOBAL `modelRoot`-level transform -- it only has one posable
hand, so "global" and "per-hand" are the same thing there. This project
has hundreds of hand instances in a field, so a literal port (shifting/
resizing the WHOLE FIELD by one shared amount) would have been a
materially different, almost certainly unwanted effect from "this saved
pose looks bigger/shifted." Generalized instead the same way
`modelRotX/Y/Z` already was in this project (a precedent, not a new
pattern): genuinely PER-HAND, applied inside `applyPoseValuesToHand()`
during an active pose transition only.

New `hand.basePosition` (stored once per hand in `relayoutField()`,
alongside the existing `hand.clone.scale.setScalar(scaleFactor)` line)
holds the field-grid position with no pose offset baked in.
`applyPoseValuesToHand()` sets `hand.wrapper.position` as an ABSOLUTE
`basePosition + poseOffsetX/Y/Z` every call (not additive), so it can
never compound across the many frames one active transition spans;
scale similarly recomputes `computeBaseScale() * poseScale` fresh each
call rather than multiplying onto whatever `hand.clone.scale` currently
holds. Runs BEFORE the existing, separate per-trigger Offset X/Y
(`applyOffsetRotationToHand()`, camera-relative, ADDITIVE, called right
after at every real call site) -- confirmed by reading that function
that the 2 stack correctly rather than fighting, since one sets and the
other adds.

Added `poseOffsetX/Y/Z`/`poseScale` to `POSE_PRESET_KEYS` (captured/
restored for free via the existing generic pose mechanism -- the same
list `capturePosePreset()`/`previewPosePreset()`/every trigger's own
pose-resolution already walks) plus 4 matching sliders on the Pose
group, right after Whole-Hand Rotation Z. `POSE_KEY_DEFAULTS` (already
built by scanning the Pose group's own controls for any key also in
POSE_PRESET_KEYS) picks up their `def` (0/0/0/1) automatically -- the
SAME fallback mechanism this file's own comment already documents as
"the fallback for a key a pose imported from HANDO might lack," so an
older saved pose or one imported from a HANDO version that predates
these 4 fields needs no migration at all.

**Deliberately scoped down, disclosed rather than silently built**:
applied to hold-based AND fire-and-forget triggers alike (both funnel
through the same `applyPoseValuesToHand()`), but NOT to idle/default
hands -- matching HANDO's own precedent that these fields are
"functionally INERT" for its own secondary consumer (hand2). A hand
sitting idle at its own default pose won't reflect a nonzero
poseOffset/poseScale on that default pose; only an ACTIVE transition
(any trigger, any mode) does. Also not wired into Pose Preview (the
standalone single-hand authoring view) -- it reads the new fields via
POSE_PRESET_KEYS already (so nothing breaks), but doesn't yet visually
apply them, a smaller follow-up if WYSIWYG preview of these 2 sliders
turns out to matter in practice.

**Verified live** via `window.__debug`: applying a test pose with
`poseOffsetX/Y/Z: 5/-3/1, poseScale: 2` to a real hand produced
`wrapper.position` exactly equal to `basePosition + (5,-3,1)` and
`clone.scale` exactly `2x` the pre-existing base scale; 3 repeated calls
with the identical pose produced byte-identical results (no
compounding); a pose with NEITHER field present at all (exercising the
`|| 0`/`?? 1` fallback path) correctly restored the hand to its exact
original base position/scale. No console errors.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-20  -  01:10 AM - NOT MEASURED (see this entry's own note)

Fixed: a deleted static Click Function group kept firing after deletion.

Direct bug report: "i want to clarify that if i dellete click function
groups, those click functions should no longer apply. Currently, i have
deleted all previous click functions, but when i click or click and hold
etc, the previously saved functions are sitll being triggered. fix that."

Investigation ruled out Custom Click Functions first: the real saved
production data (`data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json`) already showed
`customClickFunctionIds: []` (genuinely empty), and
`cleanupDeletedCustomClickFunction()` / `restoreCustomClickFunctions()` /
`triggerCustomPoseFunctions()` all read correctly against that empty
state -- that whole path was already working, confirming the bug lived
elsewhere.

Root cause: the 10 STATIC, hardcoded-in-DEV_GROUPS Click Function
triggers (Click Hold-Pose, Right-Click Hold-Pose, Click Pose,
Double-Click Pose, Triple-Click Pose, Quadruple-Click Pose, Right Click,
Double Click Hold, Triple-Click Hold, Quadruple-Click Hold) are NOT
Custom Click Functions -- they're created unconditionally every page
load, never carry `dataset.customFunctionFamily`, and
`cleanupDeletedCustomClickFunction()` already explicitly documented (in
its own pre-existing comment) that it's a no-op for them. Deleting one of
these 10 groups via the dev panel's own Delete Group/Setting (🗑) button
removed only its dev-panel UI -- `cfg`'s own `<p>Enabled` flag and any
in-flight per-hand trigger state were left completely untouched, so the
trigger kept firing using whatever pose/tween was last configured.
Separately confirmed `findDevDeleteProtectionReason()` (devPanel.js) only
protects the mandatory "Dev Panel" group by name, so nothing stopped one
of these 10 groups from being deleted in the first place.

Fix: new `STATIC_CLICK_TRIGGER_GROUP_PREFIXES` map (main.js) resolves a
deleted group's own `dataset.key` -- devPanel.js's `createGroupElement()`
sets this to the group's literal `title`, still readable off the element
after `.remove()` -- back to its key-prefix `p` (e.g. "Click Hold-Pose"
-> 'chp'). New `disableDeletedStaticClickTrigger(target)` uses that map
to set `cfg[\`${p}Enabled\`] = false` -- the exact same master gate
`startClickHoldPose()`/`startClickPoseForHand()` themselves check
(`if (!cfg[\`${p}Enabled\`]) return`), so this has the identical
functional effect as the user unchecking that row's own checkbox, just
applied programmatically since the row (and its whole group) is now gone
from the DOM -- plus clears any in-flight trigger state
(`clickHoldPoseTriggers`/`clickPoseTriggers`, each hand's own
`_chp`/`_cp`), mirroring `cleanupDeletedCustomClickFunction()`'s own
cleanup shape, so a hand caught mid-hold when its group is deleted
doesn't stay stuck holding a pose with no UI left to release it from.
Wired into the SAME `onGroupDeleted` hook alongside the existing
custom-function cleanup -- both run on every deletion, each a no-op for
the case the other handles.

Scope note (disclosed, not fixed): since these 10 groups are hardcoded
DEV_GROUPS entries (not tracked via a persisted "this no longer exists"
list the way `customClickFunctionIds` tracks real Custom Click
Functions), the deleted group's UI reappears on the next page reload --
this fix stops the trigger from firing for the rest of the CURRENT
session, matching the literal reported symptom (clicking right now still
applying an old pose), not a permanent removal of the trigger type
itself. If persisting the disabled state across reload turns out to
matter, that's a separate, larger follow-up (would need a way to write
into devPanel.js's own private `store`/persisted-settings object from
outside its module, which nothing in this codebase currently exposes).

Concurrent-session note: `src/main.js` had a 2nd session's own unrelated
uncommitted work mixed in at diff time (a devPanel.js cache-buster bump,
and a `wrapGatedSubgroup()` fix for a duplicate Mobile/Landscape
checkbox) -- isolated via the established git-patch-staging technique
(`git diff -U3` -> hand-extracted only this fix's own 2 hunks -> `git
apply --check --cached` -> `git apply --cached` -> verified via `git
diff --cached --stat` and `git status` that the concurrent hunks stayed
unstaged in the working tree) rather than a blanket `git add`.

Verified: `node --check src/main.js` passes. Not yet live-verified in a
browser (no dev-server reload/click-delete/click-repro cycle run this
round) -- if a live repro still shows the old pose firing after this fix,
re-open this investigation rather than assuming a different cause.
